Deliberately a single-slot cache, not a growing one: re-zooming back into an earlier area just refetches rather than accumulating unbounded state for a session that might zoom all over a long file - a reasoned, documented trade-off, not an oversight. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: confirmed the exact windowed IPC call resolves quickly with real, correctly-shaped peak data; confirmed the debounced fetch actually fires exactly once after a rapid 30-tick zoom-in gesture (not once per tick) and correctly applies via `setDetailPeaks`; a real screenshot at high zoom showed genuine fine wave-cycle detail (individual oscillations, not a blocky or smoothed-over band) where the base-resolution array alone would have shown nothing more than the existing smoothing pass's cosmetic interpolation; confirmed zooming back out cleanly falls back to the smooth base-resolution rendering with no stale-detail artifacts.

**"Sync it up with" — two or more Random Interval (scatter) sounds now stay locked together** ("one starts, all start"), the scatter-specific variant of the broader composite-multi-sound-effect-groups idea, picked as the next lowest-scored ranked backlog item (6,6,4=5.33). New `entry.scatter.syncGroup` (free-text, empty by default): any number of scatter-mode sounds sharing the same non-empty group name fire together, no picker UI needed since typing the same name is the whole membership mechanism - a Remix field next to the existing scatter controls, threaded through the usual places (`normalizeScatter`'s staleness comparison, `snapshotOf`'s explicit field list - the exact spot two past bugs originated from, added correctly from the start this time). Both `BufferScatterSource` and `StreamScatterSource` gained an `onShotStart` hook, called once per shot that actually started playing - wired at construction (`tabs/mixer/index.js`'s `tryCreateBufferSource`/`getOrCreateSource`, gated off for the ephemeral test-fire case) to a new `propagateSync(firedId)`: looks up the fired sound's `syncGroup`, and for every other currently-included scatter sound sharing it, calls the already-existing `fireNow()` (built for the Mixer's own test-fire button) with a new `silent: true` option. **The cascade-prevention mechanism**: `fireNow`/`_playOneShot` only call `onShotStart` when NOT silent, so a sync-triggered fire never re-notifies `propagateSync` itself - keeps a group of any size in lockstep with exactly one level of propagation per real trigger, never an infinite ping-pong between members. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: patched both `AudioContext.createBufferSource` and `HTMLMediaElement.prototype.play` to log every real shot-start; clicking one synced sound's test-fire button fired its partner within 0.2ms (the synchronous call-order gap, not a real audible delay) in both directions (A→B and B→A), always exactly 2 log entries (no cascade); a third scatter sound in an unrelated sync group present in the same mix correctly never fired; and the Remix UI field round-tripped through a real Save click, confirmed by reading the persisted `scatter.syncGroup` back via IPC afterward.

**Doppler effect toggle in Remix — simulates a sound passing by** (pitch rises approaching the loop region's own midpoint, falls after), the next ranked backlog item (7,7,5=6.33). Researched first (per [[feedback_research_before_complex_tasks]]): real sound design fakes a pass-by with a symmetric pitch bend crossing through natural pitch at the moment of closest approach, not derived physics - confirmed ffmpeg's `rubberband` filter (already used for the existing static Pitch control) accepts live `asendcmd`-driven pitch updates, verified directly against the real bundled binary (measured a genuine, monotonic frequency shift across a synthetic test tone) before writing any app code. Per the owner's own explicit design direction mid-session (a real-time correction to an in-progress semitone-slider plan): shipped as a plain checkbox next to Start/End/Length (not a tunable intensity dial - `DOPPLER_SWING_SEMITONES = 5`, a fixed reasoned constant) plus a waveform visual marking the loop region's midpoint as "closest" (a warm gold glow gradient peaking there, plus a distinct marker line + pin, layered under the waveform fill) and Start/End as "furthest." New `entry.speedPitch.dopplerEnabled` (bake-only, like Reverse - no live preview, since `PreviewSource.js`/core `SoundSource.js` are both `<audio>`-element-based and `.playbackRate` isn't a smoothly-automatable AudioParam there). `loopClip.js`'s `buildDopplerSendCmd` generates ~10 commands/second ramping *linearly in semitones* from +5 at the loop region's Start, through 0 at the midpoint, to -5 at End, composed multiplicatively with any static `pitchSemitones` already set (one `rubberband` node, not two chained) - inserted into the same pre-crossfade pass Speed/Pitch/Reverse already use. **Real bug caught during verification, not code review**: the first baked Doppler clip measured a scrambled double-hump frequency curve instead of the intended monotonic descent - traced to the auto-crossfade rotation stage (which physically reorders a clip's own audio to hide the loop seam) running *after* the Doppler-bent intermediate, exactly the same class of problem Scatter/Scheduled playback already solved by forcing crossfade to 0 for themselves. Fixed by extending `effectiveCrossfadeSeconds` (all three duplicated copies - `library.js`, `tabs/mixer/index.js`, `plugins/editor/index.js`'s two inline call sites in `save()`/`saveAsCopy()`) to also force 0 whenever `speedPitch.dopplerEnabled` is true, regardless of playMode. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app, at the pixel and frequency-measurement level**: a screenshot confirmed the gold gradient+marker renders correctly centered on the loop region's midpoint; after the crossfade fix, sampled a real baked clip's actual frequency in 0.2s windows across its whole duration and confirmed a clean, monotonic descent from ~435Hz (matching the expected +5-semitone shift on a 330Hz source) through the midpoint down to ~250Hz (matching -5 semitones) - reproduced independently both via a direct main-process bake call and through the genuine Remix Save button click; a second test sound's own baked output initially looked confusing until its *original* source audio was checked directly and found to have a real, deliberate 195Hz→895Hz jump of its own (a leftover loop-seam test fixture, not representative content) - the Doppler math checked out correctly once that was accounted for, a false alarm rather than a second bug.

**Undo/Redo, Cancel, and a leave-confirmation prompt** — the top-ranked open backlog item (7,7,6=6.67), reported directly via the inbox: "Ctrl+Z to undo, Ctrl+Shift+Z to redo, a Cancel button to revert everything since you opened the tab, and a leave-confirmation prompt (Yes/No/Don't ask again)." Built as a session-scoped snapshot-history stack, not persisted: `this.historyStack`/`historyIndex` hold full-state snapshots in the exact normalized shape `snapshotOf()` already produces for the existing dirty-check (`captureEditorState()` just `JSON.parse`s that same string instead of comparing it), so undo/redo needed zero new serialization logic. **One hook point covers every field, not per-control instrumentation**: every field-change handler in this file already ends by calling `updateSaveButtonState()` (`applyFilterControls`/`applyEqControls`/`applyCrossfadeControl`/`applySpeedPitchControls`/`applyPlayModeControl`/`applyScatterControls`/`applyScheduleControls`, plus the loop-point `onLoopChange` listener) - a single `scheduleHistoryCommit()` call added there is the only integration point, debounced 500ms (`HISTORY_COMMIT_DEBOUNCE_MS`) so a live drag or a burst of wheel/keyboard nudges settles into one history entry, not one per frame - confirmed by the owner to feel right after testing the live dev build directly, per [[feedback_feel_driven_tuning]]. `applyEditorState(state)` (the undo/redo restore path) reuses the identical `setXControls(state.x)` + `applyXControls()` pairs each field's own live handler already calls, gated by a `_restoringHistory` flag so restoring a past state doesn't itself get recorded as a new one. **Cancel** is the cheapest piece by far - `selectPickerSound(currentEntry.id)`, the exact reload-from-saved path already used throughout this session's own CDP verification to discard test edits, now wired to a button. **The leave-confirmation prompt required a real `TabHost.js` capability that didn't exist**: `onHide` is fire-and-forget cleanup, not something a switch can be blocked on, so `TabHost.js` gained a new optional `onBeforeHide` hook - `activate()` is now `async` and awaits it before actually switching tabs, canceling the switch if it resolves `false` (mirrors how `mountSticky` was added earlier as an optional additive hook, not a breaking change to every existing tab). Remix's own `onBeforeHide` is `confirmLeaveIfDirty()`: resolves immediately if there's nothing unsaved or the user already opted out, otherwise shows a modal (Yes/No/Don't ask again) and resolves whatever button was clicked. "Don't ask again" is a real persisted Settings field (`skipRemixLeaveConfirm`, `settings.js`/`ipc.js`/preload, same pattern as every other Settings toggle) rather than local UI state, since the prompt itself carries its own opt-out per the original request - no separate Settings-modal checkbox was added for it. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: a filter change correctly left Undo disabled until the 500ms debounce settled, then Undo/Redo correctly round-tripped the exact value back and forth with Save's dirty state following along at each step; Cancel correctly reverted to the real persisted value; attempting to switch tabs while dirty correctly blocked the switch and showed the dialog (confirmed the Remix section stayed visible and Mixer stayed hidden the whole time), No correctly canceled and left the state untouched, Yes correctly completed the switch; Don't ask again correctly persisted `skipRemixLeaveConfirm` (confirmed via a real settings round-trip) and correctly suppressed the prompt on a subsequent dirty leave; Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Shift+Z correctly fired only while the Remix tab was actually active and correctly left a focused text field's own native undo alone instead of hijacking it.


### The Export plugin (`plugins/export/`)

**Bakes a whole preset down to one audio file of a chosen length and format** - explicitly owner-prioritized ahead of everything else in the backlog ("I like this feature more than the others"), jumping past its own already-high ranked score. Started from an owner design question ("what about choosing a length and exporting an audio? is it hard?") that turned into the owner proposing the actual architecture themselves, live: "use the data inputted on the preset as grounds for a 'simulation'... run a 'simulation' of the events that will transpire within this [duration]... use the simulation output to create a single ffmpeg command." Confirmed sound and built essentially as proposed, with one caveat surfaced back and accepted (see below).

**Simulate first, render second.** `plugins/export/simulate.js` reuses the *exact* randomization/scheduling functions each playMode's real playback source already uses - `randomGapSeconds`/`randomPitchSemitones`/`randomVolumeScale` (mirroring `ScatterSoundSource.js`) rolled repeatedly until cumulative time exceeds the chosen export duration produces a scatter sound's shot list; `secondsUntilNextTrigger` (mirroring `ScheduledSoundSource.js`) rolled the same way produces a scheduled sound's trigger list. This is a dry run of the exact same process a live source uses to schedule its next event, just without any actual audio scheduling - zero new randomization logic, all duplicated per the plugin's own established "bundle your own dependencies" convention (a plugin can't import outside its own directory). **`simulateEvents({ entry, durationSeconds, now })` is the extensibility contract the owner explicitly asked for** ("it needs to work not only with what already exists but the features that will be added too"): dispatches by `playMode` to a per-mode simulator, so a future playMode only needs to add a case here (or, if the list grows, a lookup table) rather than the export pipeline hardcoding scatter/scheduled-specific logic inline. `'loop'` mode returns `null` deliberately - a looped sound has no discrete events, it just tiles.

**A `'times'`-type scheduled sound (real daily HH:MM triggers) is handled exactly per the owner's own explicit instruction**: "treat it as a sound with timed intervals. If the event of it playing falls out of the time scope just exclude it from the export." The simulator starts from the real current wall-clock time and rolls `secondsUntilNextTrigger` forward, including whatever real trigger moments happen to land within the export window and naturally excluding (not specially handling - the loop already stops the instant an offset would exceed the duration) any that wouldn't recur until after it ends. This means running the same export at different times of day can produce a different number of `'times'`-triggered events - an accepted, explicitly-instructed trade-off, not an oversight.

**Rendering (`src/main/ffmpeg/exportMix.js`), the one place the owner's "single ffmpeg command" framing needed a caveat** - surfaced directly and accepted before building: placing hundreds of shots (a scatter sound firing every ~30s across a multi-hour export easily reaches 500+) each with their own delay/pitch/volume in one giant `adelay`+`amix`+per-shot-`rubberband` graph would risk Windows command-line length limits and unwieldy filter-graph parsing. Practical shape instead: one ffmpeg pass **per included sound** - a `'loop'` sound bakes one crossfaded iteration (reusing `loopClip.js`'s existing bake logic completely unchanged, via a new `renderClipToPath` export that takes an explicit output path instead of the sound's own id-keyed managed clip slot, so export renders never disturb a sound's real cached buffer-mode clip) and tiles it via ffmpeg's own `-stream_loop`; a `'scatter'`/`'scheduled'` sound bakes one one-shot "shot" clip (crossfade forced to 0, matching `effectiveCrossfadeSeconds`'s existing reasoning) then places a copy at each simulated event's own offset via `asplit` (splitting the single input into N identical branches inside the filter graph, rather than opening the same short file hundreds of times) + per-branch `rubberband`+`volume`+`afade`+`adelay` + a final `amix`. **The graph is written to a script file and referenced via `-filter_complex_script`** rather than inlined on the command line, specifically so a dense export can't hit a command-line length limit regardless of shot count. Every per-sound track renders to the *exact* export duration (`apad=whole_dur=`); the final mixdown just `amix`es those together, then applies the **whole-mix EQ/gain and fade in/out last** - the owner's own explicit ordering ("should be applied on the whole mix after everything") - before encoding to the chosen format (wav/mp3/opus/flac/ogg).

**Verified directly against the real bundled ffmpeg binary before any of this was wired into the app**, per the project's own established discipline: a synthetic spike test placing 40 then 150 pitch/volume-varied one-shot clips via `asplit`+`adelay`+`amix` first caught a real bug in the *test methodology itself* (`amix`'s default `duration=first` stopped the output at the first branch's own short length, not the intended target - fixed with `duration=longest`+`apad`) before confirming the technique renders correctly and fast (150 shots across 5 minutes in ~4s, ~77x realtime). **Verified end-to-end against the real dev app**: a genuine 3-sound preset (one of each playMode - loop/scatter/scheduled) exported to a real 90-second file in ~1.8s; the output's actual measured duration was exactly 90.00s; RMS-sampling the real decoded audio across the whole file confirmed continuous non-silent content from all three sources, and precise sampling at the very start/end confirmed the fade-in/fade-out ramps land exactly where expected (near-silent at the true start/end, full level by ~1s in); a separate MP3-format export was independently verified to produce a valid, correctly-durationed MP3 stream. New UI: its own tab (not just a Presets-modal button, per the owner's explicit "I think this should be a new plugin... a new tab is the best approach") with preset/length/format pickers, a whole-mix Highpass/Lowpass/Gain+fade section (deliberately a simpler 3-control set for v1, not the full 7-band parametric EQ the per-sound Remix editor has - noted directly as a scope call, not silently reduced), and an Export button that opens a native save-location picker before running.

**Scheduled playback gained the exact same pitch/volume randomization and fade in/out controls Random Interval already has** - direct owner request, flagged explicit priority ("this takes precedence over anything"), reversing `v0.1.77`'s original deliberate scope cut (its own doc comment used to say "no pitch/volume randomization or fade envelope, since the whole point here is a *deterministic* cue at a known time"). Only the *timing* stays deterministic clock math; each trigger's own character is now randomized identically to a scatter shot. Implemented by exporting `randomPitchSemitones`/`randomVolumeScale`/`fadeSeconds`/`scheduleShotEnvelope` from `ScatterSoundSource.js` and importing them into `ScheduledSoundSource.js` (same directory, no cross-plugin restriction, so no third copy needed) - both `BufferScheduledSource` (gained a per-shot `shotGain` node, mirroring `BufferScatterSource`) and `StreamScheduledSource` (gained a persistent `shotGainNode` spliced into its filter chain) now apply the identical detune/playbackRate-pitch + shot-gain-envelope treatment on every trigger. New Remix UI fields (Pitch min/max, Volume min/max, Fade in/out) in the schedule section, threaded through `snapshotOf`'s explicit field list from the start this time. **Also updated the Export plugin's `simulateScheduledEvents`** (a real missed-duplication-site risk caught proactively during this same change, not after shipping) - it previously hardcoded neutral pitch/volume/fade for every scheduled event, which would have silently ignored the new randomization in exported files; now rolls the same `randomPitchSemitones`/`randomVolumeScale` and reads the real `fadeInMs`/`fadeOutMs`/computed shot duration, matching how it already simulates scatter events. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: configured a scheduled sound with a real pitch/volume/fade range, fired it via the Mixer's test-fire button (confirmed already wired for scheduled sounds from `v0.1.75`), and read the live `shotGainNode.gain.value` directly - landed at 0.656, correctly inside the configured 0.5-1.0 range and past the 200ms fade-in's settling point.

**BUG FIX: Export could eat all available RAM and never finish, on any preset with a scatter/scheduled sound whose trimmed clip wasn't a brief "event" length** - reported directly via the inbox ("Export feature is taking half the memory on my computer... I let it run for a loooooooooooong time, it took whatever ram it could get and still produced nothing"). Root cause, confirmed by measuring the real bundled ffmpeg binary rather than guessing: the one-ffmpeg-pass-per-sound design above put every simulated shot's own `rubberband` instance into a *single* filter graph, all held alive at once - and rubberband's cost scales with the shot's own duration, while ffmpeg runs a filter graph's branches on one thread, so N branches means N times the work serialized into one process with no bound. A 1-hour export (the Export tab's own default length) of a single default-settings scatter sound (194 shots) with a realistic ~90-second shot never finished in 90+ seconds and was still climbing past 1.3GB of RAM when killed - reproducing the report exactly. The same 194 shots at a short (3s) shot duration finished fine (56s, 330MB) - so the failure mode was specifically long-shot × many-event combinations, not scatter/export in general, which is why the original spike test (short synthetic clips, ≤150 shots) never caught it. **Fixed by bounding, not restructuring**: `exportMix.js`'s `renderEventBasedTrack` now renders events in fixed-size batches (`EVENT_BATCH_SIZE = 20`, one ffmpeg pass per batch, a size measured directly to stay comfortably within safe territory - 25 branches measured at ~40s/500MB) rather than one graph for every event, then cheaply `amix`es the batch tracks together (no rubberband involved in that step, so it stays fast regardless of batch count). This bounds peak memory to a constant regardless of total event count, trading "one huge process that may never finish" for "several bounded processes that reliably do." Also skips the `rubberband` node entirely for a near-zero pitch shift (`IDENTITY_PITCH_EPSILON_SEMITONES = 0.02`) - a free win matching how `eqNodeType` elsewhere in this codebase treats a true no-op as "skip the node," not "run it at neutral settings." **Verified end-to-end against the real bundled binary at the exact scale that broke before**: the same 194-event/90s-shot/1-hour scenario, run through the new batched pipeline exactly as the code now builds it, completed all 9 batches in ~283s with peak memory flat at ~400MB throughout (never climbing, unlike the unbatched run) - the final merge step (9 tracks -> 1) took ~13s at 286x realtime, and the merged output's real measured duration was exactly 1:00:00.

### The Composite plugin (`plugins/composite/`)

**Composite multi-sound "effect" groups** (e.g. a door: knob turn + creak + bell) - the item that had been gated on the owner confirming a design direction, resolved via a live conversation right after the Scheduled-randomization fix shipped. The owner's own framing superseded Claude's earlier Tags-based recommendation entirely: pick multiple existing sounds, arrange them with their own relative delay, and **bake** them into one new real audio file ("it is in fact a new audio file... so the existing infrastructure is still valid for it") - not a live multi-source playback rig. The result is created via the exact same `library.addSound` every plain import already goes through, so it's a completely normal entry from that point on: Loop/Random Interval/Scheduled, filters, EQ, Export, Save as copy all already work on it with **zero new playback code anywhere else in the app**. A `compositeSource: { members, createdAt }` field marks it purely for display (a gold "Composite" badge on its Mixer row, `SoundRow.js`) and so the Composite tab can find and re-edit it later - it plays no role in playback/eligibility/staleness at all.

**Rendering** (`src/main/ffmpeg/composite.js`): reuses `loopClip.js`'s `renderClipToPath` (built for the Export plugin) to bake each member's own [loopStart,loopEnd] with its own real filters/speedPitch (crossfade forced to 0, same one-shot reasoning Scatter/Scheduled/Doppler already use) into a temp clip, then combines with plain `adelay`+`amix` - a handful of genuinely *different* files, not the same file split N ways like Export's per-shot placement, so no `asplit` needed here. `library.js` gained `markAsComposite`/`updateComposite` for the metadata half; `ipc.js`'s `composite:create`/`composite:rebake` orchestrate resolving member sound IDs to real filter/trim/speedPitch data (mirroring `export:run`'s own id→data boundary) and calling the render.

**Re-editing an existing composite** re-bakes to the *same* file path in place (same id, same `storedFileName`) rather than creating a new entry - resets the same "not yet baked" loop-clip fields a fresh import would have, since the underlying audio content just changed out from under any existing Remix trim.

**Real bug caught during verification, not code review**: the first real bake attempt failed with ffmpeg's "Unable to choose an output format" - traced to the `.tmp`-then-rename atomic-write pattern (borrowed from `loopClip.js`'s own convention) meaning ffmpeg's actual output path ended in `.wav.tmp`, not `.wav` - its format auto-detection reads the *last* extension and found an unrecognized one. Fixed by adding an explicit `-f wav`, which the codebase's other `.tmp`-writing call sites either already had or never needed (writing straight to a real extension with no rename step). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: baked two real sounds (one at delay 0, one at delay 1000ms) into a new composite, confirmed the resulting entry has the exact same field shape a plain import produces (`storageMode: 'copied'`, all default playback fields present) plus `compositeSource`; measured the actual baked file's real duration (3.80s, exactly matching `max(3.0, 1.0+2.8)`) and RMS-sampled real audio content at multiple points confirming both members present at their correct offsets (level measurably increasing once the second member's overlap begins); re-opened it in the Composite tab, changed a delay, re-baked, and confirmed the *same* entry (not a duplicate) was updated in place with a new, correctly-changed duration (3.30s).

**BUG FIX: the Doppler effect didn't sound like a pass-by (v0.1.91)** — reported directly ("just lowering the pitch progressively while the audio plays"). `loopClip.js`'s `buildDopplerSendCmd` ramped pitch *linearly in semitones* from `+intensity` at Start through `0` at the marker to `-intensity` at End - a straight monotonic slide across the whole clip, which is exactly a slow continuous detune, not a "whoosh past." Its own comments even called this "the intended monotonic descent." Replaced with the physical Doppler contour (`f'/f = c / (c + v*cos0)`, where `cos0` swings +1→0→-1 as a source passes on a straight line): pitch now holds roughly flat and high while approaching, swings fast through the marker, holds flat and low while receding - renormalized so the endpoints still land on exactly `±intensity` regardless of the shape. New **Sharpness** control (`speedPitch.dopplerSharpness`, 0..1, default 0.5, a Remix slider next to Intensity) maps exponentially to the model's `k` ("how close does it pass"): low `k` ≈ the old near-linear glide (now the *floor* of the range, not the only option), high `k` ≈ hard plateaus with the whole swing in a burst at the marker. Reversed mode keeps Start/End at natural pitch with the swing concentrated at the marker via a raised-cosine window. Threaded through the usual duplicated sites - `speedPitch` defaults + `normalizeSpeedPitch` in `library.js`, `snapshotOf` + `setSpeedPitchControls` + load defaults in `plugins/editor/index.js`, and the Mixer's own `normalizeSpeedPitch` copy (which was *also* brought up to full parity here - it had been silently missing `dopplerClosestFraction`/`dopplerIntensitySemitones`/`dopplerReversed` too, so a Doppler-settings change on an already-baked sound wasn't invalidating the Mixer's staleness check). **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** (not the dev app - the owner couldn't hear the dev preview and asked to just ship it): baked a 300Hz tone with Doppler at intensity 7 and measured output frequency in 0.4s windows - at sharpness 0.5 the pitch holds ~+6.7st through the first quarter, transitions through the marker at t=½, and plateaus ~-6.7st; at 0.9 it pins at the extremes with a ~0.8s central transition; reversed keeps the ends near natural with the ±swing concentrated at the marker. Existing sounds already baked with Doppler keep their old contour until re-Saved in Remix (their stored settings still match the bake), same as every prior Doppler math change. Default Sharpness (0.5) and the `k` mapping curve are un-feel-tested - flagged to the owner to tune after they hear it in the shipped build.

**In-app "Update Available" modal replacing the native OS dialogs (v0.1.92)** — the whole update flow (found / Download? / no update / check failed) used `dialog.showMessageBox` from the main process; now `src/renderer/core/UpdateModal.js` renders it in-app with the version delta (current → new), the GitHub release body as scrollable release-notes text, and a styled progress bar during download. `autoUpdate.js` was restructured to push `update:*` IPC events (`available`/`progress`/`downloaded`/`not-available`/`error`) to the window instead of popping dialogs — `initAutoUpdate` now takes the `BrowserWindow`, and new exports `getUpdateState`/`downloadUpdateNow`/`dismissUpdate` back three new IPC handlers plus the pre-existing `autoUpdate:checkNow` (which now returns `{ packaged }` so the modal can show a "only in the installed app" message under `npm run dev` instead of spinning forever). The modal holds **no state of its own** — every render is driven by an event or a one-off `getState()` read on open. **Settings entry point**: the "Check for updates now" button became "Check for updates…" and opens the modal (which runs the check and shows the result) rather than firing a check whose result appeared as a separate native dialog. **Preserved decisions**: the single-confirmation rule stays (the one "Download & install" click is the only prompt — download then auto install+restart, now with a 1.5s "restarting to finish installing" beat so the modal can paint it first); `autoInstallUpdates` still skips the modal entirely on detection and only surfaces it at the very end for that same 1.5s restart notice; a background re-check of a version the user dismissed this run stays silent, but an explicit Settings check always surfaces regardless. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: confirmed the preload API surface (`checkNow`/`getState`/`download`/`dismiss`/`onEvent`), that the Settings button opens the modal showing the dev-mode message, and — via a temporary `window.__updateModalTest` hook feeding `handleEvent` directly (removed after) — every render state: `available` (version row + release-notes box + Download button + "Not now" close label), `downloading` with a real percent (`42% · 42.0 MB / 100.0 MB · 5.0 MB/s`, determinate bar) and with none (indeterminate bar + "Starting download…"), `downloaded` (100% bar, close disabled), `not-available`, and `error` (with a "Try again" button); screenshot confirmed the layout reads cleanly on-brand.

**Portable preset export/import — the preset config only, no audio yet (v0.1.93)** — first half of the "share presets with trusted people" backlog item; the owner's own scoping: "do it just the preset first (it'll signal the missing audio files to be loaded if you want to import a preset). Once this works, study a way to bundle the audio some way." A saved preset is only `{ soundId, volume }` (see `presets.js`) — the per-sound DSP settings (trim/filters/EQ/playMode/scatter/schedule/speedPitch/crossfade/tags) live on the shared library entry. The portable form (`src/main/presetPortable.js`, a `.ncvpreset` JSON file) carries the preset name plus, per sound, `{ name, fileName, fileSizeBytes, durationSeconds, volume, settings }` where `settings` is exactly `library.js`'s new exported `IMPORTED_SETTINGS_KEYS` list (+ non-empty `tags`) — the same keys `library.applyImportedSettings` reads back, so export and import can't drift. **Import matching**: `analyzePortablePreset` pairs each portable sound with an existing library entry by case-insensitive `basename(originalPath)` (Windows/NTFS, same key `library.js` already dedups paths on), preferring one whose display name also matches. Matched sounds are used **as-is** — only the mix volume is applied, never the portable settings — which is exactly consistent with how loading a *local* preset already behaves (it never rewrites a sound's trim/filters either). A sound with no match shows in the import dialog as "Needs file — <name>" with a "Locate…" button; `importLocatedSound` then `addSound`s it (`keepCopy: false`, matching a normal file-add) and applies the portable settings so a freshly-located sound comes in sounding as intended — unless that exact file path is already in the library, in which case it reuses that entry (`reused: true`) rather than making a duplicate (`library.findSoundByOriginalPath`, also new). Unresolved sounds are simply left out of the created preset. **UI**: an "Export" button per row in the Presets modal (native save dialog), an "Import preset…" button in that modal's actions row opening a new `#preset-import-dialog` (per-sound resolved/needs-file list + "Create preset"), all wired from `tabs/mixer/index.js` alongside the existing preset load/save/delete. `renderPresetImportList` is the one new bit of `PresetsModal.js`. The `.preset-save-hint` line (previously error-only, red) gained an `.ok` neutral variant reused for "Exported …" / "Preset imported." confirmations. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** — the native file dialogs aren't drivable, so three temporary dev-only IPC hooks (`__test:presetExportTo`/`presetAnalyze`/`presetResolve`, bypassing only the dialog call, removed after) exercised the real `presetPortable.js` functions end to end: exported a real 2-sound preset (one sound carrying a distinctive trim + EQ band + scatter playMode + tags + crossfade), confirmed the manifest captured exactly those settings; re-analyzed it and confirmed both sounds matched their library entries; built a modified manifest with one bogus `fileName` and confirmed that sound came back `matchedSoundId: null` while the other stayed matched; resolved the missing one against a genuinely new `.wav` and confirmed a new library entry was created with all portable settings applied (`storageMode: 'reference'`), and separately against an already-registered file and confirmed it reused that entry with the library not growing and its settings left untouched; drove the real import dialog (via a temporary `window.__testImport` renderer hook, removed after) through "Create preset" and confirmed a new preset with the right soundIds/volumes was saved and the dialog closed; and confirmed the malformed-file and newer-format-version validation error messages. Screenshot confirmed the import dialog layout. **Phase 2 — bundling the audio (v0.1.97)** — picked via the ranking system (7.33 tie, broken by the owner's own pre-authorization: "Once this works, study a way to bundle the audio some way"). **Research** (per [[feedback_research_before_complex_tasks]]): the industry-standard "settings + assets in one shareable file" pattern is a **zip** (DAWproject, the PreSonus/Bitwig cross-DAW format, is exactly this — a zip with `project.xml` + `audio/`). No P2P — a bundle is a file you send however you like, which sidesteps the entire NAT-traversal/signaling surface the owner originally worried about (and their Phase-1 framing already implicitly accepted this). **Zero new dependencies** — a `.ncvpreset` is now a zip written/read by a hand-rolled `src/main/zip.js` (~180 lines: store-method writing, CRC32, central-directory reading, plus `zlib.inflateRawSync` so a bundle re-zipped by Explorer/7-Zip still opens). Verified the output against .NET's `System.IO.Compression.ZipFile`, Windows `Expand-Archive`, and round-tripped a PowerShell `Compress-Archive` (DEFLATE) zip back through the reader. `presetPortable.js` grew `buildPortableBundle`/`writePortableBundle` (manifest = the same Phase-1 schema, now `formatVersion: 2` when audio is included, `1` when not; packed as `preset.json` + `audio/<basename>` with collision-suffixing) and `readPortablePresetFile` (sniffs the `PK\x03\x04` magic — a zip → unpack; else → Phase-1 raw JSON, still supported). **Import lifecycle**: on `pickImport` the bundle's audio is extracted to `userData/preset-import-tmp/<uuid>/` (a session), the analysis marks each sound matched / `fromBundle` / needs-locate, and "Create preset" (`finalizeImport`) adds the `fromBundle` ones via `addSound(keepCopy: true)` + `applyImportedSettings`, saves the preset, and tears the session down; Cancel (`cancelImport`) also tears it down, and `sweepImportSessions()` on startup nukes the whole tmp root (a session never outlives the app). **Security** (the owner explicitly wanted "a safe way"): `sanitizeAudioEntryName` only honors entries directly under `audio/` with a known audio extension and no `..`/nested-dir/`:*?"<>|` trickery; `importSessionFile` re-applies `path.basename`; total/per-file/entry-count caps (1 GB / 512 MB / 250) guard against a zip bomb; a BOM on `preset.json` is stripped before `JSON.parse`. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp dev-only IPC to bypass native dialogs, removed after): exported a 2-sound preset (distinctive trim + EQ band + tags + scatter playMode) → confirmed the `.ncvpreset` is a real zip with `preset.json` + both wavs; deleted the sounds + source files, re-imported → both came in `fromBundle: true`, `finalizeImport` added them as `storageMode: copied` with every setting restored and the preset recreated with correct volumes, temp session cleaned; a Phase-1 raw-JSON `.ncvpreset` still analyzed correctly (matched an in-library sound, flagged a missing one for Locate); and a **hostile bundle** (`audio/../../hack.wav`, `audio/virus.exe`, `audio/subdir/nested.wav`) extracted only the one legit `.wav` and wrote nothing outside the session dir. Screenshot confirmed the import dialog's "Included in this file" state.

**Export progress bar + verbose log (v0.1.98)** — inbox request ("a clearer way to see what's going on when the user tries to export the preset as an audio, like a progress bar + verbose of what's going on under the hood"), picked via the ranking system (4/4/3 = 3.67, lowest open). The Export plugin's multi-minute batched ffmpeg pipeline (`exportMix.js`) was fire-and-forget from the renderer's view — one spinner line. Now: `runFfmpeg.js`'s `runFfmpegToFile` takes an optional `{ onProgress }` that parses ffmpeg's own `time=HH:MM:SS.ms` stderr lines into output-seconds; `exportMix.js` gained a `makeReporter(onProgress, sounds)` that counts the *heavy* passes (each loop tile / event batch / batch-combine / final mixdown — the quick shot renders aren't counted, just announced) and emits `{ type:'step', label, fraction }` / `{ type:'tick', fraction }` (fraction 0..1 across the whole export) threaded through every render function. `ipc.js`'s `export:run` handler forwards these to `event.sender.send('export:progress', …)` (ticks throttled to ~120ms, steps always through so the log stays complete); `preload` exposes `export.onProgress(cb)`; the plugin (`plugins/export/index.js`) subscribes in `mount()`, shows a progress bar (`#export-progress-bar` width = %) + a scrolling log (`#export-log`, one line per step) while exporting, and unsubscribes in `onunload()`. The renderer passes each sound's `name` through now so the log reads "Placing "Chirp" (2/2): batch 3 of 3…", "Combining 3 batches…", "Final mixdown…", "Encoding to WAV — done." **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (a temp `window.__exportPlugin` hook + a Proxy over `this.api.export` to stub the native save dialog, both removed after): ran a real 2-sound export (a looped bed + a scatter sound simulating 51 plays over 2.5 min → 3 batches), confirmed the log captured all 9 pipeline steps in order with real sound names, the bar advanced monotonically (0 → ⅓ → ⅔ → 1, matching 2 sounds + mixdown = 3 units) and finished at 100%, and a fast export still shows every step even when ffmpeg passes complete inside the tick throttle window.

**Export pipeline rewrite — "make multi-hour Export as fast as possible", Part A (v0.1.99)** — owner-prioritized (`#next`, "this has to get the fastest it can"). The old `exportMix.js` ran one full-export-duration ffmpeg pass *per sound per event-batch*, plus a per-sound batch-combine, plus a final mixdown — `~2N+2` full-duration passes, so a 3h export with a couple of Random Interval sounds churned through 100+ hours of audio. Rewritten to **~1 full-duration pass total**: (1) one short "shot" clip per sound (unchanged `renderClipToPath` bake; a loop sound's already-cached `getLoopClipPathForId` clip is now reused directly when its trim/filters/crossfade/speed still match — `ipc.js`'s `export:run` resolves and passes `cachedClipPath`); (2) each scatter/scheduled sound's events (chronological — verified in `simulate.js`) split into `EVENT_BATCH_SIZE=20` contiguous batches, each rendered over **only its own real time span** (`planBatches`: first event offset → last event offset + pitch-adjusted shot length + `BATCH_TAIL_MARGIN_SECONDS`), not `apad`'d to the whole export length — so the sum of every batch's span across every sound is ~1× the export duration instead of `nBatches × duration`; (3) one final ffmpeg pass — every loop shot as a `-stream_loop -1 -i` input through `volume`, every sliced batch track `adelay`'d to its absolute offset, all `amix`ed, then whole-mix highpass/lowpass/gain + fade in/out applied last, then encoded. Intermediates are now **FLAC** (`INTERMEDIATE_EXT`, roughly halves the multi-GB temp I/O a long dense export writes) and forced to stereo/44.1k (`aformat=channel_layouts=stereo` on every branch, `adelay=delays=N:all=1` not the old hardcoded `N|N`) so the final `amix` never hits a channel-layout mismatch (the old code silently assumed stereo). Sounds that never fire in the window are skipped entirely (`events.length === 0` → not added to the mix); an all-silent export still produces a valid full-length file via an `anullsrc` fallback. The v0.1.98 progress reporter was reworked from "count heavy passes" to **weight by audio-seconds** (`makeReporter(onProgress, totalWorkSeconds)` where total = `durationSeconds + Σ batch spans`; each `renderSlicedBatch` reports against its span, the final mix against the full duration) — verified monotonic to 1.0.

**Deliberate behavior change, flagged to the owner:** all `amix` stages now use `normalize=0` (honest summing) instead of ffmpeg's default `normalize=1` (which scales every input by `1/nInputs`). The old cascaded `normalize=1` at three stages (per-batch amix ÷≤20, batch-combine ÷nBatches, final ÷nSounds) meant a Random-Interval/Scheduled sound in a long multi-batch mix was attenuated to near-inaudibility — measured directly in an A/B on identical input: old-pipeline output had a scatter event's own window at exactly the same level as the loop-only windows (i.e. the events contributed nothing measurable), while the new pipeline has them ~3–4 dB above the bed as intended. Consequence: **exported files are ~5–7 dB louder overall** and scatter/scheduled sounds are now prominent. Honest summing matches how the live Mixer's own `AudioContext` combines per-sound gains. Clipping risk is low in practice (4 full-scale decorrelated tones at `volume:1.0` summed to only −9 dB peak in a stress test) and the whole-mix Gain control (−24…+24 dB) is the release valve; noted in the CHANGELOG so it isn't a surprise.

**Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness that imports the *actual* `exportMix.js` (bare `electron` stubbed through a `module.register` resolve hook, so `loopClip.js`/`runFfmpeg.js`/`ffmpegPath.js` all run unmodified): a 10-min 3-sound export (loop + 27-event scatter + 2-event scheduled, one source deliberately mono) → `ok`, output exactly 600.00s, stereo, no clipping, progress reached 1.0 without going backwards, **zero leftover temp files**, mono source correctly upmixed with no `amix` channel error; event placement confirmed by windowed `volumedetect` (the scheduled chime's 30s window measured ~4 dB above a bed-only window). Edge cases: cached-clip reuse leaves the sound's real managed clip on disk untouched; a non-firing scatter sound is skipped without crashing the mix; an all-non-firing preset yields a correct-length silent file; MP3/other formats still encode. Benchmark (30-min export, 1 loop + 2 scatter × 119 events × 7s rubberband shots): new pipeline **56.7s, exactly 1800.00s output, node peak RSS 64 MB, no clipping** — the old pipeline was still running when killed at the 2-minute mark on the same input. Part B (a "Faster export — uses more CPU and memory" toggle: bounded core-parallel short renders, rubberband speed mode, segmented parallel mixdown) is deliberately deferred to its own pass.

**Export pipeline — Part B (v0.1.100)** — the resource-hungry half, behind a new Settings checkbox **"Faster export — uses more CPU and memory"** (`settings.fasterExport`, OFF by default). Two of the three planned pieces shipped; the third (segmented parallel mixdown, item 11) stayed deferred — it's the least valuable (only helps many-core machines on very long exports) and the highest correctness risk (concat-boundary artifacts). **(1) Bounded-parallel independent passes**: `exportMix.js`'s formerly-sequential shot bakes + sliced-batch renders now run through a new `mapPool(items, concurrency, worker)` (at `concurrency: 1` it's a plain sequential walk, so the OFF path is byte-identical to pre-toggle). Restructured from "bake a sound's shot → render its batches → clean its shot" interleaved, to three phases: bake *every* shot (loop + event) → render *every* batch flattened across all event sounds (keeps the pool saturated across sound boundaries) → final mixdown; all owned shot clips are kept alive through the batch phase and cleaned at the very end. Concurrency = `fasterExport ? max(1, min(cpus-1, FASTER_EXPORT_CONCURRENCY_CAP=4)) : 1` — hard-capped because the pipeline has a documented "took all my RAM and never finished" bug (v0.1.85); a measured batch is ~180 MB, so 4 in flight ≈ 730 MB, well below blowup territory and firmly bounded (unlike v0.1.85's unbounded single graph). **(2) rubberband speed mode**: `eventBranchChain` appends `:pitchq=speed` to the rubberband filter when `fasterExport` — its fast, slightly-lower-quality pitch mode; gated separately-conceptually but under the same toggle since it's an unexpected quality change, not just a resource one. **Architecture note**: `exportMix` takes `fasterExport` as an explicit parameter (the `export:run` IPC handler passes `getSettings().fasterExport` straight through) rather than importing `settings.js` itself — keeps the module free of the electron-store dependency so the ffmpeg Node harness can still import it unmodified (importing `settings.js` broke the harness: electron-store, run under plain `node` with no real Electron, ignores a stubbed `app` and falls back to `%APPDATA%\electron-store-nodejs\...`, so the setting always read false). The progress reporter (`makeReporter`) gained a monotonic clamp on every reported fraction, since several phases now tick concurrently and a fresh batch's early tick could otherwise read lower than a previous one. **Verified**: `mapPool` unit-tested (max-in-flight honored, all items processed, sequential at concurrency 1, empty-list safe, first-error propagates); `rubberband=pitch=X:pitchq=speed` confirmed accepted by the bundled ffmpeg with correct output duration; the real `exportMix.js` run through the Node harness (electron stubbed) at two scales — a 10-min/41-event case (OFF 11.7s → ON 9.7s) and a 20-min/115-event/16s-shot case (**OFF 64.6s → ON 39.9s, ~1.6×**), both with output duration exact, windowed RMS equivalent between modes (≤0.2 dB drift from `pitchq=speed`), progress monotonic to 1.0, zero leftover temp files, no clipping; peak aggregate ffmpeg working set OFF ~190 MB vs ON ~730 MB (bounded, as designed). CDP against the real dev app confirmed the Settings checkbox round-trips (`settings.get().fasterExport` false→true→false) and that `getSettings().fasterExport` is what the `export:run` handler now forwards.

**"What's new" screen on demand — Settings entry point (v0.1.100)** — inbox request ("The what's new screen should also be accessible via settings menu"). Was auto-open-once-after-update only. Added a **"What's new"** button next to "Check for updates…" in Settings. `autoUpdate.js` gained `getWhatsNewForCurrentVersion()` (new `autoUpdate:getWhatsNewCurrent` IPC + `preload` `getWhatsNewCurrent()`) — returns the *current* version's changelog entry via `changelogTextBetween(version, version)` (which falls through its range filter to the exact-match branch; `v0.1.102` collapsed a duplicated inline reimplementation of that helper into the call), always (not consume-once), works in dev too (no `app.isPackaged` gate, unlike the update check), `previousVersion: null` since it's not tied to an update. `UpdateModal.js` gained `openWhatsNewModal()` and its `'whats-new'` render state now handles `previousVersion == null`: the version row (a "from → to" pair) is hidden, and the message reads "You're running Noctívago vX.Y.Z." instead of "updated from … to …". **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: the button opens the modal titled "What's new in v0.1.99" with the right message, version row hidden, the changelog entry in the notes box, Download hidden, Close enabled.

**"Suggest a loop point" — automatic loop-region detection in Remix (v0.1.94)** — picked via the ranking system (7.33, but the one tied-lowest item that was fully de-risked: research already done, concrete design ready). `src/main/ffmpeg/loopSuggest.js`: `suggestLoopPoints(inputPath, durationSeconds, options)` → `{ ok, wholeFile, loopStart, loopEnd }`. **Analysis-only** — it just populates the Start/End fields (through the exact same path a manual edit takes, so Save + undo pick it up); it never bakes or renders anything, the existing crossfade-rotation bake (`loopClip.js`) still does the actual seam-smoothing on whatever region gets saved. **Why it doesn't reuse PyMusicLooper/LoopAuditioneer's approach**: those target *musical* content and lean on beat/pitch periodicity; Noctívago's sounds (rain/wind/forest/crowd) are non-tonal textures with no periodic structure to phase-align, so the perceptually relevant cue at a loop seam is *energy-envelope continuity*, not sample phase. So this works on a cheap per-frame energy envelope — full-band RMS + a low/high split from a one-pole 500Hz filter, computed as it streams (flat memory regardless of file length, same reason `waveformPeaks.js` reduces to peaks instead of buffering). **Search**: one ffmpeg decode to 22050 mono → 20ms-frame envelope, normalized per-feature by its own median (scale-invariant across files, still preserves *within*-file level jumps as a real cost). Two-stage: a coarse pass over an envelope downsampled to ~2000 frames (cost bounded no matter the length; adaptive stride if the loop-length range × frame count would exceed ~800K candidate pairs), then a full-resolution refine within a window of the coarse winner (capped at 250 frames / 5s so a multi-hour file's wide coarse cell can't blow up stage 2 — past a few seconds of precision the difference isn't audible for looping ambience anyway). Seam score = triangular-weighted sum of per-frame `|Δfull| + |Δlow| + |Δhigh|` over a ±0.5s window around each candidate seam, minus a mild length bonus (`LENGTH_WEIGHT = 0.02`) so a longer region is preferred when the seam matches equally well (more material = less obviously repetitive) — but only mildly, never enough to pick a much worse seam for length. Loop-length constrained to `[12s, 75s]` (both clamped to the file); files ≤5s return `wholeFile: true`. New `audio:suggestLoopPoints` IPC + `api.audio.suggestLoopPoints`, called from a new "Suggest a loop point" button in the Remix `.editor-times` area (with an inline status line: "Analyzing…" → "Suggested 0:17 loop — adjust if needed, then Save."). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** with synthetic test files built to exercise each behavior: a 40s stationary-texture file → a near-max-length loop (correct — stationary content loops fine anywhere, longer is less repetitive); a 50s wind bed with a loud 2s gust at t=32 → seam correctly *avoids* landing on the gust (it plays once per loop cycle); a 40s file that's calm for 20s then agitated → loop region stays entirely within the calm half, not straddling the loudness jump (the key discriminating case, confirmed both in the returned numbers and visually in a screenshot of the waveform's loop overlay); a 12-minute file → 630ms total, bounded. Full UI flow verified: button disables + "Analyzing…" during, Start/End/Length update, Save enables, undo history commits, and a real Save after suggesting bakes the suggested region correctly (`loopClipReady`, clip points match). **Un-feel-tested tunables flagged to the owner**: `LENGTH_WEIGHT` (0.02) and the `[12s, 75s]` preferred-length range — a `## Questions` inbox note asks whether the suggested loops feel right.

**"What's new" screen after an update installs (v0.1.95)** — inbox request: "The update screen should also open when the update is done. And show a summary/rundown of what changed on this version." A new repo-root `CHANGELOG.md` (user-facing prose, one `## vX.Y.Z — Heading` section per release, newest first — the counterpart to the release *title* I already set per release; keep bullets one-line since the in-app box wraps them) is inlined into the main bundle via `import ... from '../../CHANGELOG.md?raw'` (verified `?raw` works in electron-vite's main build — no fs, no resources-path resolution, identical dev/packaged). `src/main/changelog.js` parses it and `changelogEntriesBetween(from, to)` / `changelogTextBetween(from, to)` return every entry strictly after `from` up to and including `to` (so updating across several releases at once shows all of them, newest first; falls back to just the `to` entry if `from` predates the changelog). **Detection**: `settings.js` gained `lastRunVersion`; `autoUpdate.js`'s new `detectWhatsNew()` (called at the top of `initAutoUpdate`) compares it to `app.getVersion()`, always persists the current version, and — only for a packaged build, and only when the stored version exists and differs — builds `whatsNew = { version, previousVersion, notes }`. New `getWhatsNew()` IPC (`autoUpdate:getWhatsNew`) is **consume-once** (nulls the module var after returning it, so a renderer reload — really only a dev thing — doesn't re-pop it). **UI**: `UpdateModal.js` gained a `'whats-new'` render status reusing the existing modal — dynamic title (`What's new in vX.Y.Z`), a `previousVersion → version` row (the version row and notes box are now shown for `available` OR `whats-new`), the changelog text in the notes box (or a `You're now on vX.Y.Z.` fallback if the version has no changelog entry), and just a Close button. `installUpdateModal()` calls `getWhatsNew()` once on startup and opens the modal in that state if non-null (deferred + guarded so a real `update:available` from the startup check still wins if it lands first). This covers *both* halves of the request: "open when the update is done" = the modal reopening post-restart, and "rundown of what changed" = the changelog notes. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temporary `!process.env.NOCTIVAGO_TEST_WHATSNEW` un-gate + directly editing the dev `settings.json`'s `lastRunVersion`, both reverted): with `lastRunVersion` set to `0.1.93` the modal auto-opened on startup titled "What's new in v0.1.94" with the `0.1.93 → 0.1.94` row and the v0.1.94 changelog entry; with it set to `0.1.90` the notes box correctly showed all 4 intervening entries (0.1.94→0.1.91) newest-first, excluding 0.1.90 itself; a normal launch (stored version == current) showed nothing and logged nothing; the Settings "Check for updates…" path still works unchanged; screenshot confirmed the layout. **Also**: going forward the GitHub release body should be set from the CHANGELOG entry (`gh release edit <tag> --notes ...`) — done manually as part of the release step for now (the release workflow only sets the title), which also makes the *update-available* modal show notes, since electron-updater's GitHub provider reads `info.releaseNotes` from the release body.

**Sleep timer (v0.1.96)** — inbox request ("a feature for setting up a timer that will turn off your computer"), owner-confirmed to build. New moon button in the toolbar (`#open-sleep-timer`, between the mute button and the spacer) opens a modal: a minutes field + quick-preset chips (15/30/45/60/90/120) and a "when the time is up" radio group — **Stop all sounds** (default), **Put the computer to sleep**, **Shut down the computer**. **Architecture**: the *timer itself* lives in the main process (`src/main/sleepTimer.js`, a plain `setTimeout` - deliberately not a renderer timer, which Chromium throttles hard when the window is minimised to the tray, exactly the state this runs in; same lesson as the v0.1.71 scatter-rAF bug). On elapse main sends `sleep-timer:elapsed` to the renderer; `core/SleepTimer.js` handles it: `stop` dispatches a `noctivago:stop-all-playback` window CustomEvent the Mixer listens for (same DOM-event bridge as `noctivago:solo`, since the core module has no import path into Mixer playback state), while `sleep`/`shutdown` open a **second warning modal** with a 30-second countdown that can still cancel - only when *that* completes (or "Do it now") does the renderer call `sleepTimer:runEndAction`, which spawns `shutdown /s /t 0` or `rundll32.exe powrprof.dll,SetSuspendState 0,1,0` (detached+unref'd; sleep may hibernate instead on a system with hibernation enabled - accepted, no clean bundled-tool-free fix). So a destructive action is never one timeout away from firing - the warning always gets to intervene. The last-used minutes + action persist via `settings.js` (`sleepTimerMinutes`/`sleepTimerAction`) so a nightly user isn't re-picking every time; the *active* timer is session-only main-process state (not resumed across an app restart - a sleep timer is too "now" for that to make sense). The toolbar button shows the live countdown (`#sleep-timer-remaining`, from `formatDuration` on `firesAt - Date.now()`, ticked by a renderer `setInterval` purely for display) and goes `.btn-svg-icon-active` while running. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (with a temporary `NOCTIVAGO_TEST_NO_SHUTDOWN` env guard neutering the actual OS command, removed after): the setup→active UI transition (countdown, toolbar readout, button state, Start↔Cancel swap); preset chips set the minutes field; a 2s `stop` timer fired the `noctivago:stop-all-playback` event exactly once and cleared itself; a 2s `shutdown` timer opened the warning modal ("Noctívago will shut down your computer in 30 seconds."), Cancel dismissed it *without* running the command (confirmed via the absence of the neutered command's log line), and "Do it now" on a `sleep` timer did log the (neutered) sleep command; cancelling an active timer from the modal restored the setup view; and the minutes/action prefs round-tripped through `settings` across a close-and-reopen. Screenshot confirmed the modal layout.

**Seamless looping for long stream-mode sounds (v0.1.101)** — the ranked backlog's "hybrid bridge-clip seamless loop" item. A sound whose loop region is over the `MAX_BUFFER_CLIP_SECONDS` (600s) cap never gets a baked buffer clip, so it looped via `LocalFileSoundSource`'s plain `currentTime = loopStart` hard seek — an audible click/gap on any content that doesn't already self-loop. **Design fork surfaced to the owner** (real fork in the app's most-debugged subsystem, owner originated the feature with a specific approach in mind): their original idea was a pre-baked ffmpeg "bridge clip" (render a ~2s tail⨯head crossfade segment, hand off stream→buffer→stream at the seam); while implementing, a simpler approach that hits the same goal turned up and the owner picked it — **two `<audio>` elements of the same file** feeding a pair of crossfade `GainNode`s that sum into the shared filter chain. No ffmpeg round-trip, no `clips/<id>.bridge.wav` cache, no staleness tracking, filters stay live on both voices, the loop region stays exact, and it's automatic (not opt-in) since it's cheap. See the rewritten "Stream mode" paragraph above for the mechanism (`_tick` seam detection → `_startCrossfade` → `setTimeout`-driven `_finalizeCrossfade` role swap). `fade` reuses the existing per-sound `entry.crossfadeSeconds` (the same Remix "Loop crossfade" slider that drives the baked crossfade — which was always shown for stream-mode sounds but previously did nothing for them); `null` → a local `DEFAULT_STREAM_CROSSFADE_SECONDS = 0.2` mirroring `loopClip.js`'s already-feel-tuned `DEFAULT_CROSSFADE_SECONDS`; an explicit `0` is a real opt-out back to the hard seek. Mixer changes are two lines: pass `crossfadeSeconds: entry.crossfadeSeconds` into the `LocalFileSoundSource` constructor, and a `hasCrossfadeSeconds`/`setCrossfadeSeconds` reconcile in the `loop-stream` branch. Buffer mode (`BufferSoundSource`), `desiredSourceKind`, `loopClipEligible` — all untouched. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** by importing `SoundSource.js` + `AudioEngine.js` directly and driving a real `LocalFileSoundSource` on a generated 8s test file (+ a temp `disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows` switch so the backgrounded dev window's timers/rAF weren't clamped, and a temp `window.__mixerState` hook, both removed after): structure (2 elements, gains [1,0], active tracking); a full crossfade sampled mid-ramp (`g0` 0.8→0.6→0.4→0.2→0 while `g1` 0.2→0.4→0.6→0.8→1, sum 1.0 throughout, standby playing from `loopStart`, then active index flips, gains snap to 1/0, retired element paused and rewound); the real rAF `_tick` loop cycling through **3 seamless loops** in 6s on a 2s region unattended; `crossfadeSeconds: 0` → immediate exact seek to `loopStart`, no crossfade; pause-mid-crossfade finalizes synchronously and resume restores gains/playback; dispose during a pending crossfade doesn't throw and clears everything; filter + EQ (add/remove band, topology rebuild) live-updates still work through the new summed chain; and a real >600s stream-mode sound included + played through the actual Mixer builds a `loop-stream` `LocalFileSoundSource` with 2 elements, no console errors, position advancing at 1×. **Not feel-tested** — the 0.2s default reuses buffer mode's own owner-tuned constant and is per-sound adjustable in Remix, but whether the dual-element seam *sounds* as clean as the baked one on real ambient audio, and whether automatic (vs. opt-in) is the right call, want the owner's ear on the shipped build — flagged in the inbox Questions with an offer to make it opt-in.

**Small fixes from a self-review pass (v0.1.102)** — no inbox items and the ranked backlog's autonomously-pickable list was exhausted after v0.1.101, so a read-only `/code-review` of the v0.1.99–101 diffs stood in for a task. Six findings; the four safe/mechanical ones shipped here, the two behavioral ones went to the inbox Questions. Shipped: (1) `SoundSource.js` `_recover()` (stream-mode stall recovery) called `el.load()` — which per the HTML spec resets `playbackRate` to 1 — but never restored `this.speed`, so a stall-recovered sound with a non-default Speed silently reverted to 1×; now re-applies it right after `load()`. (2) `_tick()`'s seam-crossfade trigger compared `currentTime` against `loopEnd - fade` where `fade` is wall-clock, so at Speed ≠ 1 the active voice over/undershoots `loopEnd` by `(speed-1)·fade`; now triggers at `loopEnd - fade*speed`. Both only bite the rare long-loop-region + non-default-Speed combination (default Speed 1× is byte-identical). (3) `exportMix.js`'s `mapPool` rejected on the first worker error like `Promise.all` but left the other in-flight runners pulling new items — under "Faster export" a failed export kept spawning ffmpeg in the background and leaked temp files whose paths landed in the cleanup arrays *after* the `finally` already ran; now every runner stops pulling on first error, the pool waits for the ≤`concurrency-1` stragglers to settle, then rethrows, so cleanup sees complete arrays. (4) `bakeShotClip` named its shot clips `.flac` but `renderClipToPath` always writes `-f wav` pcm — renamed to `.wav` so the extension is honest (the sliced-batch tracks are the real FLAC intermediates). Also: `getWhatsNewForCurrentVersion` was a byte-for-byte reimplementation of `changelog.js`'s `changelogTextBetween` — collapsed to a call. **The two deferred to inbox Questions**: scatter/scheduled export shots are pitched with `rubberband=pitch` (duration-preserving) while live playback resamples via `detune`/`playbackRate` (pitch+tempo coupled, shorter when pitched up) — so exports don't match the Mixer for any sound with pitch variation (the scatter default), and the fade-out envelope + batch span math in `exportMix`/`simulate.js` both use the resampled duration against a rubberband-rendered shot. Recommended fix (owner's call): switch event shots to resampling — matches live *and* is far cheaper than rubberband, which would sidestep much of the Part B rubberband-cost problem for these. **Not run against the dev app** — all four are mechanical, `node --check`-clean, and no-ops on the default paths.

**Presets list layout — long names no longer clipped (v0.1.103)** — inbox note: the Presets modal cut preset names off "practically entirely," owner asked for the modal to accommodate the text smarter (scale with the window, or just resize). Root cause: `.preset-row` was a single flex row — name (`flex:1`, ellipsis) followed by three `btn-small btn-icon-text` buttons (Load / Export / Delete), which in the fixed-360px `.modal-card` left ~80px for the name. Fixed two ways: (1) `#presets-modal .modal-card` (and the sibling `#preset-import-dialog`) now `width: min(520px, 92vw)` — grows with the window (the app default window is only 480px wide, resizable with no max), capped at 520; (2) `.preset-row` is now `flex-direction: column` — the name gets the full card width on its own line, with a new `.preset-row-actions` flex row of equal-width (`flex:1`) buttons beneath it. `renderPresetList` in `PresetsModal.js` wraps the three buttons in the `.preset-row-actions` div and sets `name.title` for a full-name tooltip when a genuinely long name still ellipsises. `#preset-list` gained `max-height: min(52vh, 440px)` + `overflow-y:auto` so a long preset list scrolls inside the modal instead of pushing the Close/Import row off-screen. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch adding `--remote-debugging-port`, removed after): at the 480px default window the name span measures ~348px wide (was being squeezed to a fraction of that); screenshot confirmed all four of the owner's real saved presets show their full names with buttons evenly spread below; at a 900px-emulated viewport the card caps at 520px and a 65-char synthetic name fits on one line untruncated; list scrolls within its max-height. Pure CSS + DOM-structure change, no JS logic touched.

**Roomier modals app-wide + Faster-export toggle relocated to the Export tab (v0.1.104)** — the owner broadened the v0.1.103 inbox note ("the app's modals in general are too tight, noticed mostly in presets") and added a second note ("faster export toggle should be on the export tab, not settings — you're about to create an export, you should tell the app then whether to drain resources"). (1) `.modal-card` base width `360px` (+ `max-width:90vw`) → `min(440px, 92vw)` so every dialog grows with the window and no longer squeezes content; `.modal-card-wide` `680px` → `min(680px, 94vw)`; added `max-height:88vh; overflow-y:auto` to `.modal-card` so a tall dialog (Settings) scrolls internally instead of overflowing. The `#presets-modal`/`#preset-import-dialog` `min(520px, 92vw)` override from v0.1.103 stays. (2) The `#settings-faster-export` checkbox-row is gone from the Settings modal (`index.html`) and its wiring removed from `SettingsMenu.js`; a new `#export-faster` checkbox lives on the Export tab (`plugins/export/index.js` `mount()`, between Format and Whole-mix processing, new `.export-check` style in the plugin's `styles.css`). It's still backed by the same persisted `settings.fasterExport` (read on mount via `this.api.settings.get()`, written on change via `setFasterExportEnabled`) — so `ipc.js`'s `export:run` handler is unchanged (`getSettings().fasterExport`), the last choice still sticks between sessions, and it's now a decision made at export time. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp switch, removed after): the Export tab checkbox renders with wrapped label text and reflects `settings.fasterExport`; toggling it round-trips to the settings store (`afterOn:true` / `afterOff:false`); the Settings modal no longer contains the row or the text "Faster export" and lays out cleanly without it; presets modal card measures ~427px at the 464px inner width.

**Explicit "Fully random" toggles for Random Interval / Scheduled pitch + gap (v0.1.105)** — from the owner's inbox answer to the v0.1.102 Question: "Random interval audios should not be full random if they're at 0 pitch variation... If they're set to 0 it should be 0. But you should also add a toggle... saying 'Fully random'." Since v0.1.34, `randomPitchSemitones` treated `minPitchSemitones === 0 && maxPitchSemitones === 0` as a *hidden* trigger for fully-random ±12 st — now 0/0 means literally no variation, and two new explicit boolean flags drive the fully-random behavior:
- `scatter.pitchFullyRandom` / `schedule.pitchFullyRandom` → `randomPitchSemitones` rolls uniformly across the whole ±`DEFAULT_PITCH_RANGE_SEMITONES` (12) st range, ignoring the min/max bounds.
- `scatter.gapFullyRandom` → `randomGapSeconds` rolls uniformly in `[0, FULLY_RANDOM_MAX_GAP_SECONDS]` (new const, 120s — deliberately *not* the 3600s the Remix field allows, since a fully-random gap averaging half an hour is just "never plays"; feel-tunable, flagged in inbox Questions). No gap equivalent for `schedule` (its timing is deterministic clock math).

Threaded through every duplicated site the scatter/schedule config already touches: `ScatterSoundSource.js` (`randomPitchSemitones`/`randomGapSeconds` logic + `normalizeScatter` staleness key), `ScheduledSoundSource.js` (`normalizeSchedule` key — it imports `randomPitchSemitones` so gets the logic for free), `library.js` (scatter + schedule defaults, both `false`; comment rewrite), `plugins/export/simulate.js` (its necessarily-duplicated copies of both fns), and `plugins/editor/index.js` (three new checkboxes — `#editor-scatter-gap-fully-random`, `#editor-scatter-pitch-fully-random`, `#editor-schedule-pitch-fully-random` — in the scatter/schedule control HTML + `.editor-scatter-check` style; els, change listeners, `currentScatterConfig`/`setScatterControls`/`currentScheduleConfig`/`setScheduleControls`, `snapshotOf` dirty-check keys, and `updateScatterRangeDisabled`/`updateSchedulePitchDisabled` which grey out the now-inert min/max inputs when a box is checked). **Behavior change for existing sounds** (owner-accepted, in the CHANGELOG): a scatter sound previously left at pitch 0/0 expecting hidden fully-random pitch now plays at fixed pitch until re-opened in Remix and the box ticked. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp switch, removed): importing the real `ScatterSoundSource.js` — `randomPitchSemitones({0,0})` returns exactly 0 across 5000 rolls (was ±12), `{pitchFullyRandom:true}` spans −11.99..12.0, `{2,5}` stays in [2,5]; a real `BufferScatterSource.hasScatterConfig` flags a change in either new flag (staleness works); in the Remix UI, loading a saved scatter sound restores both checkboxes, ticking one greys its min/max + flips Save to enabled (dirty-check sees it), and a real Save persists `scatter.pitchFullyRandom:true` to the library. Screenshot confirmed the layout. The bigger half of that same Question — pitch shifting shouldn't change shot *length* (owner wants it length-preserving on both Mixer and export; would need a phase-vocoder AudioWorklet for live playback) — is deferred to its own pass, left in inbox Questions.

**Remix: full sound names + a roomier sticky bar (v0.1.106)** — inbox note: "Remix tab is also a bit crumpled up. The names frequently don't show up complete and the autoscroll on the name if it's cut off doesn't work properly." The Remix panel's selected-sound `<h3>` (`#editor-sound-name`) truncated with a plain `text-overflow: ellipsis` and had **no marquee at all** — the Mixer got hover marquee-scroll on truncated names back in v0.1.52 but the plugin never did. Fixed by duplicating the Mixer's exact mechanism into `plugins/editor/` (per the plugin's "bundle your own copies" convention): a module-level `wireNameMarquee(el)` (mirrors `SoundRow.js`'s `wireMarquee` — same `MARQUEE_BASE_PX_PER_SEC`/`SPEED_SCALE`/`MAX` constants, same `scrollWidth − clientWidth` overflow test, same `--marquee-distance`/`--marquee-duration` custom props) wired once on `this.els.soundName` in `mount()`, plus `.editor-sound-name-marquee` + an `@keyframes editor-sound-name-marquee` in `styles.css` mirroring `main.css`'s `.sound-row-name-marquee` (held start/end, translateX to the negative overflow mid-cycle). Also: `h3.title` is now set to the full name (tooltip fallback); a new `setSearchValue(name)` helper (used by `selectPickerSound` and `refreshLibrary`) sets the picker input's value **and** `title` **and** resets `selectionStart`/`scrollLeft` to 0 so a too-long name shows its *start* in the field, not its cut-off tail; picker dropdown `<li>`s gained `white-space:nowrap; overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis` + a per-option `title` so long names list one-per-line instead of wrapping. Sticky-bar de-crumple: `.tab-sticky-panel[data-tab-id='remix']` gap `10px` → `6px 8px`, and `.editor-sticky-progress` `flex: 1` → `flex: 1 1 90px; min-width: 90px` so the progress bar wraps as a unit rather than collapsing to a sliver when the bar's ~12 controls wrap at the default narrow window. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed after): selecting a sound sets `h3.title` + `soundSearch.title` + `scrollLeft: 0`; dispatching `mouseenter` on a truncated `h3` adds `editor-sound-name-marquee` with a computed `--marquee-distance: -44px` / `--marquee-duration`; dropdown `<li>` computed style is `text-overflow: ellipsis / white-space: nowrap`; screenshot confirmed the tighter sticky rows. Pure display/CSS change — no playback, bake, or persistence code touched.

**BUG FIX: a shared preset with bundled audio lost every per-sound setting on import (v0.1.107)** — reported via the inbox ("export a preset and send it to someone [over WhatsApp]... when you finally import a preset it doesn't retain the settings of the audio files. It just started playing everything on loop very LOUDLY"). **Root cause**, reproduced directly against the real dev app (two temp dev-only IPC hooks `__test:presetExportTo`/`presetImportFrom` bypassing the native dialogs, removed after): `analyzePortablePreset` (`src/main/presetPortable.js`) matched every portable sound against the recipient's own library **by file basename first** (`matchExistingSound`), and only fell back to the bundled audio when *no* basename match was found (`const fromBundle = !match && …`). Two unrelated sounds sharing a filename (`rain.mp3`) is common, so on the recipient's machine the preset silently bound to *their* own, unconfigured `rain.mp3` — `playMode: 'loop'` instead of `scatter`/`scheduled` ("everything on loop"), `filters.gainDb: 0` instead of the sender's −18/−20 dB ("very LOUDLY"), no trim, default volume. The bundle's audio — which carries the sender's exact `IMPORTED_SETTINGS_KEYS` snapshot — was never touched. **Fix**: when the bundle contains a sound's audio, `fromBundle` now wins unconditionally (`const fromBundle = Boolean(bundle && bundleKey && bundle.has(bundleKey)); const match = fromBundle ? null : matchExistingSound(...)`) — a Phase-2 bundle's whole purpose is "here's my mix exactly," so it reproduces faithfully; basename matching still applies for a Phase-1 config-only `.ncvpreset` or a sound whose file was missing at export. Accepted tradeoff: re-importing a bundle of sounds the recipient legitimately already has now makes duplicate `copied` library entries rather than risk the wrong-substitution bug (content-hash dedup is a possible later refinement). **Also fixed in the same pass**: (1) `importLocatedSound` now seeds the fresh entry's own `volume` from the portable per-sound volume (was left at the 0.7 default — harmless when the preset loads, since `loadPreset` applies its own stored volume, but wrong if the sound is later played on its own); (2) `buildPortableBundle` names the bundled file after the sound's *original* basename (`entry.originalPath`) not `getPlaybackPathForId`'s output, which for a `copied` sound is an opaque `<id>.wav` — the zip's `audio/` contents are now readable and `bundleName` matches `fileName`; (3) `presets:pickImport`'s dialog filter accepts `.zip` (and adds an "All files" entry), since WhatsApp/chat apps append `.zip` when forwarding — `readPortablePresetFile` already sniffs the `PK\x03\x04` magic so the extension never mattered past the picker. **Verified via CDP** against the real dev app: the exact collision scenario (sender's configured `sameName.wav` in a bundle + recipient's own unrelated `sameName.wav` in their library) now imports `fromBundle: true`, `matchedSoundId: null`, and the created entry has the sender's `playMode: scatter` / `gainDb: -20` / `lowpassHz: 600` / `loopStart: 0.5` / `loopEnd: 2.5` / `volume: 0.15` — none of the recipient's own sound; a no-collision fresh import still round-trips every setting and now also carries volume; Phase-1 config-only files are unaffected (bundle is null → basename match as before).

**Countdown before an automatic update installs (v0.1.108)** — inbox note: "When a new update is detected do a in app pop up notification and start a 5 seconds countdown to updating the app." Owner clarified the scope on ask: **only when `autoInstallUpdates` is on** — the default click-to-confirm flow is untouched. Before, `autoInstallUpdates` skipped the modal entirely and called `autoUpdater.downloadUpdate()` the instant `update-available` fired. Now that branch instead: shows the existing Update modal via `send('update:available', { …, autoCountdownSeconds: 5 })`, and starts a `setTimeout` **in the main process** (`AUTO_INSTALL_COUNTDOWN_SECONDS = 5`, `autoInstallCountdownId`, `clearAutoInstallCountdown()`) that calls `downloadUpdate()` when it elapses. Main-process timer, not renderer — the window is usually minimized to the tray by then, where Chromium throttles renderer timers hard (same lesson as `sleepTimer.js` and the v0.1.71 scatter-rAF bug); the renderer only runs a cosmetic 5→0 tick on the modal message. The timer is cleared on: `dismissUpdate()` (the modal's "Not now" / close — so bailing skips this update until next launch, via the existing `dismissedVersion`), `downloadUpdateNow()` (the modal's "Download & install" — go now, don't double-fire), and `setAutoUpdateEnabled(false)`. Also moved the `dismissedVersion` early-return **above** the `autoInstallUpdates` branch so "Not now" on the countdown actually suppresses the next periodic re-check (previously the auto-install path ignored `dismissedVersion` entirely). `UpdateModal.js`: `renderState` gets a `clearCountdown()` at the top + in `hide()`, and when `status === 'available' && typeof state.autoCountdownSeconds === 'number'` it overrides the message with a live countdown line and starts `countdownIntervalId`. No new IPC/preload — reuses `update:available` (extra field), `autoUpdate.download`, `autoUpdate.dismiss`.
