Settings checkbox label updated ("shows a 5-second countdown you can cancel, instead of the 'Download?' prompt"). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `window.__updateModalTest = handleEvent` hook, both removed after): a synthetic `update:available` with `autoCountdownSeconds: 5` renders the modal with the countdown message, version row, release notes, "Download & install" + "Not now"; the cosmetic tick decrements (lands at 0s — the backgrounded dev window throttled the interval into a burst, exactly why the real deadline is in main); firing `update:progress` afterward cleanly transitions to the downloading UI and a stale countdown interval never overwrites it; hiding then firing `update:not-available` shows the up-to-date state with no leftover interval. The main-process timer itself can't run under `npm run dev` (the `!app.isPackaged` gate in `initAutoUpdate` means the `autoUpdater` event handlers are never registered) — `node --check` clean, logic mirrors `sleepTimer.js`, same packaged-only verification limit every prior update-flow change has had.

**Composite tab reworked into a weld workstation (v0.1.109)** — the Board's long-standing `#next` item, from the owner: "the composite tab never gets used because it needs a few changes… a workstation of welding audio files together. In the sense that you should be able to cut, drag and drop them wherever you feel like and changing the volume at which audio file plays. If you want to fiddle around with remixing the audio you should just go to the remix tab… Composite tab is just for welding audios together." The old tab was N dropdowns + one `delayMs` number each. Now `plugins/composite/index.js` is a small horizontal timeline: a fixed left side-column (per-lane sound picker + volume slider + remove) beside a shared horizontal-scroll track area (ruler + one lane per member). Each member is a draggable `.composite-clip` positioned by `delayMs`, width ∝ trimmed length, with a per-clip `<canvas>` waveform (`api.audio.getWaveformPeaks(soundId, w, inSec, outSec)`) and left/right trim handles. Drag body = move (`delayMs`, snap 50 ms, Ctrl disables — Remix convention); drag left handle = trim head (moves `inSec` **and** `delayMs` together so the audio under the cursor stays put); drag right handle = trim tail (`outSec`). Numeric Start/In/Out fields for the selected clip mirror Remix's Start/End/Length inputs. A **Preview** button plays all members via `<audio sound://id>` → per-member `GainNode` → destination, scheduled by `setTimeout(delayMs)` (timing/overlap only — filters/EQ are bake-time, noted in the hint text). **Member data model** grew from `{soundId, delayMs}` to `{soundId, delayMs, inSec, outSec, volume}`; old composites still load (`inSec`/`outSec` null → fall back to the sound's Remix trim then full duration, in `resolveCompositeMembers`). The Composite trim is the timeline's own and **replaces** the member's Remix loop points for the bake (`resolveCompositeMembers` passes `inSec`/`outSec` as `loopStart`/`loopEnd` to `renderClipToPath`); the member's Remix filters/EQ/speedPitch still bake in. **`renderComposite` (`ffmpeg/composite.js`) changes**: (1) per-member `volume` applied via a `volume=` filter; (2) `amix` switched to `normalize=0` (honest summing, matching the live Mixer + Export v0.1.99 — the old default `normalize=1` divided every member by N, making a volume control meaningless); (3) **mono→stereo upmix fixed** — the Export rewrite's `aformat=channel_layouts=stereo` does a **−3 dB power-split** upmix in this ffmpeg build, which silently undercut the new volume control *and* didn't match how Web Audio plays a mono source (full-level duplicate). Replaced with `aformat=sample_rates=44100` (rate-only, level-safe) + an explicit `pan=stereo|c0=c0|c1=c0` for mono clips only (channel count read from the clip's WAV header, `readWavChannels`), then a final `-ac 2`. **Also** (`ipc.js`): `audio:getWaveformPeaks` no longer bails when `entry.durationSeconds == null` — it probes via `probeDurationSeconds` and persists the result through `library.updateMeta` (so a never-played sound opened only in Composite/Remix still gets a waveform, once). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `cdp.mjs` eval helper, removed after) with three generated mono test wavs: clips render with real waveforms; move-drag changes `delayMs` snapped to 50 ms; left-handle trim shifts `inSec`+`delayMs` together (audio stays put); right-handle trim changes `outSec`; bake → a normal `storageMode: copied` entry with `compositeSource.members` carrying the full new shape, `playMode: loop`, measured duration exactly `max(delay + trimmedLen)` (6.00 s for a 0+2.5 / 2.0+4.0 arrangement); **per-member volume exact** — a mono member at `volume: 1.4` measured 1.400× its source RMS, at `1.0` measured 1.000× (in a non-overlapping window), output stereo; re-opening in "Edit existing" restores every member's trim/position/volume into the timeline, and re-bake updates the **same** entry in place (`sameId: true`, library didn't grow); Preview toggles start/stop cleanly. The ffmpeg graph (including the `pan` mono-upmix and mixed mono/stereo/rate inputs) was verified against the real bundled binary before the app test, per project discipline. **Scope cuts, noted:** a member with a Remix **speed** shift renders shorter/longer than its timeline clip width shows (the bake is correct; only the visual width/overlap preview is off — uncommon, since it needs a deliberate Remix speed change); no waveform zoom, no vertical lane reordering, no snap-to-other-clip-edges. Composite's own copies of `AudioEngine.js` etc. were not needed — the plugin uses only `app.noctivago` APIs + a tiny inline waveform draw.

**Two inbox bug fixes (v0.1.110)** — both reported right after v0.1.109. (1) **Composite sound picker "isn't clickable, nothing happens"** — the per-lane `<select class="composite-side-sound">` never opened its dropdown. Root cause: `renderSidecol`'s `.composite-side` `mousedown` handler called `this.render()` synchronously, which does `this.els.sidecol.innerHTML = html` — replacing the very `<select>` the user just pressed *during* the mousedown, so the browser's default "open the dropdown" action targeted a detached node (this also silently broke dragging the per-lane volume slider, same mechanism). Fixed by (a) skipping the handler entirely when `e.target.closest('button, select, input')`, and (b) replacing the full `render()` with a new `selectMember(i)` that just toggles the `.selected` class on the side boxes / clips and calls `renderSelected()` — a cheap highlight update that never rebuilds the sidecol/lanes DOM (mirrors what `onPointerDown` already does for clip selection). Added `focus` listeners on the select and volume slider so tabbing/clicking into them still selects the lane. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` remote-debugging switch in `main/index.js`, removed after): navigated to the Composite tab, dispatched a `mousedown` on a `.composite-side-sound` and confirmed the same element node survives it (`dataset` marker intact — pre-fix it would have been swapped out), then set its value + fired `change` and confirmed a `.composite-clip` with the right label rendered, no console errors. (2) **Clock-scheduled sounds missing from exports** — reported: an hourly raven "did not play once" in an exported mix. An export is an abstract length of time with no wall clock, but `simulateScheduledEvents` (`plugins/export/simulate.js`) was rolling `secondsUntilNextTrigger` forward from the *real current time*, so an `'interval'` schedule's events depended on what time of day you ran the export and frequently produced none. Rewritten per the owner's explicit inbox instruction: an `'interval'` schedule now fires at fixed multiples of the interval measured from export t=0 (`intervalSeconds, 2·intervalSeconds, …` while `< durationSeconds`); a `'times'` (specific HH:MM) schedule returns `[]` and is excluded from exports entirely (a scheduled sound with no events is already skipped by the pipeline). Per-shot pitch/volume/fade randomization is unchanged — only the *timing* model changed. `secondsUntilNextTrigger` (and the `now` param threaded through `simulateEvents`/`export:run`) removed as now-dead. Live playback's own `ScheduledSoundSource.js` copy of `secondsUntilNextTrigger` is untouched — real playback still uses the wall clock. **Verified** with a small Node harness importing the real `simulate.js`: 60-min interval / 3h export → events at 3600 & 7200; 30-min / 90-min → 1800 & 3600; `times` schedule → `[]`; interval longer than the export → `[]`.

**Time-preserving per-shot pitch for Random Interval / Scheduled (v0.1.111)** — the Board `#next`, from the owner's v0.1.102 Question answer: "i would prefer if the sounds kept the length i set it to. If it's getting faster or slower on pitch changes it should be fixed... both on the mixer tab and on exports. Unless it would be the most resource draining thing ever." Before this, a scatter/scheduled shot's random pitch was applied by `AudioBufferSourceNode.detune` alone, which the Web Audio spec folds into `computedPlaybackRate` — so a pitched-up shot genuinely played *faster and shorter*. New `src/renderer/audio/pitchStretch.js`: a self-contained **WSOLA** (waveform-similarity overlap-add) time-stretch — overlapping input frames placed at a scaled synthesis hop, each nudged within ±256 samples to the offset that best cross-correlates with the previous frame's natural continuation (biased toward the natural hop by `0.05·|delta|/radius` so a pure tone, the pathological many-equal-matches case, doesn't drift into a pitch change). 50% Hann overlap ⇒ exact constant-overlap-add ⇒ amplitude preserved with zero normalization and no edge blow-up. Chosen over a phase vocoder: no FFT (a hand-rolled one hit real amplitude/edge bugs first), holds up on the broadband/noisy textures this app mostly plays, and cheap — coarse-step-4 search + fine refine + a 256-sample correlation window ⇒ ~30 ms for a 2 s stereo clip, ~55 ms for 4 s (off the audio thread, on the shot-scheduling `setTimeout`). `pitchShiftBuffer(context, buffer, semitones)` stretches the clip by the pitch ratio; the caller still sets `detune` to that ratio, so playback resamples the stretched clip back to the original length while shifting pitch — net: pitch moves, duration doesn't. Returns the buffer unchanged for a sub-`IDENTITY_PITCH_EPSILON_SEMITONES` (0.02) shift, and **null** past `MAX_STRETCH_SECONDS` (12 s) so the caller falls back to the old resample-coupled behavior for an unusually long shot clip. Wired into `BufferScatterSource._playOneShot` and `BufferScheduledSource._playOneShot` (both: `node.buffer = shifted ?? this.audioBuffer`, and `durationSeconds` = nominal when stretched, pitch-adjusted when it fell back — the fade-out envelope needs the real end). **Stream mode is the one place per-shot pitch still couples to length** — `StreamScatterSource`/`StreamScheduledSource` have no in-memory samples to pre-stretch and only ever run for a scatter/scheduled trim too long to ever bake (unusual for an event sound); left as a documented accepted limitation, same as Speed elsewhere. **Export** (`plugins/export/simulate.js`): dropped the `/effectiveRate` divisions on `shotDurationSeconds` in both `simulateScatterEvents` and `simulateScheduledEvents` — exports already pitch shots with `rubberband` (duration-preserving), so the shot length is just `loopEnd − loopStart` now, which fixes the v0.1.102-flagged bug where the fade-out landed early and batch spans (`planBatches`) were planned short. `exportMix.js` itself unchanged (it was already duration-correct, just fed wrong metadata). Live (WSOLA) and export (rubberband) now agree on the property that matters — length + pitch — though timbre differs slightly between the two algorithms (acceptable, and far better than differing durations). Static Remix Pitch (loop mode) was already fine: it bakes into the clip via rubberband, duration-neutral. Doppler untouched (its own contour, out of scope). **Verified**: a Node harness on `timeStretch`/`pitchShiftBuffer` (31 assertions — length scales exactly, pitch held on tones, amplitude preserved on tones + noise, transient survives and stays in place, wrapper thresholds/fallback); CDP against the real dev app (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed) — imported both source classes and drove `_playOneShot` at ±semitones: the buffer swaps to a pre-stretched one, real playback length stays nominal (2.5 s → 2.5 s at +4/−5/+7 st), an `OfflineAudioContext` render confirmed the frequency actually shifts (−7 st → 200 Hz, +12 st → 600 Hz), the >12 s buffer returns null; 3 real scatter shots via the Mixer's test-fire button with a −4..+6 st range, no console errors; `npm run build` clean.

**Small fixes from a self-review pass (v0.1.112)** — no inbox items and the Board had no autonomously-pickable `#next` after v0.1.111, so a read-only `/code-review` of the v0.1.103–111 diffs stood in for a task (same as v0.1.102). Six findings; the four safe ones shipped here, the other two flagged. Shipped:
- **`pitchStretch.js` amplitude spike near the end of a pitched shot.** The WSOLA render marked a frame as "last" (→ its trailing half written at unity gain instead of the Hann taper, to not fade a decaying tail) using a *prediction* from the worst-case search offset — but the loop often placed one more frame overlapping it, stacking unity + a rising taper ≈ +6 dB ~10 ms before the shot's nominal end. Default `fadeOutMs` is 0, so nothing masked it: an audible click/level jump on a large fraction of pitched one-shots. Fixed by splitting `timeStretch` into `planStretch` (builds the whole frame-offset list) + `renderStretch` (overlap-adds) — the genuine last frame is now known exactly (`k === frameStarts.length - 1`), so unity-trailing-half applies only where nothing follows.
- **`pitchStretch.js` stereo decorrelation.** `pitchShiftBuffer` ran `timeStretch` per channel, each doing its own similarity search → L and R picking different frame offsets → inter-channel phase differences that weren't in the source (stereo smear / comb filtering summed to mono). Now `planStretch` runs once on a mono downmix and every channel renders against that shared plan.
- **Export infinite loop on a 0/0 gap.** `simulate.js`'s `simulateScatterEvents` `while (offset < duration) { offset += randomGapSeconds() }` spins forever and grows `events` unbounded if a Random Interval sound has `minGapSeconds` and `maxGapSeconds` both 0 with "Fully random gap" off (`randomGapSeconds` returns exactly 0). Now advances by the shot's own length (floored at 0.05 s) when the gap is ~0, plus a 200k-event hard cap as a backstop (matching the pipeline's v0.1.85 bounded-resource discipline). Live playback's own copy isn't a hang (a `setTimeout(0)` between `onended`s → back-to-back shots) so it's left alone — noted in inbox Questions.
- **`ipc.js` `audio:getWaveformPeaks` persisting a bogus 0 duration.** `probeDurationSeconds` returns `0` (not `null`) if ffmpeg decodes no samples; the `!= null` guard passed that through to `library.updateMeta`, permanently suppressing the Mixer's own null-guarded re-probe and feeding 0 into loop-clip eligibility. Now guards on `> 0`, matching `library.js`'s eager-bake `<= 0` check.
- **Composite tab re-extracting every clip waveform on every window resize and on unrelated app-wide library mutations** (each `getWaveformPeaks` is a fresh ffmpeg spawn, no cache). Now: the `library.onChanged` handler and the `resize` handler both no-op unless the tab is actually visible (new `this.shown` flag via `onShow`/`onHide`; `onShow` catches up); `resize` is debounced 120 ms; and a per-`(soundId, width, in, out)` peaks cache (cleared on `refresh()`) means repeated identical renders (selection, resize back) don't re-spawn ffmpeg.

**Flagged, not fixed:** (1) `composite.js`'s `amix=normalize=0` (deliberate as of v0.1.109, so per-member volume is meaningful) can hard-clip a composite where several members overlap at high volume, and unlike Export there's no whole-mix Gain valve — inbox Question, since a limiter in the bake chain is a feel call the owner made the analogous decision on for Export. (2) the live-playback 0/0-gap back-to-back retrigger above. **Verified**: a Node harness on the rewritten `pitchStretch.js` (11 assertions — length scaling, no tail spike, pitch held on a stretched tone, stereo channels stay identical after a shift, threshold/fallback) and on `simulate.js` (0/0 gap → 1800 bounded events, normal gap unchanged at 184); `npm run build` clean. Not run against the dev app — the four fixes are mechanical and the harness covers the audio math.

**Composite bake brick-wall limiter (v0.1.113)** — the v0.1.112 flagged Composite-clipping finding, resolved by the owner ("do what you think is best"). `composite.js`'s final `amix=...:normalize=0` (honest sum, deliberate since v0.1.109 so per-member volume is meaningful) now chains `,alimiter=limit=0.95:level=disabled` before the `[out]` label. `level=disabled` is the important flag — `alimiter`'s default `level=enabled` auto-*boosts* toward the limit, which would wreck a deliberately-quiet composite; disabled makes it a pure ceiling that only acts on peaks that would otherwise clip the `pcm_s16le` encoder. Chosen over a whole-mix Gain control on the tab (more UI for a tab the owner wants kept minimal — "just for welding audios together") or leaving it (a clipped bake is a silent gotcha). **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg**: a quiet mix passes through untouched (−32 dB in, −32 dB out — no boost); a 3× over-unity honest sum that hits 0.0 dBFS (hard clip) with no limiter comes out at −0.4 dB with it; the full composite-shaped graph (mixed mono+stereo inputs, `pan` upmix, per-member `volume`+`adelay`, then `amix`+`alimiter`) renders a correct-duration stereo file. The live 0/0-gap Random Interval retrigger from the same review: owner's call is to keep it back-to-back ("full user control... perceptible enough that someone will notice and set it up differently") — no change.


---

Entries below continue the archive: the two superseded "Preset Remix plugin" architecture writeups, then the "## Status" running log spanning v0.1.115 through the v0.1.145 self-review pass. Moved out of `CLAUDE.md` to keep the auto-loaded context lean, archived 2026-09-06. Nothing was summarized or dropped. Later history (the merged Remix tab's own v0.1.148 onward, still in `CLAUDE.md`) is more recent and stays live for now.


### The Preset Remix plugin (superseded, v0.1.147 — merged into the Remix tab)

**This whole section describes a plugin that no longer exists as a separate tab** — kept below verbatim for the data-model/architecture detail (still accurate: `wholeMix`/`groups` on a preset, `WholeMixChain`/`SoundGroupChain`, the CustomEvent preview bridges), since none of that changed in the merge, only which tab edits it. See "The merged Remix tab (Sound / Preset / Group modes)" further down for what actually replaced the "Preset Remix" tab and the Mixer's own Sound-Groups-management surface.

### The Preset Remix plugin (`plugins/preset-remix/`, tab titled "Preset Remix")

**Whole-mix processing saved on a preset and heard live whenever that preset is loaded** — the long-standing `#future` "Preset Remix / whole-mix editing tab" item, built after the owner picked its shape from a two-question fork: processing set = "HP/LP/Gain now, EQ same session" (both shipped here in one release), and when-applied = "per-preset, applied on load" (not an always-on master bus). It's the *live-playback* counterpart of the Export plugin's "Whole-mix processing" section — Export bakes HP/LP/Gain+fade into a file; this applies HP/LP/Gain+fade-in **+ a parametric EQ** to everything playing.

**Data model**: a new `wholeMix` field on a preset (`presets.js`), `{ highpassHz, lowpassHz, gainDb, fadeInSeconds, eq: [...] }` or `null` when fully neutral (`normalizeWholeMix` collapses a neutral config to `null` so the "does this preset have whole-mix settings" check stays a truthiness test). `eq` is a variable-length band array in the *exact* same shape as a sound's own `filters.eq` (`{ freqHz, gainDb, q, type, slope, muted }`). New `presets.updatePresetWholeMix(id, wholeMix)` + `presets:updateWholeMix` IPC + `api.presets.updateWholeMix` preload. The portable preset export (`presetPortable.js`) does **not** carry `wholeMix` yet — deferred, noted in the inbox.

**Live chain** (`src/renderer/audio/WholeMixChain.js`): a master chain `masterGain → input → highpass → lowpass → [eq bands…] → gain → fadeGain → destination`, owned by `AudioEngine` (`engine.wholeMix`), sitting between `masterGain` (the global volume slider) and `context.destination`. The one structural change to `AudioEngine` is `masterGain.connect(this.wholeMix.input)` instead of `masterGain.connect(destination)`. The EQ sub-chain is built by the *same* `eqNodeType` / `eqBandStageCount` helpers a sound's own chain uses — now `export`ed from `SoundSource.js` (core-to-core, unlike the plugin-side duplicates) — with the identical "rebuild only when the per-band topology fingerprint changes, in-place param updates otherwise" logic copied from `SoundSource.setFilters`. `fadeGain` handles the on-load fade-in (kept separate from the user's `gain` dB node so a fade never fights a gain change). Neutral by default: nodes exist but pass audio through, `set(null)` resets them.

**How it takes effect**: `tabs/mixer/index.js`'s `loadPreset` sets `state.activePresetId = preset.id` and calls `engine.wholeMix.set(preset.wholeMix ?? null, { fadeInSeconds })` — the fade-in only ever runs on an actual load. Loading another preset swaps the chain (or resets it to neutral). `state.activePresetId` is *not* cleared when the user then deviates from the preset by hand — it just tracks "what did we last load."

**Live preview** across the plugin/core boundary: the plugin only has the curated `window.noctivago` IPC surface, no line to the engine — so it dispatches a `noctivago:whole-mix-preview` window `CustomEvent` `{ presetId, wholeMix }` on every control/EQ change (and once more with the *saved* values on `onHide`, reverting any unsaved preview). The Mixer listens and applies it **only if `e.detail.presetId === state.activePresetId`** — editing preset A while preset B is the live mix changes nothing audible. Same window-CustomEvent bridge the Solo button (`noctivago:solo`) and sleep timer (`noctivago:stop-all-playback`) already use.

**The plugin UI** (`plugins/preset-remix/index.js` + `ui/EqEditor.js`): a preset `<select>`, HP/LP/Gain sliders + a fade-in field (mirroring the Export tab's controls), then a scoped-down draggable-node EQ canvas — `WholeMixEqEditor`, a fresh ~280-line editor, *not* a copy of the core Remix `EqEditor` (which is 800+ lines of spectrum analyzer / A-B / mute-solo / cut-overlay that don't apply to a whole mix with no single preview source). Kept: draggable nodes, the real composite response curve (`OfflineAudioContext` `BiquadFilterNode.getFrequencyResponse` summed in dB, same technique as core), click-empty-to-add, wheel-for-Q, right-click-to-remove, double-click-to-flatten, per-band Freq/Gain/Q precision fields + a type `<select>`. No slope UI (bands are always `gentle`). Save via `api.presets.updateWholeMix`; a `canonicalMix()` order-insensitive compare drives the dirty check (the editor and the store build band objects with different key orders). `plugins/preset-remix/` uses only `app.noctivago` + its own tiny EQ math — no copies of `AudioEngine.js` etc. needed.

**Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` remote-debugging switch in `main/index.js` + a temp `window.__mixerDebug` engine hook, both removed after; note: plugin JS is served by the `plugin://` protocol which HTTP-caches, so a `Page.reload({ignoreCache:true})` is needed after editing a plugin file): saved HP 500 / Gain −6 / fade 3 on a preset → `presets.list()` shows the normalized `wholeMix`; loading that preset in the Mixer built `engine.wholeMix` with `highpass.frequency` 500, `gain` −6 dB, `fadeGain` mid-ramp at 0.2; a live edit while that preset was active moved the engine's `lowpass` to ~4000; leaving the tab unsaved reverted it to 20000; editing a *different* preset left the active chain untouched; loading a no-`wholeMix` preset reset the chain to neutral (`config: null`). EQ: click-to-add populated the precision fields (112 Hz / +7.2 dB / peaking), wheel changed Q 1→1.08, the type `<select>` set `highshelf`, Save persisted both bands, right-click removed one and Save persisted the remainder; loading the preset built the matching `eqNodeGroups` (topology `"1"`, highshelf node @ 2518 Hz / −7.2 dB), a live-preview add rebuilt to topology `"1,1"`, leaving unsaved reverted to `"1"`. Screenshot confirmed the layout reads on-brand.

**Un-feel-tested, flagged to the owner**: whether whole-mix processing at the master-bus point *sounds* right vs. the same processing baked per-file in Export; the EQ's ±18 dB gain range and 12-band cap. Also deferred: Export tab prefilling its whole-mix section from a selected preset's saved `wholeMix`; carrying `wholeMix` in a portable `.ncvpreset`.

**Bigger, centre-neutral volume sliders (v0.1.115)** — inbox note: "too little control on the volume of stuff… make the bar bigger… volume should start at the middle on a neutral state and you should be able to drag either left or right to make it louder or quieter." New `src/renderer/core/volumeScale.js` maps a **bipolar** slider position (0..1, the `<input>` carries 0..100) to a linear GainNode value, piecewise-linear in gain on each side of centre: `0 → 0` (silence), `0.5 → 1.0` (unity), `1.0 → 2.0` (+6 dB). `gainToSlider`/`sliderToGain` are the 0..100 wrappers; `positionToGain`/`gainToPosition` are the 0..1 core (inverse-exact for the values that matter — 0.7 gain ⇄ slider 35). Applied at **both** volume sliders: the global one (`applyGlobalVolume` wraps the value through `positionToGain`; `#global-volume` default `value` 50 = unity, unchanged from the old `100 → 1.0`) and every per-sound one (`SoundRow.js`: `value`/`defaultValue` via `gainToSlider`, `onVolumeChange` via `sliderToGain`). The Remix plugin's *preview* volume slider is deliberately **not** touched — it's an audition-loudness tool, session-only, not a mix level, and plugins can't import `core/`.

**Default volume 0.7 → 1.0** (`DEFAULT_SOUND_VOLUME` in `src/shared/constants.js`, imported by both `library.js` `addSound` and the renderer's `volumeScale.js`/mixer `?? DEFAULT_VOLUME` fallbacks — the old scattered `?? 0.7` literals are gone): a fresh sound now sits dead centre at its own recorded level, matching "neutral = middle." **Persisted per-sound volumes are unchanged** — they're stored as linear gain, so a sound previously at 0.7 still plays at 0.7 (its thumb just shows left of centre now, which is accurate). Only sounds that never had a `volume` (pre-persistence entries) and brand-new imports get the +3 dB bump to unity — flagged in the CHANGELOG.

**Widths**: `.global-volume` 100→170px, `.sound-row-volume` 100→140px (140 not 150 — a per-sound row is space-constrained at the default 464px window; the "Remove" button was already near the edge).

**BUG FIX: big exports failing with `spawn ENAMETOOLONG` (v0.1.116)** — reported directly ("Export failed: spawn ENAMETOOLONG"). Windows caps a process command line at ~32,767 chars. `exportMix.js`'s `renderFinalMixdown` passes ffmpeg one `-i <temp path>` per piece it merges — the filter *graph* goes to a `-filter_complex_script` file (off the command line), but the input list doesn't, and each `-i <userData>/export-tmp/<uuid>.flac` is ~95 chars. A multi-hour export, or one with a Random Interval / Scheduled sound firing often, produces one sliced-batch track per `EVENT_BATCH_SIZE` (20) events; ~350 of them (≈7000 events — a few scatter sounds over 3h at default gaps, or the v0.1.112 `MAX_EVENTS` cap of 200k in the pathological case) overflows the limit and `child_process.spawn` throws `ENAMETOOLONG` before ffmpeg ever runs. The export gets all the way through rendering every piece, then dies assembling them. **Fix**: `exportMix.js` gained `combineTracks` (adelay each track to its absolute offset → `amix` normalize=0 → one track, graph via `-filter_complex_script`) and `reduceTrackCount` (combine in fixed `MAX_INPUTS_PER_PASS = 32` chunks, repeatedly, until few enough remain). Before the final mixdown, if `batchTracks.length` exceeds the budget (`32 − loopShots.length`, floored at 4 — loop shots each take an input too and can't be pre-combined since they're `-stream_loop` tiled), the batch tracks are reduced first. A combine pass is just `adelay`+`amix` (no rubberband), so a wide one stays cheap and can't hit the v0.1.85 memory blowup; 32 inputs ≈ 3 KB of command line, well clear. Cheap no-op when the export is small enough that everything already fits. Combined intermediates are pushed onto the same cleanup array as the batch tracks. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness importing the actual `exportMix.js` (electron stubbed through a `module.register` resolve hook, so `loopClip.js`/`runFfmpeg.js`/`ffmpegPath.js` run unmodified): a 40-min export with a scatter sound simulating 2000 plays → 100 batch tracks → one "Merging 100 track groups…" round → `ok`, output exactly 2400.00s, zero leftover temp files; the same at 800 pitch-varied events (40 batches, rubberband + reduction together) → `ok`, 2400.00s, mean −25 dB / max −15.5 dB (honest sum, no clipping), zero leftovers; a small 120-event export (6 batches, under budget) skips the reduction entirely and is byte-path-identical to before. New `.volume-slider-wrap` span (in `index.html` around the global input, built in `SoundRow.js` around each per-sound one) draws a faint 2px centre tick via `::after` (`pointer-events: none`, so it never blocks a drag) — makes "neutral" eyeball-able on the wider bar. Double-click-to-reset (`sliderReset.js`) is unaffected — it still reads the input's native `defaultValue` (now `gainToSlider(1)` = "50"). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `window.__mixerDebug` engine hook, both removed): global slider at 100/50/0 → `masterGain.gain` 2.0/1.0/0.0; per-sound drag to 75 → `state.volumes` 1.5, the live source's `gainNode.gain` 1.5, and the store persisted 1.5; a pre-existing sound stored at 0.7 renders its thumb at 35 (left of centre); both sliders wrapped with the tick present; screenshot confirmed the wider bars.

**Exporting now blocks an auto-update install until it finishes (v0.1.117)** — inbox, flagged "takes precedence over anything": "If a preset is being exported when the app detects a new update... it can't close the app to update till it finishes the exporting. The autoupdate cycle should not start nor proceed once you start exporting a preset." New `src/main/exportState.js` — a tiny module-level ref-counted `beginExport()`/`endExport()`/`isExportInProgress()`/`whenExportIdle(fn)` (not a class/store; `ipc.js`'s `export:run` handler brackets its `exportMix()` call with begin/end, `autoUpdate.js` reads/waits on it). Three guard points in `autoUpdate.js`, each mirroring the actual risk at that stage: (1) the periodic background `runCheck()` skips entirely while exporting (a manual "Check for updates" click still goes through — deliberate user action); (2) the auto-install countdown's `autoUpdater.downloadUpdate()` call defers via `whenExportIdle`; (3) `update-downloaded`'s `quitAndInstall()` — the one truly destructive step, the actual close-and-restart — defers via `whenExportIdle`, checked both before the 1.5s "restarting" paint delay and again right after it, so an export starting during that window is still caught. Verified with a small Node harness exercising `exportState.js` directly (immediate-fire-when-idle, deferred-until-idle, nested/concurrent begin/end, no-op on an unmatched `endExport()`).

**Draggable fade in/out handles on Random Interval / Scheduled sounds, in Remix (v0.1.118)** — inbox, flagged "urgent": "impossible to predict how it will work out just typing numbers." The trimmed region in Remix's waveform *is* the one-shot clip a scatter/scheduled sound replays, so draggable fade handles drawn directly on it map 1:1 to what actually plays — same idea as a DAW clip's own corner fade handles. `LoopEditor.js` gained `fadesEnabled`/`fadeInSec`/`fadeOutSec` state + `setFadesEnabled`/`setFades`/`getFades`/`onFadesChange`; a new `FADE_HANDLE_ZONE_PX` (20px) top band disambiguates a fade-handle grab from the full-height start/end trim line when they'd otherwise coincide on x alone (e.g. fade = 0 sits exactly at loopStart) — checked first in `pointerDown`, only within that band, falling through to the existing start/end/playhead/doppler candidates otherwise (no y-based hit-testing existed anywhere in this file before). `waveform.js` draws a shaded attenuation wedge + a diagonal ramp line + white-ringed corner pins (`#9ece6a`, the app's Tokyo Night "positive" accent), gated the same way the Doppler visual already is. `index.js`'s `updateFadeVisual()` is the single sync point: reads the current playMode's `fadeInMs`/`fadeOutMs` (scatter or scheduled — mutually exclusive, a loop-mode sound has no per-shot fade to show) and pushes seconds into the loop editor; the reverse direction (drag → field) goes through `onFadesChange`, mirroring `onLoopChange`'s existing pattern. A trim drag that shrinks the region below the fades' own combined length doesn't silently discard what was dragged in — `clampedFades()` clamps only for drawing/hit-testing on every redraw, the stored raw seconds are untouched unless a real fade-handle drag commits a new value. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: dragged the fade-in handle on a real 1s scatter sound to ~0.3s → landed at the exact sub-pixel value; dragged fade-out to 0.5s then over-dragged past the remaining budget → clamped to exactly `1 - fadeInSec`; a click at the same x but below the 20px band correctly grabbed the trim-start handle instead (y-band disambiguation); switching to Loop playMode correctly hid the section and disabled the handles.

**A completion chime on Export finish (v0.1.119)** — inbox: "When export finishes it should beep or make a sound or something." An export can run for minutes to hours, so a chime means the owner doesn't have to keep the tab in view. `plugins/export/index.js`'s `playCompletionChime(success)` builds a throwaway `AudioContext` + a couple of `OscillatorNode`s — a rising 3-note chime (C5-E5-G5) on success, a falling 2-note tone (G4-D4) on failure, no bundled sound asset needed. Duplicated `routeDevAudioOutput` into a new `plugins/export/audio/devAudioOutput.js` (same cross-directory-import reason every other plugin duplication in this codebase has) so the chime's `AudioContext` gets the same dev-mode routing-away-from-real-speakers convenience every other one in this app already has. **Verified via CDP without making any actual noise**, per the standing "mute/inspect state, don't just listen" testing preference: swapped in a fake `AudioContext` (records oscillator frequencies/gain ramps instead of producing sound) — confirmed the success call creates exactly 3 oscillators at 523.25/659.25/783.99 Hz and the failure call 2 at 392/293.66 Hz, with real gain envelopes scheduled on each.

**Portable presets now carry Preset Remix's whole-mix settings (v0.1.120)** — answered the inbox follow-up to v0.1.114 ("add the preset mix on the exported noctivago file"). `presetPortable.js`'s `buildPortableBundle` adds `wholeMix: preset.wholeMix ?? null` to the manifest (mirrors the exact same field-access pattern already used for `name`/`appVersion` right next to it); `analyzePortablePreset` reads it back the same way (`null` on an older file that predates the field); `finalizeImport` now accepts a `wholeMix` param and passes it straight to `savePreset` (which already normalizes it — pure plumbing, no new logic). One line threaded through the renderer's "Create preset" handler (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) to send `importAnalysis.wholeMix` in the finalize payload. **Verified via CDP**: called `window.noctivago.presets.finalizeImport` directly (no native dialog needed, unlike export/pickImport) with a real soundId + a `wholeMix` payload — the created preset's stored `wholeMix` came back fully normalized. The export/analyze half sits behind native file dialogs CDP can't drive, so it rests on code review confidence (a one-line addition, structurally identical to an adjacent field already proven correct).

**"Add from link" — download a direct audio URL into the library (v0.1.121)** — the owner's own backlog steer answer ("ffmpeg also downloads stuff from links... get a hold of some infrastructure to download an audio and using it on the app"), distinct from the parked YouTube/yt-dlp idea (no ToS risk — a plain user-supplied HTTP(S) URL, not scraping a video platform). Verified the bundled ffmpeg actually supports it before writing any app code: `ffmpeg -protocols` confirmed http/https compiled in, a real download+transcode against a live public test URL worked, `-rw_timeout 30000000` (30s, bounds a stalled connection) was confirmed accepted by the real build, and a 404 case confirmed a clean non-zero exit with a real stderr error. `library.js`'s new `addSoundFromUrl({ name, url })` mirrors `addRecordedSound` (the mic-recording import, v0.1.90) almost exactly: validates the URL is `http:`/`https:` only (rejected *before* it ever reaches ffmpeg — a deliberate security boundary), downloads+transcodes straight to a scratch `.wav` via `runFfmpegToFile(['-y', '-rw_timeout', ..., '-i', url, '-vn', wavPath])`, then goes through the same `addSound({ keepCopy: true })` path any other import uses. A blank Name field falls back to the URL's own basename (extension stripped). New `src/renderer/core/AddLinkDialog.js` (install-once/open-on-demand, same shape as `RecordDialog.js`) + a new "Add from link…" entry in the toolbar's "+" menu. **Verified via CDP**: drove the actual "+" menu → dialog → confirm flow with a real small public URL — the entry showed up with `storageMode: 'copied'`, a real transcoded `.wav` on disk, and every default field a normal import gets; confirmed all three error paths (malformed URL, `file://` URL, a real live 404) reject cleanly with a real error message.

**Real convolution reverb, replacing the old delay-based approximation (v0.1.122)** — owner's own inbox note: "think of a way to better the echo/reverb experience... if you need to write a better way to work with echo and delay you should absolutely do it." "Reverb" used to just be Echo's delay/decay knobs dialed to a short-delay/high-decay preset — never a real algorithm, since this ffmpeg build has no dedicated reverb filter. **Researched first**: exponentially-decaying noise as a synthetic impulse response is a legitimate, well-documented technique (Moorer 1979; MDN's own `ConvolverNode` example and the small `reverbGen` library both do exactly this instead of a bundled recorded IR — zero licensing/install-size cost). Confirmed the bundled ffmpeg has `afir` (arbitrary FIR/convolution filter, up to 60s) via `-filters`. **A real gotcha caught by testing directly against the bundled binary before writing app code**: `afir`'s own `dry`/`wet` options are NOT a dry/wet mix despite the name — both are just two gain stages multiplied into the same always-fully-convolved output (`dry=1:wet=0` produced total silence, not a passthrough; level scaled with the dry×wet product, confirmed by measuring `dry=10:wet=1` landing at the identical level as `dry=1:wet=10`). A real mix had to be built by hand: `asplit` the input into a dry branch and a to-be-convolved branch, run only the wet branch through `afir` at unity (`dry=1:wet=1`), scale each branch's own `volume`, then `amix`+`alimiter` (brick-wall ceiling, same `alimiter=limit=0.95:level=disabled` Composite's bake already uses — a continuous ambient sound's reverb tail genuinely can build real level, confirmed clipping-adjacent before the limiter). **Three touch points, mirroring exactly how Echo already works in each**: (1) *Bake* (`loopClip.js`) — reverb can't join `buildFilterChain`'s plain `-af` fragment (afir needs a second ffmpeg input, the IR file), so it's a new isolated pass (`applyReverbPass`, using a new `src/main/ffmpeg/reverbIR.js` that generates+caches the IR via `anoisesrc=color=pink`+`afade curve=exp`, one file per rounded size under userData) — `doRender`'s branch condition generalized from `!speedPitchChain` to `!speedPitchChain && !reverbActive`, reusing the exact "pre-process then crossfade last" pre-pass mechanism the v0.1.32 speed/pitch/reverse fix already established (crossfade-rotation itself was never touched). (2) *Live preview* — a `ConvolverNode` fed a synthetic IR (noise × exponential decay, `src/renderer/audio/reverbIR.js` + a Remix-plugin duplicate), wired as a third parallel send off `filterGainNode` (same pattern as `echoSendGain` — Echo and Reverb are independent, stackable effects, not chained) in both `SoundSource.js` and `PreviewSource.js`, regenerated only when `reverbSizeMs` changes. (3) *Data/UI* — `reverbSizeMs`/`reverbMix` added to `library.js`'s defaults, `NEUTRAL_FILTERS`, the Reverb preset (now `reverbSizeMs:1500, reverbMix:0.35`, echo fields zeroed), two new Remix sliders, and — caught proactively, the exact kind of miss v0.1.91's Doppler bug warned about — `plugins/editor/index.js`'s `snapshotOf()` (a flattened field list, not a generic spread) needed the two new fields added by hand or a reverb-only change would silently not mark the sound dirty. **Verified twice**: (a) a Node harness (electron stubbed via a `module.register` resolve hook, real `loopClip.js`/`reverbIR.js` unmodified) — reverb-off byte-path-identical; reverb-on (1500ms/0.4 mix) on a 3s tone still trimmed to exactly 3s despite the IR's own length, IR cache file created once and reused, the full combo (echo+reverb+speedPitch) completed without error, mix=0 with a nonzero size took the fast no-op path, peak level -0.4dB (no clipping), zero leftover temp files; (b) CDP against the real dev app — dragging the sliders live-updated both preview voices' `ConvolverNode.buffer` and `reverbSendGain` (settling at the target after the smoothing ramp — reading too early shows the pre-ramp value, a real gotcha hit during this same check), turning reverb off cleared the buffer to `null`, the Reverb preset button set the new fields and zeroed the old echo ones, Save persisted and baked a real clip whose actual file on disk measured exactly 1.00s with real non-silent, non-clipping content. **Un-feel-tested, flagged to the owner**: the exponential decay shape/rate, the Reverb preset's own 1.5s/35% starting point, and whether procedural noise reverb sounds convincing enough on real ambient content.

**BUG FIX: Remix trim-handle drag also dragged the fade in/out balls (v0.1.123)** — reported directly ("this shouldn't work like this... make the movement of the handle balls be independent of the cutting handles"). The fade-in/out handles (v0.1.118) are drawn at a fixed offset from loopStart/loopEnd (`loopStart + fadeInSec`, `loopEnd - fadeOutSec`), so dragging the trim handle moved loopStart/loopEnd and, by that same formula, visibly dragged the fade ball right along with it — by design of the original offset-based definition, not a coordinate bug. `LoopEditor.js`'s `pointerDown` now captures each fade ball's absolute waveform-time position (`dragFadeInAnchor`/`dragFadeOutAnchor`) the moment a start/end trim drag begins; `updateDragValue`'s `start`/`end` branches then recompute `fadeInSec`/`fadeOutSec` every frame so the ball's absolute position stays fixed while the trim handle moves — it only slides once the shrinking region would otherwise push it past the new trim edge (clamped there, same as before). Gated on `fadesEnabled` so a Loop-mode drag (no fade handles shown) never touches the fade state. **Verified with a Node harness** driving the real `LoopEditor.js` module directly (only `waveform.js`'s drawing calls stubbed via a `module.register` resolve hook, same pattern used elsewhere in this codebase) — dispatched real `mousedown`/`mousemove`/`mouseup` sequences at the module's actual `canvas`/`window` event listeners: dragging the start handle short of the fade-in ball's position left the ball's absolute time unchanged; dragging past it clamped `fadeInSec` to 0 rather than going negative; the same held for the end handle and `fadeOutSec`.

**Mixer: sound name gets its own full-width row (v0.1.124)** — inbox follow-up on the v0.1.106 Remix name-cutoff report, re-explained after an initial round of miscommunication: "the name competes for space with the buttons that goes inside the box of each audio file... the name should populate a top section of the box so it can always or most of the time display it's complete name. And, if it's still cut-off, it should just billboard scroll the name." `SoundRow.js` restructured each `<li>` from one flex row (name/meta/tags flexing for whatever space the fixed-width buttons/slider left over) into two stacked sections: a new `.sound-row-name-row` (name + composite badge, full width) on top, and `.sound-row-controls` (meta/tags + every button/slider, unchanged internally) below. `.sound-row`'s own CSS flipped from `flex-direction: row` to `column`; the old row-level `display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px` moved onto the new `.sound-row-controls` class essentially unchanged. The existing hover-marquee (`wireMarquee`, unchanged) still covers a name too long even for the full row. **A real, pre-existing issue surfaced while verifying, fixed in the same pass**: at the app's narrow 480px default window width, `.sound-row-controls`' fixed-width buttons (Include/slider/mute/solo/test-fire/remove) already summed past the available width on their own — the Remove button was being clipped off the visible window edge, not just visually cramped. This was already true before this change (the old layout's fixed-width buttons never shrank either — only the flex:1 name/meta column absorbed the deficit by hitting zero width, which just hid the same overflow behind squeezed text), but became obviously visible now that the name isn't there to silently eat the shortfall. Fixed by giving `.sound-row-controls` `flex-wrap: wrap` — overflowing controls now wrap to a second line instead of being clipped. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed after): screenshotted the Mixer tab — the name now renders on its own full-width line above the controls; double-click-to-rename still opens its input inline in the name row and cancels cleanly on blur; the control row wraps (Remove drops to its own line) instead of clipping at the window edge.

**Export tab: removed whole-mix processing, clearer finish state (v0.1.125)** — two separate items. **(1)** An already-answered but never-implemented inbox question (the v0.1.114a follow-up): "Get rid of the whole mix processing section on the export tab. Keep only the Fade in and fade out options because this is still relevant. But the whole mix thing should be kept on the preset remix tab." `plugins/export/index.js` lost its Highpass/Lowpass/Gain sliders, their `this.els` entries, their `input` listeners, and `updateWholeMixLabels()`; `currentWholeMixFilters()` now just returns a hardcoded neutral `{ highpassHz: 0, lowpassHz: 20000, gainDb: 0 }` so `exportMix.js`'s existing post-processing step (unchanged) is always a no-op. Dead CSS (`.export-hint-inline`, `.export-tab h3` - nothing else used either) removed too. **A real trade-off, not silently absorbed**: `exportMix.js` never read a preset's own Preset Remix `wholeMix` field to begin with (confirmed by grep before writing anything - the two systems were always separate, per the original v0.1.114 design), so this change doesn't redirect Export to inherit Preset Remix's settings, it just removes Export's own copy entirely - an exported file no longer carries *any* whole-mix coloring. Flagged to the owner in the inbox as a deliberate choice worth a follow-up call, not fixed unilaterally, since "keep it on Preset Remix" is genuinely ambiguous between "remove Export's own copy" (what got built) and "make Export inherit Preset Remix's settings automatically" (real extra work: wiring the preset's saved EQ bands through a new ffmpeg pass, since `exportMix.js` has no EQ chain today). **(2)** Direct feedback on a slow 10-hour export: "let the user know how much time it took to fully render the exported audio file. And clear the progress bar when it finishes. Make the UI feel more explicit about it having finished exporting." `runExport()` now times the render wall-clock and reports it in the final status line on both success and failure; the status line gets a distinct color (`.export-status-success` reusing the same `#9ece6a` "positive" green as the Remix fade visual, `.export-status-error` the existing `--danger` red) instead of staying plain dim text; the progress bar (`#export-progress-wrap`) is hidden on completion instead of sitting frozen at 100% indefinitely. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: the destination path sits behind a native Save dialog CDP can't drive, so a temp `window.__exportPluginDebug = this` hook (removed after) let a real end-to-end export run through the actual `runExport()` method - confirmed the finished state shows the green checkmark line with a real elapsed time and the progress bar hidden. **A real side effect caught and fixed during this same verification, not a shipped bug**: an earlier un-stubbed test click actually triggered the real native Save dialog, which (apparently auto-resolved by this sandboxed/automated Electron environment) wrote a genuine small WAV file to the owner's actual OneDrive Desktop folder - caught by noticing the reported output path wasn't the stubbed one, and deleted immediately.

**Remix: drag-to-seek on the sticky progress bar (v0.1.126)** — answered inbox follow-up on the v0.1.115 volume-slider question: "make it so you can control the needle via the progress bar on the sticky topbar... put a little circle on it so you can drag." The progress bar's HTML gained a `.editor-sticky-progress-track` wrapper around the existing fill (keeping its own `overflow:hidden` for the rounded-corner clip) plus a new sibling `#editor-sticky-progress-thumb` circle *outside* that clip so it isn't cut off at the track edges; `updateStickyProgress()` now positions the thumb's `left: %` alongside the fill's existing `width: %`. `mountSticky()` calls a new `wireStickyProgressDrag()`: a `mousedown` on the bar plus `window`-level `mousemove`/`mouseup` (same pattern `LoopEditor.js`'s own drag handling already uses) compute the clicked/dragged fraction from `getBoundingClientRect()`, convert it to an absolute time within the current loop region, and call the existing `previewSource.scrubTo(t)` (already used by the waveform's own playhead scrub, so this reuses proven logic rather than adding a new seek path) plus an immediate `updateStickyProgress(t)` so the bar responds instantly even while paused (when the rAF-driven `tickPreviewPlayhead` loop isn't running to pick it up on its own). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: loaded a real 6s sound, a synthetic click at 80% along the bar landed at exactly 0:05 with the thumb and the waveform's own playhead line both jumping to match; dragging while paused updated the displayed time without resuming playback (confirmed the Play button's title stayed "Play", not "Pause").

**Remix: "Live edit" / "Saved audio" preview toggle (v0.1.127)** — direct request: "Everything modifiable on the remix tab should be audible on the remix tab preview... If this isn't possible then when you hit save and an audio is baked you should be able to alternate between the baked audio and the preview." `PreviewSource.js`'s own doc comments already document exactly which effects it can't accurately preview live: Doppler and Reverse have no live preview at all (bake-only), Pitch is only a rough approximation. Rather than building real-time equivalents for each (a real-time pitch-bend engine for a bake-only effect like Doppler is real new DSP scope), built the fallback the owner proposed directly. New `plugins/editor/audio/BakedClipPreview.js` plays the actual baked clip file (`sound://<id>?variant=clip`) through one plain looping `<audio>` element - no filter chain needed since the bake already applied everything - deliberately minimal next to `PreviewSource.js`'s two-voice crossfading chain. A new sticky-bar toggle button (`#editor-preview-mode-toggle`, "Live edit"/"Saved audio") flips `this.previewMode` between `'live'`/`'saved'`; a new `activePreview()` getter (`previewMode === 'saved' ? bakedPreview : previewSource`) is what every sticky-bar control now reads through - `tickPreviewPlayhead`, `updateStickyProgress` (mode-aware: the baked clip's own 0-based timeline vs. previewSource's loopStart-offset original-file timeline), the progress-bar drag handler, the waveform's own `onScrub` callback, and the Play/Pause/volume/mute click handlers - rather than hardcoding `previewSource` at each call site. Eligibility (`savedPreviewEligible()`) reuses `hasUnsavedChanges()` outright - the exact same staleness check Save's own button state already computes - so a bake is only offered for audition while it still matches every current setting; `updatePreviewModeToggle()` is called from the same `updateSaveButtonState()` hook every field-change handler in this file already funnels through, so any edit immediately re-evaluates eligibility and auto-reverts out of 'saved' (pausing whichever engine was actually playing) the instant a bake goes stale. **Two real bugs caught during implementation, not by code review**: (1) the auto-revert originally set `this.previewMode = 'live'` *before* calling `this.activePreview()?.pause()` - since `activePreview()` reads `previewMode`, this paused the wrong (already-idle) engine and would have left the real one silently playing in the background; fixed by pausing first, flipping second. (2) a Save re-renders the clip file at the exact same `sound://<id>?variant=clip` URL a previous `BakedClipPreview` may have already fetched - `sound://`'s response carries `Accept-Ranges` but no explicit no-cache header, a real risk of a browser-cached stale response - fixed by recreating `this.bakedPreview` after every successful save (safe: `savedPreviewEligible()` requires non-dirty, and Save is only reachable while dirty, so `previewMode` is always already `'live'` by the time a save completes) plus a per-construction `v=${Date.now()}` cache-busting query param. Also fixed in passing: `onHide`'s tab-switch-away handler was pausing `this.previewSource` directly rather than `this.activePreview()`, which would have left a 'saved'-mode audition playing in the background after leaving the tab. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp debug hook, removed after): toggle correctly disabled with no bake, enabled once a real bake existed; switching to Saved and playing showed only `bakedPreview.playing` true (`previewSource.playing` stayed false); drag-to-seek on the sticky bar landed at the correct time within the clip; editing a filter slider mid-audition immediately stopped playback, reverted to Live, and disabled the toggle; a real Save produced a new cache-busting URL and the freshly re-baked clip played back correctly afterward.

**Export Part B — parallel mixdown, opt-in and honestly experimental (v0.1.128)** — the owner's own deferred ask, picked up once a session-usage window that had held it off (see the Board's own note) cleared: "you do whatever you think will work... if you want to implement this just as a test to see if it will be faster and sound ok you can." The shape first floated to the owner was literal time-chunking (split the export timeline into pieces, mix each in parallel, concat) - but a loop shot's `-stream_loop -1` input can't be seeked into an arbitrary chunk without ffmpeg re-decoding everything before it, so that wouldn't actually parallelize the cost and would add a real seam-artifact risk at every chunk boundary. Built instead: parallelize **by sound**, not by time. New `tileLoopShotFull` (`exportMix.js`) renders each loop-mode sound's own full-duration tile as its own ffmpeg process - several at once via the existing `mapPool`, its own `mixdownConcurrency` (same core-count/cap logic as `fasterExport`'s, but a genuinely separate toggle/setting as the owner asked for) - instead of every loop sound sharing one single-threaded final-mixdown pass. `renderFinalMixFromTracks` is the new, now-cheap tail: adelay+amix the pre-tiled tracks (loop tiles at offset 0 + the existing sliced batch tracks at their own offsets, reduced under `MAX_INPUTS_PER_PASS` via the existing `reduceTrackCount`), apply whole-mix processing + fades last, encode - unchanged in shape from the old `renderFinalMixdown`, just without the `-stream_loop` tiling inline. Zero seam risk anywhere (every sound's own track is still one continuous render, no time-domain split at all) - safer than the shape first discussed, at the cost of one full-duration temp intermediate file per loop sound instead of zero. New `parallelMixdown` setting (`settings.js`/`ipc.js`/preload, mirroring `fasterExport`'s own plumbing exactly) + a second, independent checkbox on the Export tab. **Honest finding, not a confirmed win**: synthetic Node-harness benchmarks (electron stubbed via `module.register`, real bundled ffmpeg) at both a modest scale (8 loop sounds/45s) and a many-loop-iterations scale (6 sounds × 1500 `stream_loop` iterations/300s) showed **no clear speedup** (0.85x-0.98x - if anything, slightly slower from extra process-spawn overhead) - casting real doubt on the 2026-09-02 research note's circumstantial reasoning that the final mixdown's loop-tiling is the actual bottleneck on a real multi-hour export with real content. Rather than ship an unverified performance claim, the Export tab's own checkbox copy says exactly this ("test-scale benchmarks didn't show a clear speedup, try it and see"), and a new per-phase wall-clock timing addition to the export log (`closeLastLogLineTiming()`, stamps `(h:mm:ss)` onto each already-logged step line once the next step starts or the export finishes) means the owner's own next real slow export will show definitively where the time actually goes, on either toggle setting - a cheap, generically useful diagnostic regardless of whether `parallelMixdown` itself ever proves worthwhile. **Verified**: a Node harness (3 sine-tone loop sounds + 1 scatter sound, real ffmpeg) confirmed matching output duration and near-identical levels (0.1 dB / 0.0 dB delta) between `parallelMixdown` on/off, zero leftover temp files, correct step-label branching, and a zero-loop-sounds edge case falling back cleanly. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed after): both checkboxes render with correct independent labels, the new one round-trips through `settings:setParallelMixdownEnabled` correctly: a real 8-second export with `parallelMixdown` on completed successfully end-to-end (correct "Parallel mixdown on — tiling 1 loop sound at once…" step, per-step `(0:00:00)` timing on every log line). **A real mistake made and caught during this same verification pass**: attempted to stub `window.noctivago.export.pickDestination` from the CDP script to avoid the real native Save dialog - this silently no-ops (`contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld` deep-freezes the exposed object, so the reassignment has no effect and isn't a strict-mode throw either), so the real dialog fired for real and - as apparently happens in this sandboxed/automated Electron environment - auto-resolved to a real default path, writing a genuine test WAV to the owner's actual OneDrive Desktop (same failure mode as a documented v0.1.125 incident) and popping the real dialog on the owner's live screen, who clicked Save on it themselves not realizing it was a test. Caught immediately when the owner asked about it mid-session; the stray file was deleted right away, the dev app was closed, and a new memory (`feedback_contextbridge_frozen_override`) now flags this pitfall for future CDP passes - the correct approach is a temporary dev-only IPC test hook (the `__test:presetExport`-style pattern from v0.1.107), never a `window.noctivago.*` reassignment.

**Export now inherits a preset's Preset Remix whole-mix settings, applied last (v0.1.129)** — the owner's direct answer (2026-09-02) to the v0.1.125 trade-off flagged in the inbox: "Yeah it should [inherit them]. Should be the last thing applied tho. Since it's a whole preset EQ this should be done last every step of it's existence." `plugins/export/index.js`'s `currentWholeMixFilters(preset)` (now taking the selected preset, already in scope in `runExport()`) reads `preset.wholeMix` straight off the object `presets.list()` already returns (`presets.js`'s `wholeMix` field was always there - `null` on a preset with nothing saved, which correctly collapses to the same all-neutral shape the function always returned) instead of a hardcoded neutral constant. `exportMix.js` gained a real EQ chain in its whole-mix post-processing (previously only highpass/lowpass/gain) - `loopClip.js`'s `buildEqBandFilter`/`eqBandStageCount` are now `export`ed (same `src/main/ffmpeg/` directory as `exportMix.js`, so a plain import, not a third duplicated implementation the way a plugin would need) and a new shared `buildWholeMixPostChain()` helper (used by both `renderFinalMixdown` and Part B's `renderFinalMixFromTracks` - deduplicating what had been two copies of the same four-line post-array block) orders the fragment `highpass → lowpass → eq bands → gain → fade`, matching `WholeMixChain.js`'s own live node order exactly, per the owner's "last... every step of its existence" instruction. **Verified via a Node harness** (real bundled ffmpeg, electron stubbed via `module.register`): a +18dB peaking EQ boost centered exactly on a 1000Hz test tone raised the exported file's measured level by exactly +18dB; pairing that boost with a -18dB whole-mix gain landed the output within 0.1dB of the untouched-neutral baseline (confirms gain really is applied *after* the eq bands, not before); the identical +18dB-boost export produced byte-for-byte-equal measured levels under `parallelMixdown` on vs. off (proves the new shared helper behaves identically on both of Part B's code paths); a highpass=15000 regression check on the same 1000Hz tone still dropped it ~57dB below neutral (unchanged pre-existing behavior); zero leftover temp files. The UI-wiring half (reading `preset.wholeMix` off the already-in-scope `preset` variable, four lines) rests on code-review confidence rather than a live CDP export - deliberately skipped a live dev-app pass this time to avoid a second real native Save-dialog popup on the owner's screen in the same session (see the `parallelMixdown` entry just above).

**BUG FIX: the Export tab vanished entirely in v0.1.129 (v0.1.130)** — reported directly by the owner ("Where's the export tab?... 100% gone"), caught and fixed live. Root cause: the v0.1.129 EQ-inheritance commit's updated `mount()` doc-comment wrote `` `wholeMix` `` (backtick-quoted, a markdown/prose habit) inside an HTML comment - but that HTML comment lives inside `plugins/export/index.js`'s own `container.innerHTML = \`...\`` JS template literal (the whole tab's markup is one big backtick string). The first backtick around `wholeMix` closed that *outer* template literal early; the bare word `wholeMix` right after it then parsed as a dangling identifier (`SyntaxError: Unexpected identifier 'wholeMix'`), and the second backtick opened a *new*, unintended string that swallowed the rest of the file's markup up to the real closing backtick. Since a plugin's `import()` failure is caught by `PluginLoader.js`'s own fail-soft per-plugin loading (`try { await import(...) } catch`, logged as a console error, every other tab keeps working) - exactly the mechanism this plugin architecture was built to have - the failure never surfaced as a visible crash or error dialog, just a silently-missing tab. **A real gap in this session's own verification, worth naming plainly**: `node --check plugins/export/index.js` (a `.js` file with no `"type": "module"` in package.json) reported this file as syntactically valid the whole time - it does *not* reliably validate ESM syntax for a plain `.js` file the way the browser's own module loader does. Copying the exact same content to a `.mjs` path and re-checking reproduced the real error immediately. **Fix + verification for next time**: removed the stray backticks (plain text in the comment); confirmed via the `.mjs`-forced check *and* by fetching the actual bytes `plugin://export/index.js` serves in a live dev app and dynamically `import()`-ing them directly - both clean after the fix, Export tab back in the tab bar. **Standing lesson for any future edit to a plugin's `innerHTML`-template-literal file** (every plugin's `mount()` builds its markup this way): never put a backtick inside text that lives inside that template literal, including inside HTML comments - and `node --check somefile.js` is not sufficient to catch it if the file lacks `"type": "module"`; verify via a `.mjs`-renamed copy or an actual live plugin load instead.

**Mixer: "Save changes" updates the loaded preset in place (v0.1.131)** — inbox, flagged "takes precedence over anything": "Save button for saving changes made on the current preset without the need of creating a new one." Presets could previously only ever be created fresh via the name-input + Save row - editing the sound list after loading a preset meant retyping a name and creating a brand-new preset every time, leaving the original untouched and duplicates piling up. New `presets.js` `updatePresetSounds(id, sounds)` mirrors `updatePresetWholeMix`'s own shape exactly (same `sounds.map` structure `savePreset` uses, so a preset updated this way is structurally identical to one created fresh) - only touches `sounds`, leaves `name`/`createdAt`/`wholeMix` untouched. New `presets:updateSounds` IPC + `api.presets.updateSounds` preload, mirroring `updateWholeMix`'s own plumbing. The Mixer already tracks `state.activePresetId` (set by `loadPreset`, never cleared just by editing the mix by hand - the exact "what did we last load" semantic Preset Remix's live-preview bridge already relies on) - reused directly rather than adding new state. A new `#update-active-preset` button in the Presets modal (`updateActivePresetButtonState()`, called from `refreshPresetList()`) shows/hides itself and labels itself `Save changes to "<name>"` based on whether `state.activePresetId` still matches a preset in the current list (hidden if nothing's loaded yet, or if that preset was deleted since); a new `currentMixSounds()` helper deduplicates the sounds-array-building logic the "Save changes" and the existing "Save as new preset" (relabeled from "Save current mix" for clarity) buttons both need. **Verified**: a Node harness against the real `presets.js` (electron stubbed via `module.register`) confirmed `updatePresetSounds` replaces the sound list while leaving id/name/createdAt/wholeMix untouched, doesn't create a second preset, returns `null` on an unknown id, and that `updatePresetWholeMix` + `updatePresetSounds` don't clobber each other's fields. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed after): the button is correctly hidden before any preset is loaded; loading a preset shows it with the correct dynamic label; adding a sound to the mix via its Include button, then clicking "Save changes", persisted the updated 2-sound list onto the *same* preset id (confirmed via `presets.list()`) rather than creating a new one.

**A real production incident during this same session, worth recording accurately**: right after shipping v0.1.129, the owner reported the Export tab "100% gone." Investigated and fixed live - see the "BUG FIX: the Export tab vanished entirely in v0.1.129" entry just above for the full root cause (a stray backtick inside an HTML comment closing a plugin's `innerHTML` template literal early) - shipped as the v0.1.130 hotfix in between this feature's own build and its own release. A `Get-Process | Where-Object {ProcessName -like '*electron*'} | Stop-Process` used during CDP-verification cleanup earlier in this session is suspected to have also closed the owner's own actual running copy of the app (not just the dev/test instance), which is what prompted the "where's the export tab" question in the first place, on top of the real v0.1.129 bug underneath it - a reminder that a broad process-name kill filter during cleanup can hit more than just the intended dev session.

**"Add from link" now handles YouTube and other video sites via yt-dlp (v0.1.132)** — the owner's own direct answer to the earlier-parked idea, re-raised in the inbox: "The audio downloads should work for youtube and for whatever this can be done to. If shutter encoder can why can't we? Do it." **Researched before building** (per the standing rule): every maintained npm wrapper package considered either bundles a stale binary or - like `youtube-dl-exec` - requires Python just to run `npm install` (confirmed directly: `npm install youtube-dl-exec` failed outright on this machine with "youtube-dl-exec needs Python"), a dealbreaker for a non-developer owner's dev machine and a real fragility risk for anyone building this app fresh. Went straight to the source instead: a new `scripts/download-ytdlp.mjs` (wired as this repo's own `postinstall`, not a dependency's) fetches the real standalone Windows `yt-dlp.exe` (confirmed via a live download: no separate Python install needed, a genuine self-contained PE executable) from yt-dlp's own stable "always latest" GitHub URL into `resources/bin/yt-dlp.exe` - not checked into git (same reasoning `ffmpeg-static`'s own binary isn't), packaged via a new `electron-builder.yml` `extraResources` entry (not `asarUnpack`, since it's never inside `node_modules/asar` to begin with). New `src/main/ytdlp/ytDlpPath.js`/`runYtDlp.js` mirror `ffmpegPath.js`/`runFfmpeg.js`'s own hand-rolled spawn-wrapper shape exactly, deliberately not depending on a wrapper package's own exec API - consistent with how this app already treats `ffmpeg-static` as "just the binary," never the process-spawning logic itself. **A real gotcha caught by testing directly, not assumed**: `ytDlpPath.js`'s dev-mode relative path initially used its own *source* file depth (`src/main/ytdlp/` -> 3 levels up to repo root) - wrong, because electron-vite bundles every `src/main/**` file into one flat `out/main/index.js`, so `__dirname` at runtime is always `out/main/` regardless of a module's original source depth (confirmed by inspecting the real build output: no `out/main/plugins/` or `out/main/ffmpeg/` subdirectories exist). Fixed to match `registry.js`'s own already-correct `bundledPluginsDir()` convention (`../../` from `out/main/`) instead.

**Integration, not a second UI**: rather than a new "+"-menu entry, `library.js`'s existing `addSoundFromUrl` (v0.1.121) now falls back to a new `downloadViaYtDlp()` whenever the direct-ffmpeg attempt fails (a video-site page URL isn't itself a raw media file, so ffmpeg's own `-i` on it fails immediately and cheaply) - a plain direct-audio-link URL never reaches the fallback at all, zero added latency/risk to the already-shipped behavior. `downloadViaYtDlp` passes `--ffmpeg-location` pointing at this app's own bundled ffmpeg (no second copy needed) and writes to a `%(ext)s`-templated output path rather than a literal fixed `.wav` name, since yt-dlp's own post-processing decides the real final extension - the resulting file is found afterward by its own uuid prefix instead of assumed.

**YouTube's bot-detection, confirmed directly rather than assumed**: a `--simulate` (metadata-only, no real download - the Claude Code sandbox's own safety classifier blocked an actual content-download command outright, both informative signals kept in mind) probe against a real YouTube URL returned `ERROR: Sign in to confirm you're not a bot` - a current, active obstacle, not hypothetical. Surfaced this to the owner directly via AskUserQuestion (3 options: build with browser-cookie support / skip YouTube specifically / hold off entirely) rather than silently picking a scope - owner chose cookie support. New `ytDlpCookiesBrowser` setting (`none`/`chrome`/`edge`/`firefox`/`brave`, off by default) threads straight into yt-dlp's own `--cookies-from-browser` flag when set, letting it authenticate as a real signed-in browser session - a new Settings dropdown + hint text is explicit that this only ever reads cookies, never a password, and requires already being signed into YouTube in that browser.

**Verified**: the real downloaded `yt-dlp.exe` (17.8MB PE executable) ran directly and reported its own version with zero Python present; a `--simulate` probe against a real YouTube URL reached the bot-detection error, confirming the binary + network path work end-to-end short of the actual auth gate. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app**: the Export tab (fixed moments earlier as v0.1.130) stayed stable across this session's reloads; the new Settings dropdown round-tripped correctly (`chrome`/`firefox`/etc. persist via `settings:setYtDlpCookiesBrowser`, revert to `none` cleanly); the direct-download happy path (a locally-served test WAV, sidestepping external network flakiness) still added a sound correctly end-to-end after `library.js`'s refactor - and, incidentally, a bad first test URL (a real 404) proved the fallback chain itself works correctly live: the direct attempt failed, `downloadViaYtDlp` was automatically invoked, yt-dlp itself then failed for an unrelated reason (a Cloudflare challenge on that specific test host, nothing to do with YouTube), and both failure reasons surfaced together in one clear error message - real end-to-end proof of the fallback wiring without needing to touch actual YouTube content. **Un-verified, flagged plainly**: an actual successful yt-dlp download (with or without cookies) was never run end-to-end in this session, blocked by the sandbox's own classifier on the download step itself - the owner should try a real "Add from link" against a YouTube URL (with a browser picked in Settings) and report back whether it actually works.

**BUG FIX: exporting to Opus format always failed (v0.1.133)** — reported directly, with the real ffmpeg error pasted in: "Specified sample rate 44100 is not supported by the libopus encoder. Supported sample rates: 48000, 24000, 16000, 12000, 8000." `exportMix.js`'s final encode step (`renderFinalMixdown`/`renderFinalMixFromTracks`, both) hardcoded `-ar 44100` regardless of output format - every other codec here (pcm_s16le, libmp3lame, flac, libvorbis) accepts 44100 fine, but libopus specifically doesn't, so **every Opus export had always failed**, deterministically, 100% of the time, since the format existed. New `OUTPUT_SAMPLE_RATES`/`outputSampleRate(format)` maps `opus -> 48000`, everything else stays 44100 (the default) - ffmpeg resamples automatically at the output side regardless of the mix's own internal 44100 rate, so this is a fully isolated fix with no upstream changes needed. The owner also mentioned trying twice and only seeing the error on the second attempt - since this failure is deterministic (same format, same code path, no randomness), both attempts would fail identically; most likely explanation is simply not having the window in view during the first (an 8.5-minute export) rather than a separate bug, flagged as an assessment rather than something independently fixed. **Verified**: a Node harness (real bundled ffmpeg, electron stubbed via `module.register`) reproduced the exact failure on the pre-fix code, confirmed the fix produces a real 48000Hz Opus file of the correct duration, and confirmed WAV export (representative of every other format) is byte-path-unchanged at 44100Hz - zero leftover temp files either way.

**Whole-mix gain/EQ gets a brick-wall limiter, live and exported (v0.1.134)** — the owner's own answer ("Do it then.") to a self-review flag left in the inbox Questions while shipping v0.1.129: a preset's Preset Remix whole-mix `gainDb` (up to +24 dB) and EQ bands (up to +30 dB each) were applied with nothing capping the summed output — the same clipping exposure Composite's bake had before v0.1.113 added a limiter there — and it affected **both** the live Mixer (`WholeMixChain.js` never had a limiter) and, since v0.1.129, Export (which now inherits the same settings). Fixed in both places at once so they stay in agreement (fixing only one would make export and live playback sound different — cuts against "export sounds like what you hear live"). **Live** (`WholeMixChain.js`): a `DynamicsCompressorNode` as the last node in the chain (`...→ gain → fadeGain → limiter → destination`), configured as a brick-wall limiter (threshold −1 dB, knee 0, ratio 20, attack 3 ms, release 50 ms) — ceiling ≈ 0.95 to match the ffmpeg side, transparent well below that so a neutral / cut-only whole mix is untouched. **Export** (`exportMix.js`'s shared `buildWholeMixPostChain`): appends `alimiter=limit=0.95:level=disabled` (the exact filter Composite's bake and the reverb pass already use) right after the whole-mix gain/EQ, gated on `post.length > 0` so it's only ever added when whole-mix processing is actually present — a plain export with no Preset Remix settings leaves `post` empty and is byte-path-unchanged (keeps the blessed export loudness, see [[noctivago_feel_verdicts]]). **Verified**: direct ffmpeg test against the bundled binary — a full-scale sine + `volume=12dB` with no limiter clips hard (samples pinned to ±32767/−32768, peak 0.0 dB), the same chain with `alimiter=limit=0.95:level=disabled` lands at exactly −0.45 dB peak (0.95 × full scale), sine shape intact, no clipping. The live-side limiter's *feel* on real content (whether −1 dB threshold ever audibly pumps on a loud mix) was not A/B'd — flagged to the owner, matching how this project handles other un-feel-tested tunables.

**Per-shot Speed range for Random Interval / Scheduled sounds (v0.1.135)** — inbox: "Maybe there should be speed controls on random timer and timed scheduled audios? I think this would be cool."
