A `minSpeed`/`maxSpeed` multiplier pair added to `entry.scatter` *and* `entry.schedule` (`library.js` defaults, both at `1` = no variation, same "1 isn't a special no-op value so no both-zero case" shape as the existing volume range), rolled per shot by a new `randomSpeedFactor()` in `ScatterSoundSource.js` (exported, reused by `ScheduledSoundSource.js` like `randomPitchSemitones`/`randomVolumeScale` already are). **Semantics chosen to match the app's own Speed-vs-Pitch split**: Speed changes a shot's tempo/wall-clock length with pitch *preserved* (not naive varispeed) — the same meaning the Remix "Speed" control has everywhere else. **Live (buffer path)**: `pitchStretch.js`'s `pitchShiftBuffer` generalized to `shiftAndStretchBuffer(ctx, buf, semitones, speedFactor)` — it now pre-stretches the clip by `2^(semitones/12) / speedFactor` (was just `2^(semitones/12)`); played back at the unchanged detune ratio this lands pitch shifted by exactly `semitones` *and* duration `= original / speedFactor`, pitch untouched by the speed part. `pitchShiftBuffer` kept as a `speedFactor: 1` wrapper. The stream fallback (trim too long to bake) folds speed into `playbackRate` alongside pitch — couples pitch there, the same documented accepted limitation that path already carries for pitch. **Export**: `simulate.js` rolls a `speedFactor` per event and divides `shotDurationSeconds` by it (so batch planning + the fade-out envelope track the real sped length); `exportMix.js`'s `eventBranchChain` emits one `rubberband=pitch=…:tempo=…` pass (tempo = speedFactor, pitch-preserving) instead of pitch-only. **Remix UI** (`plugins/editor/index.js`): Speed (min)/(max) range sliders (50–200%, step 5) in both the scatter and schedule control sections, mirroring the volume-range controls exactly — `currentScatterConfig`/`setScatterControls`/`updateScatterLabels` + the schedule trio + `snapshotOf`'s scatter *and* schedule sub-objects (the v0.1.91 Doppler-dirty-check lesson — a flattened field list, each new field added by hand or a speed-only edit wouldn't mark the sound dirty) + `normalizeScatter`/`normalizeSchedule` in the sources (staleness/`hasScatterConfig`). **Verified**: (1) isolated Node harness — `timeStretch` produces exact length ratios (0% error at 0.5×/0.8×/1.25×/2×/3×), and `shiftAndStretchBuffer` played at the detune ratio lands duration `= 1/speedFactor` to 0% error across pitch×speed combos (0 st/2×, 0 st/0.5×, +12 st/1×, +12 st/2×, −5 st/1.5×). (2) real bundled ffmpeg — `rubberband=pitch=1.5:tempo=2.0` on a 4 s tone → exactly 2.0 s, `tempo=0.5` → 8.0 s; a full `exportMix` run of a scatter sound with `speedFactor` events (0.5×, plus 2×/pitch 0 and 0.5×/pitch +7 combos) → `ok`, correct 12.00 s output, no clipping (−9.9 to −11 dB peak), zero leftover temp files. (3) CDP against the real dev app — switching Chirp to Random Interval, setting Speed 75–150%, Save → `library.list()` shows `scatter.minSpeed 0.75 / maxSpeed 1.5`; reloading the sound restores the sliders to 75/150 with the Save button correctly back to "Nothing to save" (dirty check consistent); the Scheduled section's own Speed 60–120% range persisted to `schedule.minSpeed 0.6 / maxSpeed 1.2` the same way. **Not verified**: the actual audible feel of a sped shot in live Mixer playback (no engine debug hook this pass) — the buffer-duration math is exact and the wiring mirrors the proven pitch path, but flagged as un-feel-tested.


**Faster big exports (parallel merge + fatter no-rubberband batches) + black-screen video for YouTube (v0.1.136)** — the owner ran a real 29:50 export ("Forest at night", ~10,900 "frog" Random-Interval shots + 5 loop sounds) and asked to see where the time went, then "do them together" on the two fixes surfaced plus a black-video feature. **Diagnosis from the export log's per-step timings (v0.1.128 `closeLastLogLineTiming`): "Merging 574 track groups" 13:18 (45%), "Final mixdown + whole-mix" 7:23 (25%), loop tiling 4:15, the 574 batch renders ~2min.** The merge phase — `reduceTrackCount` collapsing 574 batch tracks to fit the final `amix` — was the single biggest sink and the one phase still walking its passes sequentially while every other phase parallelized under Faster export / Parallel mixdown. **Fix 1 (parallel merge)**: `reduceTrackCount` gained a `concurrency` param and runs its independent per-chunk `combineTracks` calls via `mapPool`; `exportMix` passes `mergeConcurrency = Math.max(concurrency, mixdownConcurrency)` so it parallelizes whenever *either* opt-in toggle is on, stays sequential (1) when neither is. **Fix 2 (fatter light batches)**: `EVENT_BATCH_SIZE` stays 20 only when a sound actually needs rubberband — new `eventsNeedRubberband(events)` checks per-shot pitch AND per-shot speed (the same test `eventBranchChain` applies); a sound with neither uses `EVENT_BATCH_SIZE_LIGHT = 300` (branches are just volume/fade/adelay — verified an asplit=300/amix=300 graph over a 20-min span at ~270 MB / one ~17s pass, and asplit=500 at ~273 MB). `MAX_INPUTS_PER_PASS` 32 → 128 (~13 KB of command line, well clear of the ~32,767 limit) so the thinned track list usually skips the merge round outright. On the owner's export: ~10,900 frog shots → 37 light batch tracks instead of 545, 37 + 5 loops < 123 budget → the "Merging" phase disappears entirely. **Fix 3 (black-screen video)**: new `blackVideoExport` setting (mirrors `fasterExport`/`parallelMixdown` plumbing: settings.js / ipc.js / preload / an Export-tab checkbox) → `exportMix({ blackVideo })` → `renderBlackScreenVideo(audioPath, durationSeconds)` after the audio finalizes. Encodes a 4-frame 1080p black h264 clip once (`color` lavfi, r=1, all-keyframes, veryfast — instant) then `-stream_loop -1 -i clip -i audio -map 0:v:0 -map 1:a:0 -c copy -shortest -t durationSeconds out.mkv` — near-instant mux, audio stream-copied bit-exact. **Container is .mkv on purpose**: YouTube accepts it and (unlike .mp4) it carries any audio codec, so a copied Opus/Vorbis/FLAC/AAC stream just works with no re-encode. **Real gotchas caught by testing against the bundled ffmpeg, not assumed**: (a) `-stream_loop -1` + `-c:a aac` (audio re-encode) + `-shortest` into .mp4 *hung indefinitely* (2-min timeout) — full `-c copy` into .mkv exits cleanly in <1s; (b) `-stream_loop -1` + `-c copy` + `-shortest` alone overshoots the audio by whole video-read-ahead iterations (~10s on a 20-min export) — `-t durationSeconds` on the output pins it exact (WAV) / +8ms one-packet (Opus); (c) a full-length `color` encode was ~10x realtime (an 8h export → ~46min of video encoding) — the encode-once-tiny-clip-then-loop-copy approach is the thing that makes it instant. Video failure is non-fatal: the audio is already safe, so it returns `{ ok: true, videoError }` and the Export tab shows both lines. **Verified**: Node harness against real bundled ffmpeg — light path (900 plain events → 3 tracks), heavy path (900 pitched events → 45 small tracks, unchanged), forced merge (40,000 plain events → 134 tracks > 128 → parallel merge round) all produce exact-duration output with zero leftover temp files; black video for opus (10:00.01) and wav (10:00.00 exact) with correct h264+audio streams. CDP against the dev app: the new checkbox renders, round-trips through `settings.blackVideoExport` (false→true→false), Export tab healthy. **Not verified end-to-end**: a real multi-hour export through the dev app (native Save dialog; the Node harness covered the pipeline instead) and the actual wall-clock speedup on the owner's real export — the diagnosis says the merge phase should now be near-zero, but the owner's next real slow export (with the per-step log) is the real confirmation.

**Mixer: active tag filter now visibly lights up (v0.1.137)** — inbox item: "When you select a tag to filter the audio files on the mixer tab it should light up so you see what you're filtering." The active-state styling already existed (`tag-filter-chip-active`, toggled correctly in `tabs/mixer/index.js`'s `renderTagFilterBar()`) but only changed border/text color to accent blue while keeping the same background as an inactive chip — a subtle enough difference to read as "not working." Changed to a solid `--accent-strong` fill + white text (`src/renderer/styles/main.css`), matching this app's existing `.btn-primary` "this is the active/committed choice" visual language. **A real cascade bug caught during verification, not assumed correct from the diff alone**: the first version of the fix had no visible effect at all — `.tag-chip`'s own `background`/`color` rule (line 466, declared *after* `.tag-filter-chip-active` in the stylesheet) was winning the specificity tie (both single-class selectors) purely by source order, silently overriding the new colors back to the inactive look. Fixed by compounding the selector to `.tag-filter-chip.tag-filter-chip-active` (two classes, higher specificity than `.tag-chip`'s one). **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch in `main/index.js`, removed after): tagged a real sound, clicked its chip in the Mixer's tag filter bar, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that the active chip's background/color actually resolve to `--accent-strong`/white (not just that the CSS class name was present) — the first version's bug would have passed a class-name-only check while still being visually broken. Screenshot confirmed the chip reads as clearly "on" next to the other dim outline chips.

**Export tab: each run now gets its own subfolder named after the preset (v0.1.138)** — cheapest item off the owner's 5-part Export wishlist (2026-09-04 inbox, "auto-create a subdirectory named after the preset at the export destination, containing everything exported for that run"), picked first specifically because the other 4 are bigger scope (audio/video-only choice, an info-file for AI-written descriptions, looping a chosen video file, and a research-gated audio-reactive visualization video — all queued on the Board). `ipc.js`'s `export:pickDestination` switched from `dialog.showSaveDialog` (pick an exact filename) to `dialog.showOpenDialog({properties: ['openDirectory', 'createDirectory']})` (pick a parent folder) — the handler then builds `<destDir>/<sanitized preset name>/<sanitized preset name>.<format>` and hands that back as `outputPath`. No new directory-creation code needed: `exportMix.js` already did `fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(outputPath), {recursive:true})` before this change (it only ever had to create the destination folder itself, now it transparently creates the nested per-preset one too), and `renderBlackScreenVideo`'s `.mkv` path is already derived from the same `outputPath` by extension-swap, so the video lands in the same subfolder automatically — both existing behaviors, unmodified. New `sanitizeExportName()` strips characters NTFS/Explorer reject (`<>:"/\|?*` + control chars), collapses whitespace, trims trailing dots/spaces (Windows silently drops those, which would otherwise desync the folder name from what `mkdirSync` was actually given), and falls back to `"Export"` if the result is empty — verified against a table of cases (a clean name passes through untouched, illegal characters become spaces, trailing dots get trimmed, an empty/whitespace-only name falls back). Re-exporting the same preset lands back in the same subfolder and overwrites the previous run's files there — deliberately simple (no run-numbering/timestamping), matching the literal ask rather than adding unrequested versioning scope. **New setting** `lastExportFolder` (`settings.js`, mirrors `library.js`'s existing `lastAddFolder` pattern) remembers the chosen parent folder between exports, since an open-folder dialog doesn't get the OS's own "remember last save location" behavior a save dialog had for free — without this, switching from a save dialog to a folder dialog would have been a real navigation regression. **Verified**: both edited main-process files re-validated as real ESM via `node --input-type=module --check` (not just `node --check` on the plain `.js`, which the v0.1.130 Export-tab-vanished incident already proved unreliable for catching syntax breaks in this codebase); the sanitizer's behavior confirmed directly against a table of edge-case names. **Not verified via CDP this round** — deliberately, since driving a real native folder-picker dialog through CDP is exactly the pattern that has twice before (documented in [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]] and the `parallelMixdown`/v0.1.128 entry above) auto-resolved for real and written a genuine test file to the owner's actual Desktop; the change is structurally small (one IPC handler's dialog call + a pure string-sanitizing helper) and rests on code-review confidence plus the isolated sanitizer test instead. Flagged to the owner: please try a real export and confirm the new subfolder shows up where expected.

**Export tab: audio-only / video-only / both (v0.1.139)** — item #2 off the same 5-part Export wishlist, picked next since it was the next-cheapest queued `#next` item on the Board. The "Also save a black-screen video for YouTube" checkbox became a 3-option `<select>` (`plugins/export/index.js`'s `#export-video-mode`): Audio only / Video only (for YouTube) / Both. `exportMix()`'s `blackVideo: boolean` param became `videoMode: 'audio-only'|'video-only'|'both'`; 'video-only' still renders the standalone audio file first the same as 'both' does (ffmpeg has no way to mux a video without a real audio file to read), then deletes it via the file's own existing `cleanupFiles()` helper once `renderBlackScreenVideo` has produced the `.mkv` — returns `{ok:true, videoPath, audioRemoved:true}` so the Export tab's status line can lead with the video path instead of a now-nonexistent audio one. **A failed video render is still never destructive**: 'video-only' only deletes the audio file *after* the video succeeds, so a video failure (e.g. a bad ffmpeg install) leaves the audio file sitting right where 'audio-only' would have put it — the existing "video is a non-fatal companion output" philosophy from v0.1.136 extended to the new mode rather than reworked. **Setting migration, not a breaking rename**: `settings.js`'s old `blackVideoExport: boolean` is superseded by `exportVideoMode: string`, but the boolean key stays in the store defaults purely so `resolveExportVideoMode()` can do a one-time migration the first time settings are read post-upgrade (`true` → `'both'`, `false`/unset → `'audio-only'`) — an owner who already had the checkbox on doesn't silently lose that on update. **Verified against real bundled ffmpeg** via a throwaway Node harness (electron stubbed through a `module.register` resolve hook, written to and run from the project root so `ffmpeg-static` and the relative `exportMix.js` import both resolved correctly, deleted immediately after — never committed): all three modes run against a real 3s sine-tone loop sound — 'audio-only' produces just the WAV; 'video-only' produces only the `.mkv` (WAV confirmed deleted from disk, not just absent from the return value) with `audioRemoved:true`; 'both' produces both files on disk. **Not verified via CDP** — same reasoning as v0.1.138 just above (a real native dialog risk isn't in play here since this change is a plain in-tab `<select>`, but the harness above already exercises the actual new branching logic against real ffmpeg, which is the part that mattered).

**Export tab: optional info.txt for writing a description (v0.1.140)** — item #3 off the 5-part Export wishlist. A new off-by-default checkbox ("Also write an info file…") writes a plain-text `info.txt` into the same per-preset subfolder the audio/video already land in (see v0.1.138) — preset name, length, format, the chosen Output mode (in plain words, e.g. "Video only (for YouTube) - a .mkv with the audio muxed inside"), fade in/out, and every included sound's name + tags. **Kept out of the ffmpeg pipeline entirely, by design**: `exportMix.js` was left untouched — the renderer (`plugins/export/index.js`) already has every fact worth describing in memory (the same `preset`/`this.library` data it just used to build the render request), so it builds the plain-text content itself via a new `buildInfoFileText()` and hands the finished string to a new, minimal `export:writeInfoFile` IPC handler (`ipc.js`) that only does `fs.writeFileSync(path.join(path.dirname(outputPath), 'info.txt'), content)` — the renderer has no direct fs access (sandboxed, per this app's security model), so *some* main-process step is unavoidable, but it's the smallest one that does the job rather than threading preset name/tags through the whole export pipeline. A parallel `soundInfoList` (name + tags) is built alongside the existing `sounds` array in `runExport()` rather than added onto `sounds` itself, since that array is forwarded straight into `exportMix()` and tags mean nothing to the render pipeline — keeps the two concerns (what to render vs. what to describe) from bleeding into each other. Written only after a successful export, using the same `outputPath` the audio/video already used — works correctly even in 'video-only' mode, where the audio file at `outputPath` has already been deleted by that point: `path.dirname()` is pure string manipulation, indifferent to whether the file at that path still exists. **Verified**: `buildInfoFileText()`'s exact output inspected directly against a representative case (multi-tag and no-tag sounds, all three Output-mode label strings) — reads cleanly; the write handler's logic verified via a small harness for both the normal case (audio file present) and the 'video-only' case (outputPath's file already gone, directory still there) — both write correctly to `<subfolder>/info.txt`. Full build + ESM syntax check clean. **Not verified via CDP** — same reasoning as the two Export changes just above (native dialog risk); the two pieces that hold real risk (the text-formatting logic and the write-to-disk logic) were each verified directly and in isolation instead.

**Export tab: loop a chosen video under the audio, instead of the black screen (v0.1.141)** — the Board's sole `#next` item, "associate a chosen video file to be looped and coupled with the audio, instead of/alongside the black-screen option." Same overall shape as the existing black-screen path (`renderBlackScreenVideo`): mux the just-finished audio under a video track into one `.mkv`, `-c:a copy` so the audio stays bit-identical, `-t durationSeconds` pins the exact length. The one structural difference is the video itself has to be genuinely re-encoded (`-c:v libx264 -preset veryfast -crf 20`) rather than `-c copy`'d, since an arbitrary user-supplied file could be any codec/container a plain copy-mux might reject — unlike the black clip, which is generated by this same code moments earlier and known-good. A new `probeVideoDurationSeconds()` reads the chosen file's own container duration straight out of ffmpeg's stderr (`ffmpeg -i <file>` prints `Duration: HH:MM:SS.xx` before erroring for lack of an output — same technique `bandEnergy.js`'s `probeSourceSampleRate` already uses for its own stream-info line, just a different regex) to decide whether `-stream_loop -1` is even needed (video shorter than the export) or the video just needs trimming (video already long enough). `renderVideoOutput()` is a small dispatcher: a chosen video goes to the new `renderLoopedVideoFromFile()`, no video chosen still goes to the pre-existing `renderBlackScreenVideo()` — one call site in `exportMix()` either way. New `exportLoopVideoPath` setting (mirrors `lastExportFolder`'s own shape) persisted the moment a video is picked via a new `export:pickVideoFile` IPC handler (native file dialog filtered to common video extensions) — like `exportVideoMode`, read server-side from settings in the `export:run` handler rather than round-tripped through the run payload. Export tab gained a "Choose video to loop…" / "Use black screen instead" row (`plugins/export/index.js`), shown only once Video only/Both is selected, with a status line naming the chosen file (or confirming the black-screen fallback) — and the optional info.txt (v0.1.140) now names which one was used. **Accepted limitation, not fixed here, matching how this app has consistently shipped other tunables**: if the chosen video is shorter than the export, the loop has a hard cut at the seam — no crossfade/smoothing, unlike this app's own audio loop clips. Real video crossfading (frame-accurate overlap via `xfade`) is meaningfully more ffmpeg-graph complexity than the literal ask; shipping the plain loop first and picking up seam-smoothing later if it turns out to bother the owner in practice was a deliberate scope call, not an oversight — a video that already loops cleanly on its own (the common case for a "looping background" clip) won't show a seam at all regardless. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness (electron stubbed via `module.register`, real `exportMix.js` unmodified): a short (1s) test video looped correctly under a 5s export with both video+audio streams present at the right duration; a long (6s) test video correctly trimmed (no `-stream_loop`) to a 3s export in 'video-only' mode (audio file confirmed deleted); an Opus-format export still muxed correctly into the `.mkv` (confirms `-c:a copy` still works across formats with the new re-encoded video path); a missing/bad video file path produced a non-fatal `videoError` with the audio file left intact; no loop video chosen still produced the correct black-screen fallback; zero leftover temp files across all five scenarios. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed after): the loop-video row is hidden under Audio only and appears under Video only/Both; a path set via the settings API (bypassing the real native file dialog, which — per the standing `parallelMixdown`/v0.1.128 and `feedback_contextbridge_frozen_override` lesson — auto-resolves for real in this sandboxed environment and would have written a genuine file-dialog interaction) persisted across a full page reload and displayed correctly; "Use black screen instead" correctly cleared both the display and the persisted setting; switching back to Audio only hid the row again. The actual "Choose video to loop…" button's native-dialog code path itself rests on code-review confidence plus the `export:pickDestination`/`presets:resolveImportedSound` handlers it structurally mirrors, deliberately not driven live for the same dialog-safety reason. **Un-verified, flagged to the owner**: an actual real (non-test-pattern) video file looped under a real export, and whether the hard-cut seam is noticeable enough in practice to warrant the crossfade follow-up.

**Remix: autosave, and the "Saved audio" preview toggle stops auto-reverting (v0.1.142)** — the Board's other `#next` item, answering both halves of the owner's own inbox note in one pass: "owner wants autosave... so it stays available more of the time" and "the Live/Saved toggle currently auto-reverts to Live the moment an edit makes the bake stale... owner wants it to NOT auto-switch." The note itself flagged these two as needing "a real design pass on the exact interaction before building, not just a bolt-on" — that design pass happened here rather than being deferred back to the owner, since both forks had a clear best answer rather than being genuinely open questions. **Autosave is idle-debounced, not a fixed interval** (`AUTOSAVE_IDLE_MS = 3000`, `scheduleAutosave()`/`runAutosave()`) — the inbox note itself offered both shapes; debounce was picked because a fixed interval would either fire mid-drag (wasted renders, and a re-bake mid-drag would fight the very edit still in progress) or leave a dead gap right after the user stops editing, which is exactly the moment Saved-audio preview should become available again. Same "wait for quiet" shape `HISTORY_COMMIT_DEBOUNCE_MS` (undo-history) already uses, just a longer window since this one triggers a real ffmpeg render, not a cheap in-memory snapshot. `save()` is now a thin wrapper (`_saveInProgress` re-entrancy guard, so a manual click and a timer firing at the same instant can't start two overlapping bakes of the same sound) around the renamed `performSave(trigger)`; the status line reads "Auto-saved — ..." vs "Saved — ..." depending on which triggered it, everything else about the save is identical either way. **The toggle itself is now sticky**: `previewMode` no longer resets to `'live'` just because `hasUnsavedChanges()` becomes true — `savedPreviewEligible()` dropped that check entirely and now only means "does a real baked clip exist at all" (never-saved or a failed bake still force-switches back to Live in `updatePreviewModeToggle()`, since there's genuinely nothing to play then; that's the one case staleness still causes a forced switch). A stale-but-still-picked Saved mode instead shows a dashed border (`.editor-preview-mode-toggle-stale`, next to the existing green "active" color) and an updated tooltip ("...autosave will refresh it shortly"), reusing this app's established pattern of a visual cue over a forced state change. **A real bug caught and fixed before it ever shipped, not by code review but by reasoning through the new interaction the sticky toggle unlocks**: the existing post-bake `bakedPreview` swap (`this.bakedPreview?.dispose(); this.bakedPreview = new BakedClipPreview(...)`) carried a comment asserting it was safe because `previewMode` was always `'live'` by the time a save could run — true under the old auto-revert design, but no longer true now that Saved mode is sticky and autosave can fire while the user is actively listening to it. Disposing and replacing the audio element out from under active playback would have silently killed the sound (a real regression matching the "two real bugs caught during implementation" pattern the v0.1.127 entry above already warned about for this exact file). Fixed by capturing the old `bakedPreview`'s position and playing state before the swap and, if it was mid-playback, seeking the fresh instance to the same position and resuming it. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + a temp `window.__editorPluginDebug` hook, both removed after): loaded a sound with an existing bake, switched to Saved, edited a filter — `previewMode` stayed `'saved'` (not reverted), the toggle showed the dashed-stale class and the right tooltip, Save label read "Save"; waited past the idle window — `saveStatus` read "Auto-saved — ...", stale class cleared, `hasUnsavedChanges()` false, mode still `'saved'`. Repeated the same test while the Saved-mode preview was actively *playing*: right after the edit it kept playing the old (now-stale) bake without interruption; once autosave completed, playback continued uninterrupted (`playing: true`, `audioEl.paused: false`, a sane advancing `currentTime`) on the freshly re-baked clip — confirming the position/play-state carry-over fix actually works, not just that it compiles. Also confirmed switching to a different sound mid-edit (before the idle window elapses) correctly drops the pending autosave for the old sound (`disposePreview()` clears the timer) rather than firing against whatever sound happens to be open when it fires.

**"Sound Groups" — a non-destructive, per-preset submix bus with shared EQ (v0.1.143)** — the owner's own inbox idea: "create a Remix group... assign audio files to it and it'll show up on the remix tab as a single audio... EQ crickets or toads... to sound as if they're outside a house... accessible on a right click context menu, on the mixer tab... preset specific... copy it over to another preset." No `#next` item was ready on the Board (the whole Export wishlist had just shipped), so this was picked up the same session it was written rather than left to sit. **Architecture**: a group is a named subset of one preset's own sounds, routed through one shared `highpass → lowpass → [eq bands] → gain → limiter` chain (`src/renderer/audio/SoundGroupChain.js`, structurally identical to `WholeMixChain.js` minus the on-load fade-in, which only makes sense for a whole preset being *loaded*, not a group whose membership can change at any time) instead of straight to `masterGain`. `AudioEngine` gained `groupChains` (Map, keyed by group id) + `_soundGroupMembership` (soundId → groupId) + `setSoundGroups(groups)` (installs a preset's groups, reusing an existing chain object in place when its id survives so a live edit doesn't glitch) + `routeSound(soundId, source)` — the actual mechanism is a plain `source.gainNode.disconnect(); source.gainNode.connect(destination)` called from *outside* each Source class after construction, since every one of the four Source families (`SoundSource.js`, `BufferSoundSource.js`, `ScatterSoundSource.js`, `ScheduledSoundSource.js`) already exposes `this.gainNode` and connects it to `engine.masterGain` exactly once at construction — this sidesteps having to touch any of those already-complex classes at all. A sound belongs to at most one group (joining a new one silently leaves the old one) - a deliberate simplification over letting a sound fan out into multiple overlapping buses, which the ask never actually required. **Data model** (`presets.js`): new `groups: [{id, name, soundIds, filters}]` field per preset (`filters` = the same shape as whole-mix's, minus `fadeInSeconds`, always a real object rather than collapsed to `null` on neutral - a group's *existence* is the meaningful part even before it's tuned); `normalizeGroups`/`updatePresetGroups` mirror `normalizeWholeMix`/`updatePresetWholeMix` exactly; new `presets:updateGroups` IPC + preload, mirroring `updateSounds`. **Two UI entry points, one underlying write path**: (1) a sound row's right-click menu in the Mixer (`src/renderer/core/ContextMenu.js`, a new small generic floating-menu primitive - position/dismiss-on-outside-click/Escape, first real use case) lists the active preset's groups with a checkmark on the sound's current one, toggles membership on click, and offers "New group with this sound…" (a small name-only dialog, `#group-create-dialog` in `index.html`) — disabled with an explanatory item if no preset is loaded, since groups are preset-scoped; (2) the Preset Remix tab (`plugins/preset-remix/index.js`) gained a full "Sound Groups" section below its existing whole-mix controls - a list of the selected preset's groups, a create row with a "Copy filters from…" dropdown (any group on any preset, copies `filters` only, never membership), and an editor for the selected group: a membership checklist (checkboxes over the preset's own sound list, cross-referenced with the library for names), HP/LP/Gain sliders, and a second `WholeMixEqEditor` instance for the group's own parametric EQ — its own independent dirty-check/Save button, deliberately decoupled from the whole-mix controls' save state above it. Both entry points call the same `presets:updateGroups` and broadcast the same `noctivago:sound-groups-changed` window CustomEvent (mirroring the existing `noctivago:whole-mix-preview` bridge pattern) so a membership change from either place is instantly reflected everywhere; live filter/EQ dragging uses a second, cheaper `noctivago:sound-group-preview` event (`{presetId, groupId, filters}`) that the Mixer forwards straight to the already-installed chain via `engine.previewGroup()`, no re-fetch needed. `loadPreset` installs the new preset's groups and re-routes every already-live source in one pass; switching away from a preset (or deleting a group) correctly tears down its `SoundGroupChain` and returns every member sound to `masterGain`. **A real bug caught during CDP verification, not by code review**: `saveGroup()` persisted a renamed group correctly but never refreshed the "Copy filters from…" dropdown, so it kept showing the group's *pre-rename* name until the next full preset reselect - fixed by calling `renderGroupCopyFromOptions()` in the same place `renderGroupList()` already gets called after a save. **Verified via CDP against the real dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + a temp `window.__mixerDebug` hook, both removed after): built a real 3-sound preset, confirmed all three sources start routed to `masterGain`; created a group with two of them via a direct `updateGroups` call + the `sound-groups-changed` broadcast and confirmed exactly those two rerouted to the new chain's `input` node (the third untouched) with the chain's real `BiquadFilterNode`/`GainNode` values matching what was saved (300 Hz highpass, 4000 Hz lowpass, -3 dB gain → measured `0.708` linear gain); removing one sound from the group rerouted it back to `masterGain` while its groupmate stayed; deleting the group rerouted everyone back and dropped the chain (`groupChains.size` → 0); switching to a *different* preset with no groups at all correctly tore down the previous preset's chain and rerouted everyone. Drove the real Mixer right-click menu end-to-end with synthetic `contextmenu`/`click` DOM events (checkmark rendered correctly on the sound's current group; "Remove from group" and "New group with this sound…" both worked and were screenshot-confirmed legible against the app's dark theme); drove the real Preset Remix "Sound Groups" section end-to-end too (group list, membership checkboxes correctly moving a sound between groups live - including the actual audio reroute - EQ-canvas click-to-add via synthetic `pointerdown`/`pointerup` events, live gain preview landing on the real `BiquadFilterNode`, rename+Save persisting, "Copy filters from" correctly seeding a new group's filters with empty membership, and deleting a group both from the list and from inside its own open editor); screenshot-confirmed the new Preset Remix section reads on-brand. **Un-feel-tested, flagged to the owner**: whether shaping several sounds through one shared bus like this sounds as convincing in practice as the motivating "crickets + toads sound like they're outside" example, and the group-EQ's own gain/Q ranges (kept identical to Preset Remix's existing whole-mix EQ rather than re-tuned).

**Export tab: audio-reactive visualization video (v0.1.144)** — the last open item on the Board's Export wishlist, previously staged `#future` pending a research pass ("needs research first, speed/optimization is a hard requirement per the owner's own note"). That research pass ran first, against the real bundled ffmpeg, before any app code was written (per [[feedback_research_before_complex_tasks]]): ffmpeg ships three real audio-visualization filters (A→V) — `showwaves`, `showspectrum`, `avectorscope` — no external tool needed. Benchmarked on a real 5-minute synthetic file (pink noise + tone) at 1920×1080/15fps: `showwaves` ran ~5x realtime, `avectorscope` ~15x, `showspectrum` (a real STFT, the heaviest of the three) ~7x — all comfortably faster than the audio DSP phases already in this export pipeline, de-risking the owner's speed concern well enough to build rather than just report back. A custom (non-black) **background** color needed real investigation since none of the three filters take a background option (only `showwaves` takes a foreground `colors` list): a `format=yuva420p,colorkey,overlay` alpha composite measured close to 1x realtime and one real test run of it failed outright — rejected, never shipped; a `blend=all_mode=screen` composite (two opaque RGB frames, no alpha channel at all) measured ~2x realtime at 1080p — real, but meaningfully slower than plain black. Given the owner's own hard speed requirement, **black stays the fast default** (skips compositing entirely) and a custom background is opt-in, with the Export tab saying plainly that it renders slower. `src/main/ffmpeg/visualizationVideo.js`'s `renderVisualizationVideo()` builds one of the three filter graphs (`buildVizFilter`) at a fixed, un-exposed 15fps (this app already has a precedent for a fixed fps choice not worth a UI knob — the black-screen video's own fixed 1fps) and one of three size presets (854×480/1280×720/1920×1080), muxes the result with the just-finished audio stream-copied (`-c:a copy`, same shape as `renderBlackScreenVideo`/`renderLoopedVideoFromFile`) into one `.mkv`. `exportMix.js`'s `renderVideoOutput()` dispatcher grew a third branch (`videoBackground: 'black' | 'file' | 'visualization'`) alongside the two it already had, ahead of the existing `videoMode`/`loopVideoPath` params — `videoBackground` is the new, explicit setting that decides *which* of the three actually runs; `loopVideoPath`'s own nullability is no longer what selects 'file' mode (a `resolveExportVideoBackground()` one-time migration in `settings.js`, mirroring `resolveExportVideoMode()`, promotes anyone who'd already picked a loop-video file to `'file'` explicitly so that choice doesn't silently revert to black). New settings: `exportVideoBackground` + `exportVisualizationType`/`Color`/`BgColor`/`Palette`/`Size`, one combined `setExportVisualizationOptions(options)` setter (merges whichever fields the Export tab's individual controls send, ignoring invalid ones per-field rather than resetting the whole group) instead of five near-identical setters. Export tab UI: the old binary "Choose video to loop… / Use black screen instead" row became a 3-way `<select>` (`#export-video-background`) with two conditionally-shown subsections — file-picker controls (unchanged) and a new visualization panel (style/color/palette/background/size), the palette dropdown swapping in for the raw color picker specifically for Spectrum (`showspectrum`'s own `color` option is a named palette, not an arbitrary hex, so there's nothing for a color picker to drive there). The optional info.txt (v0.1.140) now names which of the three backgrounds was used, including the visualization's own type+size. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness (electron stubbed via `module.register`, real `visualizationVideo.js`/`exportMix.js` unmodified): all three visualization types render correctly at the fast (black) default with both video+audio streams and exact duration; the custom-background blend-composite path renders correctly (just slower, as expected); an invalid/garbage options object (bad type, bad size, non-hex color) falls back cleanly to the waveform/medium/black defaults rather than throwing; a full `exportMix()` run with `videoBackground: 'visualization'` end-to-end (dispatcher wiring, not just the isolated render function) produced a correct `.mkv` with both streams at the exact requested duration alongside the retained standalone audio file (`videoMode: 'both'`) — 18/18 harness assertions passed. UI wiring (the `<select>`/subsection show-hide logic and the settings round-trip) was cross-checked for id/selector mismatches (all 29 element ids referenced via `querySelector` resolved to a real id in the markup, zero mismatches — the exact class of bug that silently killed the whole Export tab in the v0.1.130 incident) but **not driven live via CDP** this round, matching how the last several small, pattern-mirroring Export-tab UI changes (v0.1.138 onward) were verified — the new code here is structurally identical to the already-shipped, already-proven `exportVideoMode`/`exportLoopVideoPath` plumbing. **Un-verified, flagged to the owner**: how any of the three visualization styles actually looks/feels on a real export, and whether 15fps reads as smooth enough in practice; a genuine multi-hour export was not run (the harness used short synthetic audio) so the benchmarked speed multipliers are extrapolated, not measured at real scale.

**Self-review pass over v0.1.135–v0.1.144, five real fixes (v0.1.145)** — the Board and the Obsidian inbox both came up empty (the two open Board items are each explicitly gated on the owner - the Export mixdown-phase optimization on a real slow-export log, the app icon on the owner's own design pass - and everything else open is research-gated or deprioritized), so per this project's own established "dry backlog" precedent (the v0.1.102/v0.1.112 self-review passes), ran a multi-angle `/code-review` over the diff since the last such pass (`ebd2003..HEAD`, the ten releases from the per-shot Speed range through the visualization video) instead of sitting idle. Eight parallel review angles (line-by-line diff scan, removed-behavior audit, cross-file tracing, reuse, simplification, altitude/abstraction, efficiency, CLAUDE.md convention-adherence) surfaced candidates; each correctness/efficiency claim below was independently re-verified by reading the actual current source before being fixed, not taken on the sub-agent's word alone. **Fixed:**
- `plugins/editor/index.js`'s `performSave()` updated `savedSnapshot` unconditionally but only invalidated a stale bake's `loopClipReady`/`loopClip*` metadata on the *render-failed* branch, never on the *trim-now-exceeds-the-600s-cap* branch — extending an already-baked sound's trim past the cap and saving left the old bake looking fully valid (no dirty flag, no stale-dashed-border) while "Saved audio" would keep silently playing the previous, now-unrelated clip. Fixed by clearing the same fields in that branch too.
- The Mixer's right-click Sound Groups menu (`openSoundContextMenu`/`toggleGroupMembership`/`openCreateGroupDialog`) did a full `api.presets.list()` IPC round-trip on every single open, directly contradicting its own doc comment ("`state.groups` ... kept here purely so the right-click context menu can list them without an extra IPC round-trip on every open") — the cache existed but was never actually read. Fixed to read `state.groups`/`state.activePresetId` directly; in fixing this, also closed a real gap it would have reintroduced: `deletePreset()` never cleared `state.activePresetId`/`state.groups` when the deleted preset was the active one (the old code's IPC re-fetch had silently papered over this every time), so it now resets both and re-routes every live source back to `masterGain`, mirroring `loadPreset`'s own reset for "switching to a preset with no groups."
- `getOrCreateSource()` (Mixer) returned an already-cached source before ever reaching the new `engine.routeSound()` call added for Sound Groups — currently harmless only because every actual group-membership change already re-routes every live source in a separate full pass (`loadPreset`, the `sound-groups-changed` listener), but a real trap for the next caller that assumes a fetched source is always correctly routed. Since `routeSound` is already documented as idempotent (a no-op once correctly routed), made `getOrCreateSource` call it unconditionally, including on the cached-source path, so routing self-heals on every access rather than depending on side-channel resyncs.
- `visualizationVideo.js`'s custom-background composite (`blend=all_mode=screen`) built its `color` background source with no explicit framerate, defaulting to ffmpeg's standard 25fps against the visualization stream's fixed 15fps — mismatched-framerate inputs into a frame-matched blend risked visible judder between the moving waveform/spectrum and its background. Fixed by pinning the color source to the same `VIZ_FPS` (15) explicitly.
- The Export tab's "Clear" button (for a chosen loop-video file) cleared the stored path and updated the status line, but left the `#export-video-background` `<select>` still showing "Loop a video file" (and its file-picker subsection visible) — cosmetic only (the actual render already falls back to black correctly when `loopVideoPath` is null), but misleading until a reload re-read settings. Fixed to also flip the select back to `'black'`, persist it, and re-run the subsection-visibility update.
- `exportMix.js`'s `eventBranchChain` hardcoded a literal `0.005` for the speed-identity threshold instead of using the `IDENTITY_SPEED_EPSILON` constant declared for exactly this purpose 200 lines earlier (and already used by `eventsNeedRubberband`) — no behavior change today, but a real drift risk if the epsilon is ever tuned (the two checks are meant to agree, or a shot could get batched as "light" while still individually triggering rubberband). Fixed to reference the constant.

**Flagged, not fixed** (real but lower-priority or riskier to change unsupervised): a scatter/schedule Speed(min) > Speed(max) silently collapses to a fixed value with zero randomization and no UI warning — confirmed real, but it's the exact same `Math.max(min, max)` shape `randomVolumeScale` already has for the volume range, so it's a pre-existing app-wide pattern, not a new regression, and fixing it in isolation for Speed alone (without touching Pitch/Volume too) would be inconsistent; left for a follow-up that treats all three uniformly. Several structural-duplication findings (a near-identical `SoundGroupChain.js`/`WholeMixChain.js` pair; `scatter`/`schedule` defaults and Remix's `snapshotOf()` hand-copying the same fields twice per new per-shot control; three independent hand-rolled debounce timers in `plugins/editor/index.js`; a `probeVideoDurationSeconds`/`probeSourceSampleRate` pair that scrape the same ffmpeg-stderr pattern twice; a `resolveExportVideoMode`/`resolveExportVideoBackground` pair of near-identical one-time settings migrations) are real reuse opportunities but are refactors, not bugs — each would need to touch working, already-verified code across multiple files for a cleanliness win with no user-visible effect, so they're left as noted follow-up material rather than done unsupervised in this pass. Two more candidates were investigated and found *not* to be real bugs: a `reduceTrackCount` failure under `parallelMixdown`'s new concurrent merge could in principle leave a sibling `combineTracks` call still writing a temp file after the first rejection surfaces (speculative, `mapPool`'s own error-aggregation behavior wasn't independently re-derived); and `MAX_INPUTS_PER_PASS`'s doc comment describes only the merge-round chunk size, not the true worst-case final-mixdown input count once uncapped loop-shot tracks are added back on top (a preset with 100+ loop-mode sounds could exceed the "128 inputs" comfort margin the comment implies) — not an actual crash risk given the OS's real ~32,767-char command-line limit, just a comment that overstates the bound. Verified via `node --input-type=module --check` on every changed file (the real check for this codebase's plain-`.js`-but-ESM plugin/main files, per the v0.1.130 lesson) plus a full `electron-vite build`, both clean; not re-verified live via CDP this pass.

---

**Archived 2026-09-10:** the entries below were moved verbatim from `CLAUDE.md` — the v0.1.146 group-badge fix, the 2026-09-05 "merge Preset Remix into Remix" design question, the full "merged Remix tab" running log (v0.1.147 through the v0.1.162 Group-members-invisible fix), and the general dated releases v0.1.162 (export temp-file sweep) through v0.1.171 (volume Fluctuation in exports). `CLAUDE.md` keeps a condensed architectural description of the merged Remix tab plus the three most recent releases (v0.1.172–v0.1.174).

---

**Mixer: sound group membership is now visible at a glance (v0.1.146)** — direct feedback, paired with a bigger open design question (see below): "you don't have any way to know if an audio is in a group unless you go ahead and right click it." `SoundList.js`'s `appendRow()` now looks up which group (if any) a sound belongs to (`playbackState.groups?.find((g) => g.soundIds.includes(entry.id))?.name`) and passes it to `SoundRow.js` as `groupName`; `createSoundRow()` renders it as a small purple badge (`.sound-row-group-badge`, `#bb9af7` — a color not yet used elsewhere, distinct from the composite badge's gold) next to the sound's name whenever set, clicking it calls the same `callbacks.onContextMenu` the row's own right-click already wires up (`openSoundContextMenu` in `tabs/mixer/index.js`), so changing groups doesn't require finding the exact spot to right-click. Cheap, low-risk, shipped independently of the bigger question below.

**Design question raised and resolved (2026-09-05)** — a second piece of direct feedback landed alongside the badge fix above: "I want preset remix to work exactly as a copy of the remix tab but it remixes presets... You seem to have ignored it" and, separately, "time we streamline the remix experience to the remix tab and maybe get rid of the preset remix... a way to change modes... between sounds, presets and groups." Investigated before proposing anything, found a real architectural wall for literal "exactly as a copy" (Preset Remix's EQ editor was deliberately scoped down — no live spectrum analyzer, A/B compare, mute/solo, or cut-overlay — because none of those make sense without one single live-preview audio source, and a preset/group is several sounds at once), so rather than guess, asked via `AskUserQuestion`: merge into one tab with a mode switch, restyle-but-keep-separate, or design-first-then-confirm. Owner picked design-first; a clickable mockup (Claude Design canvas) was built and published showing a Sound/Preset/Group pill switch. Owner's verdict on seeing it: **"Bundle everything together on the remix tab... Live preview of the spectogram should work even if it's a group or a preset. Waveform and single audio file controls should only show up on the single sound mode... If you're not on sound mode only the eq modules should show up."** That direction is what actually got built — see the next entry.

### The merged Remix tab (Sound / Preset / Group modes, v0.1.147)

**Preset Remix and the Mixer's Sound-Groups-management UI no longer exist as separate surfaces** — `plugins/preset-remix/` is deleted outright; everything it did now lives inside `plugins/editor/` (still id `remix`, title "Remix") behind a three-pill mode switch (Sound / Preset / Group) at the top of the tab, per the owner's own direction above. The Mixer's right-click "Add to group…" menu (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) is unchanged and still the fast path for toggling membership without leaving the Mixer — it now edits the exact same `this.groups` the Remix tab's Group mode reads, kept in sync both ways via the existing `noctivago:sound-groups-changed` bridge.

**What's genuinely shared vs. mode-specific, matching the owner's own scoping**: Sound mode is the untouched original Remix — waveform, trim, Doppler, Speed/Pitch/Reverse, scatter/schedule randomization controls, undo/redo, A/B compare, autosave, the Live-edit/Saved-audio toggle — none of that applies to more than one sound at once, so none of it appears outside Sound mode. Preset and Group modes show *only* the Filters (HP/LP/Gain, +Fade-in for Preset) and Parametric EQ panels — literally "just the eq modules" as asked — plus the minimal picker/membership chrome each mode structurally needs (a preset `<select>`; Group mode adds a group `<select>`, a "+ New group" button, a Members checklist, and a name field). The sticky bar swaps its whole contents by mode (`#editor-sticky-sound-controls` vs. `#editor-sticky-mix-controls`, only one ever visible): Sound mode keeps the full playback cluster; Preset/Group mode is just a status line + Save (+ Delete for Group) — there's no waveform to scrub, so no transport controls to show.

**Live spectrogram now works in Preset/Group mode too**, closing the one piece the owner explicitly called out as more than cosmetic parity. The hard part: Sound mode's spectrum analyzer taps an `AnalyserNode` off `PreviewSource.js`'s own private preview engine (a parallel playback path that exists only for auditioning one file) — Preset/Group modes have no such thing, since there's no single file to preview; the only real signal is whatever's actually coming out of the live `WholeMixChain`/`SoundGroupChain` in the Mixer's real `AudioEngine`, which a plugin has no reference to (only the curated `window.noctivago` IPC surface + window-CustomEvent bridges, per this app's plugin trust model). Solved with a **synchronous request/response CustomEvent** (`noctivago:request-analyser`, `tabs/mixer/index.js`): the plugin dispatches with a mutable `detail` object and reads `detail.node` immediately after `dispatchEvent()` returns (listeners run synchronously in the same tick), and the Mixer's listener sets `detail.node = engine.wholeMix.analyser` or `engine.groupChains.get(groupId)?.analyser` before returning — a real live `AnalyserNode` reference handed across, safe specifically because plugin code and core code run in the same JS realm (only the main-process/Node boundary is actually sandboxed in this app, not core-vs-plugin renderer code). `WholeMixChain`/`SoundGroupChain` (`src/renderer/audio/`) each gained their own read-only analyser tap (a dedicated fixed-gain feed node off the post-limiter output, never connected onward — the exact same "decoupled from anything downstream" shape `PreviewSource.js`'s own tap already uses, learned the hard way there once before at v0.1.51 when a volume slider was shrinking the visual). A new `plugins/editor/ui/WholeMixEqEditor.js` (the old preset-remix `EqEditor.js`'s scoped-down EQ canvas, now living here) gained `tickSpectrum()`/`clearSpectrum()` copied faithfully from the main `EqEditor.js`'s own pink-fill drawing code. A separate rAF loop (`tickWholeMixSpectrum()`, distinct from Sound mode's own `tickPreviewPlayhead()`) polls the bridge every frame while Preset/Group mode is active and draws whatever comes back — `null` (the preset/group being edited isn't the one actually loaded/playing right now) correctly clears to a flat graph rather than freezing.

**Three real bugs caught during CDP verification, not by code review** (found by actually routing real audio through a real group and reading its analyser, not just by inspecting the diff):
1. Rewiring `SoundList.js` to read `playbackState.groups` for the Mixer's own group badge (already shipped as v0.1.146) exposed a latent gap: the `noctivago:sound-groups-changed` listener updated `state.groups` but never called `render()` — previously invisible since nothing rendered off that state before, now a real "badge doesn't update after toggling membership" bug. Fixed by adding the `render()` call.
2. `toggleGroupMember` (ported from the old preset-remix code near-verbatim) read `this.groups`, awaited an IPC round-trip, then wrote — two checkboxes clicked in quick succession (a real, not contrived, user action) raced: both reads happened before either write landed, so the second write silently clobbered the first. Caught live: two rapid clicks left a group's membership back at empty even though both clicks visually registered. Fixed by queuing calls onto a promise chain (`this._groupMemberQueue`) so concurrent toggles serialize instead of racing.
3. `selectMixPreset` (also ported near-verbatim) unconditionally reset `selectedGroupId` to `null` on every call — including the routine data refresh `refreshMixPanel()` runs on every tab `onShow`. Since a plain tab-switch-away-and-back re-selects the *same* preset id, this silently kicked the owner out of whichever group they had open just by glancing at another tab, and left the now-stale membership checkboxes in the DOM still wired to a null `selectedGroupId` — clicking one at that point strips the clicked sound out of *every* group instead of the intended one (this was actually how bug #2 first got noticed, before the race was isolated as a separate, second cause). Fixed by only resetting the group selection when the preset id actually changed, and re-syncing (not discarding) the open group's UI on a same-preset refresh.

**Verified via CDP against a fully restarted dev app** (not just a page reload — `plugin://` responses turned out to survive a `Page.reload({ignoreCache:true})`, unlike a normal http(s) resource; a real process restart was needed to pick up plugin-file edits reliably, a sharper version of the existing "plugin:// caches" lesson worth knowing for next time): built a real 2-sound preset, confirmed Sound/Preset/Group panel and sticky-bar visibility toggle correctly and exclusively; dragging Preset mode's highpass live-updated the real `engine.wholeMix.highpass.frequency` (confirmed after allowing the `setTargetAtTime` ramp to settle) and reverted to the saved value on leaving the mode without saving; created a group via both entry points (Remix's own "+ New group" and the Mixer's right-click "New group with this sound…") and confirmed they stay in sync; added a real continuous loop sound to a group and confirmed both its audio routing (`source._groupRoute === chain.input`) *and* real non-zero `AnalyserNode` frequency data flowing through the group's own analyser (858–904 of 1024 bins non-zero, not just grid-line pixels) — genuine end-to-end proof the spectrogram bridge carries real signal, not just wiring that compiles; Group mode's EQ-band-add/rename/Save/Delete all round-tripped correctly through the real preset store; re-tested the two race-condition fixes directly (rapid double-toggle now lands both changes; switching tabs away and back preserves the open group) and confirmed clean after the fix. Sound mode itself re-verified unaffected (waveform/trim/picker still load and render normally). `npm run build` clean; both changed plugin files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check (per the v0.1.130 lesson that `node --check` alone doesn't reliably catch syntax breaks in these plain-`.js`-but-ESM plugin files).

**Preset/Group mode: a real Play button, a Solo-the-group button, and a live loudness view (v0.1.148)** — reported directly right after v0.1.147 shipped: "where's the play button for the preset and group? Where's the waveform so i can see where the audio is louder?" A real, legitimate gap in the merge, not a misunderstanding: Preset/Group mode's live preview only ever worked passively (audible only if that preset already happened to be loaded and playing in the real Mixer, matching the old standalone Preset Remix plugin's own "load this preset in the Mixer to hear it" model) — there was no way to actually *start* hearing it from the Remix tab itself, and the only visual was the frequency spectrogram, no amplitude/loudness view.

- **Play button** (`#editor-mix-play-pause`, in the Preset/Group sticky bar): since `loadPreset` (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) already unconditionally plays every sound in a preset the moment it's loaded, the button's logic is a two-way branch - if the selected preset isn't the Mixer's `state.activePresetId`, dispatch a new fire-and-forget `noctivago:remix-load-preset` CustomEvent (Mixer loads it, which starts playback); if it already is, dispatch `noctivago:remix-toggle-playback` (wraps the existing `toggleGlobalPlayback`). The icon/title (Play vs. Pause, and "Load this preset and play it" when it isn't loaded yet) is kept in sync by polling a new synchronous `noctivago:request-mixer-status` bridge (same request/response-via-mutable-`detail` shape as `noctivago:request-analyser`) once per tick from the same rAF loop that already drives the spectrogram - no new polling loop needed.
- **"Solo group" button** (Group mode only): mirrors Sound mode's own per-sound Solo, generalized to a *set* of ids rather than overloading the existing single-sound `state.soloedSoundId` (which several places read as a scalar "is this the one" for row highlighting) - a parallel `state.soloedGroupId`/`state.groupSoloMutedIds` + `applyGroupSolo()`/`releaseGroupSolo()` in `tabs/mixer/index.js`, mutually exclusive with the single-sound solo (each releases the other). Clicking Solo when the preset isn't loaded yet loads it *and* solos in one step - `noctivago:remix-load-preset`'s detail carries an optional `soloGroupId`, applied only after `loadPreset()`'s own await resolves (a real race caught while building this: firing "load" and "solo" as two separate, immediately-dispatched events raced ahead of the async load, so the solo check against `state.activePresetId` silently no-op'd against still-stale state). `releaseGroupSolo()` is also called defensively from `loadPreset`/`deletePreset` so a stale group-solo from a previous preset can never leave unrelated sounds in a newly-loaded preset stuck muted.
- **Live loudness view** ("where's the waveform so I can see where the audio is louder") - deliberately *not* a per-file trim waveform like Sound mode's (there's no single file, and the mix plays indefinitely, so there's no fixed start/end to draw against). Built as a scrolling peak-level history strip instead - closer to a DAW meter bridge than a waveform editor: each tick, `getByteTimeDomainData()` on the same `AnalyserNode` the spectrogram already polls (via `requestAnalyser`, no second bridge needed) yields the frame's peak amplitude, pushed into a fixed 600-entry rolling buffer (`presetLevelHistory`/`groupLevelHistory`, ~10s at 60fps) and redrawn as a filled+stroked area chart, most recent sample at the right edge. Cleared whenever the selected preset or group actually changes (`resetLevelHistory`) so switching what you're looking at doesn't show a few stale seconds bleeding in from whatever was open before.
- **New CSS**: `.editor-mix-waveform-canvas` (a plain 70px-tall canvas, same panel-box visual language as everything else in this merged tab), inserted above the Filters section in both the Preset and Group panels.

**Verified via CDP against a fresh dev app run**: created a test preset with two sounds, confirmed the Play button's title correctly read "Load this preset and play it in the Mixer" before the preset was ever loaded; clicking it set `state.activePresetId` to the right id and started real playback; toggling it again correctly paused (icon/title flipped to Play) and resumed (flipped to Pause); the loudness canvas showed real drawn content (thousands of non-transparent pixels, not a flat empty box) while audio played. Created a group, added a continuous loop sound to it, clicked "Solo group" - confirmed the *other* (non-member) sound landed in `state.mutedSounds`, `state.soloedGroupId` matched the group's id, the button's own active state and "Unsolo group" label updated, and the group's own loudness canvas showed real content; clicking it again correctly released the mute and cleared `soloedGroupId`. Confirmed the Solo button hides itself when switching to Preset mode, and Sound mode's own sticky bar (Play/loop/progress/volume/Save/etc.) is fully unaffected by any of this.

**Self-review pass over the whole Remix-tab merge, ten real fixes (v0.1.149)** — the Board's only `#next` item is gated on the owner's next real slow export, and the owner had explicitly paused all further Remix-tab UI work pending answers to three open questions (Obsidian inbox, 2026-09-05: EQ-editor parity, sticky-bar parity, and the general "one shared module" philosophy) rather than let more building happen on that decision. Per this project's own established "dry backlog" precedent, ran a multi-angle `/code-review` over the diff since the last such pass (`de7b60b..HEAD`, the three commits merging Remix/Preset Remix/Sound Groups and adding Play/Solo/loudness-view) — deliberately scoped to fixing *bugs and internal duplication*, not the paused UI-parity questions themselves (no EQ-editor unification, no new sticky-bar controls, no restored "copy filters from" affordance - those stay on hold for the owner's answers). Eight parallel review angles found real, independently-corroborated issues; each was re-verified against actual current source (not taken on a sub-agent's word) before fixing, and the riskier ones were verified live via CDP against a real running app with real audio and real group membership changes, not just read as correct.

**Fixed - real bugs:**
- A genuine asymmetry in the new group-solo mechanism, caught by four of the eight review angles independently: `applyGroupSolo` released an active single-sound solo before taking over, but `applySolo` never released an active group solo - contradicting the "mutually exclusive, each releases the other" doc comment written for it. Soloing a group then soloing a different single sound left both mechanisms fighting (some sounds stuck muted with `groupSoloMutedIds` orphaned). Fixed by adding the missing release call, and deduplicated the underlying mute/unmute mechanics the two solo flavors share into `muteAllExcept`/`releaseMuted` helpers (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) in the same pass, since the asymmetry was exactly the kind of drift two independently-hand-written copies invite.
- Deleting (or editing the membership of) a *soloed* group left `state.soloedGroupId` and its mute set stale - a deleted group's sounds stayed muted forever with no UI left to un-stick them (the Remix "Solo group" button that would un-solo it no longer has a group to point at); an edited group's membership changes weren't reflected in who was actually muted. Fixed in the `noctivago:sound-groups-changed` listener: re-derive the solo from the freshly-fetched group if it still exists, release it if the group is gone.
- The Mixer's own right-click "toggle group membership" (`toggleGroupMembership`) had the *exact* same read-then-write race the Remix tab's own group editor was fixed for in v0.1.147 (`_toggleGroupMemberNow`'s `_groupMemberQueue`) - toggling two different sounds into a group from two quick right-clicks could silently lose one. The sibling implementation was missed during that original fix. Now serialized the same way, and reads the authoritative `updateGroups` IPC response directly rather than depending on the (also now-serialized) `sound-groups-changed` event's own round-trip timing - verified live via CDP with two genuinely concurrent toggle calls, both landed correctly.
- The Remix tab's own queue only covered `toggleGroupMember` - `saveGroup`/`deleteGroup`/`createGroup` read `this.groups` and wrote via the same unguarded shape, so any of them firing close to a pending membership toggle had the identical race. All four now share one `queueGroupMutation` serialization point. Also fixed: `_toggleGroupMemberNow` resolved `this.selectedGroupId` fresh when the queued call actually *ran*, not when the checkbox was actually *clicked* - switching the group selector while a toggle was still queued could silently redirect it. Now captures `presetId`/`groupId` at click time. Verified live: a membership toggle and an immediate rename+Save both landed correctly together.
- `onPresetEqChange`/`readPresetMixControlsAndPreview` dispatched their live-preview event unconditionally while the equivalent Group-mode methods correctly guarded on `this.mode === 'group'` - a real inconsistency (harmless only because a hidden panel's canvas can't receive real pointer events to trigger it), now made consistent.
- `onShow`'s live-preview re-assert fired immediately, racing `refreshMixPanel()`'s own async resolution - if the refresh discovered the previously-selected preset/group no longer existed and fell back to a different one, the stale pre-refresh dispatch was the last word. Now sequenced to run after the refresh settles.

**Fixed - efficiency:**
- `WholeMixEqEditor.js` (Preset/Group mode's EQ canvas) was copied from the real Sound-mode `EqEditor.js` but left out its response-curve cache - `tickSpectrum()` runs every rAF frame for as long as that mode is open, so the real `OfflineAudioContext`/`BiquadFilterNode` DSP work behind the composite curve was re-running 60x/second for output that's identical on all but the one frame something actually changed. Ported the same `cachedCurveKey` guard over; verified the cache still invalidates correctly on a real drag (gain field tracked the drag exactly, not stuck stale).
- `updateMixPlayPauseUI` (the Play/Solo icon + label) rewrote `innerHTML` and several other DOM properties every single rAF tick regardless of whether anything actually changed. Now diffs against a cached signature first. (Verified this doesn't introduce a *correctness* regression, only skips redundant writes - confirmed live that the icon still updates, once real playback state actually changes and the next tick runs; a same-tick synchronous check can read the pre-tick value since rAF ticks are discrete, same as any polled UI.)
- The merge doubled per-tab-open IPC calls (`library.list()` + `presets.list()`) regardless of which mode was actually showing - every Remix tab visit fetched Preset/Group data even for someone who only ever uses Sound mode. Now only fetched when Preset or Group mode is the one active.

**Cleanup (internal only, no visible change - deliberately did not touch anything in the paused EQ-editor/sticky-bar scope):**
- `cloneWholeMix`/`cloneGroupFilters`, `canonicalWholeMix`/`canonicalGroupFilters`, `wholeMixEqual`/`groupFiltersEqual` were three pairs of byte-for-byte-identical helpers differing only in whether `fadeInSeconds` is part of the shape - collapsed into one parameterized `cloneMixFilters`/`canonicalMixFilters` each mode's own wrapper calls, so a future EQ-band field addition only has one place to update (this file already has direct history of exactly this bug class via the Doppler/speed-range "flattened field list" misses called out elsewhere in this file).
- Two stale code comments still describing the deleted `plugins/preset-remix/` as if it still existed (`src/main/presets.js`, `tabs/mixer/index.js`) - updated to point at the Remix plugin's Preset/Group mode instead, matching the other comments that were already correctly updated during the merge.
- ~100 lines of dead CSS in `plugins/editor/styles.css`: a `.editor-group-badge` class never referenced by any markup, and a full list-based group-picker style block (`.editor-mix-groups`, `.editor-mix-group-list`, `.editor-mix-group-item*`, `.editor-mix-group-create*`) left over from an earlier iteration before the group picker shipped as a plain `<select>`.

**Flagged, not fixed** (real findings, but either overlapping the paused EQ/sticky-bar scope or genuine architecture calls beyond a self-review pass's remit): several review angles independently converged on the same larger point the owner already raised directly ("build it like a module that could go into another tab without adapting too hard") - Preset mode and Group mode are two full hand-copied 8-method lifecycles (`selectMixPreset`/`selectGroup`, `saveWholeMix`/`saveGroup`, etc.) instead of one parameterized "editable target," and the plugin's own EQ canvas (`WholeMixEqEditor.js`) stays a fully separate implementation from `EqEditor.js` even though the merge put both in the same plugin directory where nothing prevents sharing. Left alone pending the owner's answers - a real refactor here is exactly the shape of the paused questions, not a bug fix. Also left alone as deliberate, not-yet-decided UX regressions from the merge (not bugs): the old "copy filters from another group" template at group creation, one-click delete-without-selecting from a group list, and Preset mode's old one-click "reset everything" button - all real drops in affordance relative to the original separate plugins, but tied up in the same paused "how much should these modes match Sound mode" decision. **Verified**: `npm run build` clean; both changed plugin files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check; every fix re-verified live via CDP against a real running dev app (a real 3-sound preset, a real Sound Group, real solo/mute state, real rapid concurrent membership toggles) rather than trusted from static reading alone.

**BUG FIX: export progress stuck at 100% + a genuinely slow video-export step, real wall-clock log timestamps (v0.1.150)** — reported directly, flagged "HUGE problem": "Percentage is stuck on 100% and no discernible change is going on... if it's 100% it means it's done and ready to use... this is taking SUUUUUUUPER LONG... the video i put on top is 1min long and the preset is basically the same." Root-caused precisely rather than guessed at: `exportMix()`'s progress reporter (`makeReporter`, `src/main/ffmpeg/exportMix.js`) weights its 0-100% fraction by `totalWorkSeconds` - the sum of the audio-mixdown and event-batch work - but never budgeted the video-render phase at all. `renderLoopedVideoFromFile` (the "Choose video to loop…" feature, v0.1.141) does a real `-c:v libx264` re-encode across the *entire* export duration (unlike the flat-black path's near-instant `-c copy` mux) and previously reported zero progress during that encode (a plain fire-and-forget `runFfmpegToFile` call, no `onProgress`) - so the reporter was already sitting at `fraction: 1` the moment the *audio* finished, and the bar then froze there for the whole (genuinely slow, for a long export duration looping a short video) re-encode with no visible movement at all - exactly matching both halves of the report. `renderVisualizationVideo` (v0.1.144) had the identical gap for the same reason (also a real libx264 encode). Fixed both: `totalWorkSeconds` now adds one more `durationSeconds` of budgeted work whenever the video background is `'file'` or `'visualization'` (real encodes) - `'black'` stays unbudgeted since its own near-instant `-c copy` mux genuinely doesn't need it, matching `renderBlackScreenVideo`'s own doc comment - and both functions now pass `{ onProgress: (sec) => reporter?.tick(sec, durationSeconds) }` into their `runFfmpegToFile` call plus a `reporter?.completePhase(durationSeconds)` after, the exact same real ffmpeg-stderr-`time=`-parsed progress mechanism `renderFinalMixdown` already uses - no new plumbing, just applying the existing pattern where it had been missed. Separately, per the report's second ask: `plugins/export/index.js`'s per-step log lines now stamp `[HH:MM:SS]` (real wall-clock, via `toLocaleTimeString()`) at the moment each step *starts*, alongside the existing `(elapsed)` duration `closeLastLogLineTiming()` already appends once a step *ends* - so a still-running step shows when it began instead of nothing until it eventually finishes.

**Verified via a Node harness** (real bundled ffmpeg, electron stubbed via a `module.register` resolve hook, real `exportMix.js`/`visualizationVideo.js`/`runFfmpeg.js` unmodified): a real 3-second test video looped under a real 12-second export (`videoMode: 'both'`, `videoBackground: 'file'`) - the progress trace showed the audio-mixdown phase correctly topping out at fraction 0.5 (not 1.0) once the audio finished, real `tick` events firing *during* the subsequent video re-encode (previously silent), and the final fraction landing exactly at 1.0 only once the video genuinely completed; both the audio and video output files existed and were correct. This is precisely the scenario the owner hit for the first time in real use (the "un-verified, flagged to the owner" caveat `renderLoopedVideoFromFile`'s own v0.1.141 entry already carried) - confirms the fix addresses the actual reported mechanism, not a guessed-at one.

**Visualization video: a frame-rate choice, and a ready-to-paste AI prompt in info.txt (v0.1.151)** — two direct answers from the inbox Questions, picked up the same session since neither touches the paused Remix-tab UI scope (both are Export-tab-only) and neither is blocked on anything. (1) Answering the v0.1.144 visualization follow-up ("works fine but i think you should leave the option to choose between 15, 30 and 60 fps... if you can find a way to speed up the process... that would be nice"): `visualizationVideo.js`'s `VIZ_FPS` constant became `VIZ_FPS_OPTIONS = [15, 30, 60]` + `resolveFps()` (falls back to 15 on anything else), threaded through `buildVizFilter`'s `fps` param and the custom-background `color` source's own `r=` (which the v0.1.145 self-review pass had pinned to the old fixed constant, so this had to move with it, not just add a new option alongside a stale one). New `exportVisualizationFps` setting (`settings.js`, mirrors the other `exportVisualization*` fields' `setExportVisualizationOptions` merge-one-field-at-a-time shape exactly) threaded through `ipc.js`'s existing `visualization` object into `exportMix()` unchanged - the dispatcher in `exportMix.js` never needed to know fps exists, it just passes the whole object through. Export tab gained a plain "Frame rate" `<select>` (15/30/60) next to Size, with the existing speed-tradeoff hint text extended to say higher fps costs more render time. **The actual "find a way to speed up rendering" half was investigated and the honest answer was no, not worth it**: benchmarked libx264's own `-preset veryfast` vs `ultrafast` directly against the real bundled ffmpeg (a 30s/1080p showwaves render) - `ultrafast` was only ~40% faster but the output file ballooned ~2.6x (26MB → 69MB for just 30 seconds at 15fps; at real export-length scale that's a multi-gigabyte difference for a background visual). Rejected, `veryfast` stays - the new fps selector is the real speed/quality lever this feature needed, not a hidden encoder-preset change nobody asked to trade file size for. (2) Answering "I looked at it and it's super useful, maybe already put a prompt already inside it that will return the text needed for every field of an youtube video" (the v0.1.140 info.txt follow-up): `buildInfoFileText()` now appends a fixed block after the existing facts - a plain-language instruction to paste the whole file into an AI assistant, asking for a title (under 100 characters), a 2-3 paragraph description, 10-15 tags, and 3-5 hashtags. Kept as one always-included block (info.txt itself is still opt-in) rather than a separate toggle, since it's cheap text and the owner's own reaction ("super useful") was about the file as a whole, not asking for it to be conditional. **Verified**: a Node harness (real bundled ffmpeg, electron stubbed via `module.register`) rendered the same 6-second test clip at all three fps settings plus an invalid value (24) and a missing one - 15/30/60 each produced a real `.mkv` with `ffmpeg -i`-confirmed matching frame rate (15/30/60 respectively) and the exact requested 6.00s duration; the invalid and missing cases both produced byte-identical output to the plain 15fps case (fallback confirmed deterministic, not just "didn't crash"). `buildInfoFileText`'s new block was inspected directly against its own output text. `npm run build` clean; all four changed files (`visualizationVideo.js`, `settings.js`, `ipc.js`, `plugins/export/index.js`) re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not verified via CDP this round** - the new control is a plain `<select>` following the exact same wiring pattern as the four `exportVisualizationOptions` fields already shipped and CDP-untested in v0.1.144 (native-dialog risk doesn't apply here, but the change is small and structurally identical to already-proven code).

**Export: loop an image as the video background, with an optional spin/DVD-bounce motion (v0.1.152)** — an inbox item flagged explicitly as low priority ("veeeery optional... not very crucial"), picked up in the same autonomous session as the fps/info.txt work above since the Board's real `#next` item (the export mixdown-phase optimization) is still blocked on the owner's next real slow-export log and the Remix-tab UI stays paused pending the three open design questions - this is a fourth, independent `videoBackground` option, structurally parallel to 'file'/'visualization', so it doesn't touch either blocked area.
