New `src/main/ffmpeg/imageLoopVideo.js`'s `renderImageLoopVideo()` builds one of three ffmpeg filter graphs (`buildImageFilter`): 'none' scales+pads the image to fill a fixed 1920x1080 black canvas without distorting an arbitrary source aspect ratio; 'spin' scales+pads the image into a 700x700 square first (sized so its diagonal can never exceed the canvas's shorter dimension while rotating) then applies a continuous `rotate=a='2*PI*t/8'`; 'bounce' scales the image into a small box and moves it via the classic "DVD logo" triangle-wave overlay expression (`abs(mod(t*speed,2*range)-range)` on both x and y, different speeds per axis so the path doesn't retrace a single diagonal forever) - the same technique and mux shape (`-c:a copy`, `-t durationSeconds` pinning the exact length) every other companion-video renderer in this file already uses. Fade in/out reuses the export's own existing `fadeInSeconds`/`fadeOutSeconds` (already parameters on `exportMix()` for the audio) rather than a duplicate pair of fields - a deliberate simplification since a full-frame `fade=t=in/out` toward black is visually indistinguishable from fading just the image, given the canvas is already black. `exportMix.js`'s `renderVideoOutput` dispatcher gained a fourth branch (`videoBackground === 'image' && imagePath`) and `videoNeedsRealEncode` now includes `'image'` (a real `-c:v libx264` encode, same progress-budgeting fix v0.1.150 already applies to `'file'`/`'visualization'`). New `exportImagePath`/`exportImageMotion` settings (`settings.js`, mirroring `exportLoopVideoPath`'s own shape) + a new `export:pickImageFile` IPC handler (mirrors `export:pickVideoFile` exactly, filtered to png/jpg/jpeg/webp/bmp) threaded through `ipc.js`'s `export:run` into `exportMix()`. Export tab gained a third `#export-video-background` option ("Loop an image") with its own subsection (Choose image…/Clear buttons, a path display, a Motion `<select>`) - `updateVideoBackgroundSubsectionVisibility()` extended from two mutually-exclusive subsections to three. `buildInfoFileText()`'s video-description line now names the image + its motion when that background is selected. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg before writing any app code** (per [[feedback_research_before_complex_tasks]]), including sampling actual output pixels rather than just checking for a zero exit code - a real, non-obvious gap caught during this: a first pass at verifying the spin animation used a flat-single-color test image and found the sampled pixel identical at every timestamp, which would have looked like a broken/no-op rotation if trusted at face value - the actual bug was in the test, not the filter (a uniform-color square looks the same at any rotation angle); switching to an asymmetric two-color test image confirmed the sampled point genuinely cycles between both colors as the square spins. Also confirmed: the DVD-bounce overlay lands at the exact mathematically-expected (x,y) position at several timestamps (sampled real output pixels there and at a background corner); the fade brightness ramp is a smooth near-black → full → near-black curve across 5 sample points. **Verified end-to-end** via a Node harness calling `exportMix()` itself (not just the isolated renderer function) with a real one-sound preset, `videoMode: 'both'`, `videoBackground: 'image'`, `imageMotion: 'bounce'` - produced a correct 6.00s audio file and a correct 6.00s 1920x1080 `.mkv` with the audio stream intact, confirming the full dispatcher wiring works, not just the renderer in isolation. `npm run build` clean; all six changed/new files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not verified via CDP** - same reasoning as the fps/info.txt entry just above (small, structurally-identical `<select>`/button wiring); the parts carrying real risk (the three ffmpeg filter graphs and the end-to-end `exportMix()` dispatch) were the ones verified directly against real ffmpeg instead.

**BUG FIX: "Saved audio" preview locking itself out mid-playback + missing spectrogram in that mode (v0.1.153)** — reported directly, flagged fix-ASAP: "I figured a way to reproduce the live preview vs saved audio bug. It happens when the saved audio preview is playing. Once you save new changes on top of it while it plays it locks out the saved audio option and you can't choose it... The spectogram isn't visible either with the saved audio." Both root-caused via live CDP repro against a real dev build rather than guessed at — the first attempt (documented in the v0.1.142 entry's own follow-up thread) had failed to reproduce it because the earlier test sound happened to be small enough to fully buffer before any save could race it; switching to a larger baked clip reproduced it deterministically. **Bug 1 (lockout)**: a standalone Node repro script (`fs.createReadStream` held open on a file, then `fs.renameSync` over it) confirmed `fs.renameSync` throws `EPERM` on Windows when the destination file has an open read handle — exactly what `BakedClipPreview.js`'s `<audio>` element holds via the `sound://<id>?variant=clip` protocol while "Saved audio" is playing. `loopClip.js`'s `renderCrossfadedLoop` renders to a `.tmp` file then renames it over the live clip path unconditionally — while that file was mid-stream, the rename threw, `doRender` caught it and returned `{ok:false}`, and `performSave` treated a fully-successful render as a failed one: clearing `loopClipReady`, which `updatePreviewModeToggle()` reads as `savedPreviewEligible() === false`, force-reverting `previewMode` to `'live'` and disabling the toggle — precisely the reported symptom. The lock is transient (an open Range read closes once that chunk finishes, confirmed by testing both a single in-flight read and continuous back-to-back reads simulating real looping playback — the latter needed up to ~20 retries/1.8s in the adversarial case), so the fix is a new `renameWithRetry()` helper (up to 30 attempts/100ms apart, only retrying `EPERM`/`EBUSY`) wrapping that one rename call — zero cost in the common case (succeeds on attempt 1), reliably clears the lock in the one that matters. **Bug 2 (spectrogram)**: `BakedClipPreview.js` never had an `AnalyserNode` at all (only `PreviewSource.js`, the 'live' engine, did), and `index.js`'s `tickPreviewPlayhead()` only called `tickSpectrum()` inside the 'live' branch — so the EQ graph's spectrum backdrop had nothing to draw from in 'saved' mode, full stop. Fixed by giving `BakedClipPreview` its own analyser tap (a fixed-gain feed off `sourceNode`, same "decoupled from anything downstream" shape every other analyser tap in this app already uses — here there's no separate pre/post-EQ distinction to preserve, since the clip already has every filter baked in) and making `tickPreviewPlayhead()` call `tickSpectrum()` unconditionally rather than only on the 'live' branch. **Verified live via CDP against a real dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + a temp `window.__editorPluginDebug` hook, both removed after): reproduced the original bug on a real baked clip (render failed, `previewMode` reverted to `'live'`, toggle disabled) before the fix; after the fix, the identical sequence — tweak a filter while "Saved audio" is actively playing, Save — left `previewMode` on `'saved'`, `loopClipReady` true, the toggle enabled, and playback uninterrupted; repeated 5 rapid saves in a row while continuously playing, all 5 succeeded. Confirmed the analyser fix separately: real non-zero frequency data read directly off `BakedClipPreview`'s new `analyserNode` while playing, and the actual EQ canvas showed real drawn spectrum content (thousands of non-transparent pixels) in Saved mode — previously zero, since nothing fed it. Live mode's own spectrum re-confirmed unaffected by the refactor. `npm run build` clean; both changed plugin files and `loopClip.js` re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check.

**Export: the on-screen log now also saves to a file (v0.1.154)** — direct follow-up after the owner re-ran the "Forest at night" export to check the v0.1.136 speed fixes: "Man i did it but i dozed off and didn't get the log. Make the text on the log box be exported to the final folder and i'll test it later for you." The on-screen per-step log (real wall-clock timestamps + per-phase timing, v0.1.150) only ever existed in the DOM — gone the moment the tab or app closed, so a long unattended export's own diagnostic trail was unrecoverable exactly when it mattered most. Same shape as `writeInfoFile` (v0.1.140): the renderer already has the full log text sitting in `this.els.log`'s child lines (nothing new to compute), so `plugins/export/index.js`'s `runExport()` just joins them and hands the string to a new, minimal `export:writeLogFile` IPC handler (`ipc.js`, byte-for-byte the same shape as `writeInfoFile` — writes into the same per-preset subfolder `outputPath` already lives in) — the smallest main-process step that can actually write to disk, given the renderer has no direct fs access. Written unconditionally in the `finally` block (both success and failure) rather than gated on any toggle, since `exportMix()` already creates the destination subfolder as the literal first thing it does, before any real render work that could fail — there's always somewhere to put it by the time `runExport()` reaches `finally`. Deliberately silent/best-effort (a caught, logged error, no change to the status line) — this is bookkeeping alongside the real result, not a feature worth cluttering the success/failure message over. **Verified**: the exact write-to-disk logic (`path.join(path.dirname(outputPath), 'export-log.txt')` + `fs.writeFileSync`) tested in isolation, confirming the file lands in the right subfolder alongside the audio/video. `npm run build` clean; all three changed files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not verified via CDP** — `runExport()`'s own destination picker is a real native folder dialog that this project has twice before had auto-resolve for real under CDP and write a genuine file to the owner's actual Desktop (see the `parallelMixdown`/v0.1.128 and `feedback_contextbridge_frozen_override` history) — the change here is a small, additive `finally`-block write mirroring already-proven code, so it rests on the isolated-logic test plus code-review confidence instead.

**Self-review pass over v0.1.150-154, two real fixes (v0.1.155)** — the Board's `#next` queue was empty again (the export mixdown-phase optimization stays gated on the owner's next real slow-export log — now that v0.1.154 saves that log to a file, this should actually land next time; the Remix-tab UI stays paused pending the owner's three open design questions), so per this project's own established "dry backlog" precedent ran a `/code-review` over the diff since the last such pass (`d1297c7..HEAD`, the five releases from the export progress-budget fix through the export-log-file feature). Two real, independently-corroborated findings, both in the "Loop a video file"/"Loop an image" export-background dispatcher added across v0.1.141/v0.1.152: (1) `exportMix.js`'s `renderVideoOutput` — by design, per its own doc comment — falls back to a plain black screen whenever `videoBackground` is `'file'`/`'image'` but nothing was actually picked yet, meant as a safety net for a stale path, but nothing in the Export tab's UI ever stopped a real user from reaching that state by simply picking the dropdown option and never clicking "Choose…" before hitting Run — a real, reachable silent-wrong-output trap this project has otherwise been careful to avoid (Remix's Save status line, the video/info-file error lines already in this same tab exist for exactly this reason). Fixed with a pre-flight guard in `plugins/export/index.js`'s `runExport()`, alongside the existing preset/duration checks: picking a video/image background with no path chosen now blocks with a clear status message instead of running. (2) `videoNeedsRealEncode`'s progress-budget check tested only `videoBackground`'s *type*, not whether a path was actually present — so the same no-path-chosen case budgeted a full `durationSeconds` of "real encode" work that the actual (fallback, near-instant) render never completes, permanently capping the reporter's fraction below 1.0 for the rest of that export (invisible in the Export tab only because it hides the progress bar the instant the promise resolves, not because it actually reached 100% — the exact same class of bug v0.1.150 fixed for the two real-encode paths). Fixed by mirroring `renderVideoOutput`'s own dispatch condition exactly rather than approximating it a second time. **Verified**: the two boolean conditions (dispatch vs. budget) checked for exact agreement across every `{videoBackground, loopVideoPath, imagePath}` combination - zero mismatches after the fix (previously two: `'file'`/`'image'` with a null path each disagreed). `npm run build` clean; both changed files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not verified via CDP** — same reasoning as several recent Export-tab entries: the new guard is a plain status-message early-return following the exact shape of the existing preset/duration checks two lines above it, and the risk here was in the *logic* (confirmed via the isolated boolean-matrix test), not in any DOM wiring.

**BUG FIX: a real export failure diagnosed from the new export-log feature — disk-full error message + a leftover corrupt output file (v0.1.156)** — the owner pasted a real export failure straight from the new v0.1.154 log feature (the very first real payoff of shipping it): `✗ Export failed after 0:24:01: ffmpeg exited with code 4294967268: <a multi-KB wall of raw ffmpeg progress/stderr text>`. Diagnosed directly from that pasted text rather than guessed at: `4294967268` is a Windows unsigned-wraparound rendering of ffmpeg's own exit code (`2^32 - 28`) and means nothing on its own, but the stderr tail already contained the real, plain answer — `No space left on device`, repeated across the muxer/trailer-write errors right as a ~1h22m/849MB WAV was still growing during the final mixdown step. Not a Noctívago crash — a real full disk — but investigating it surfaced two genuine, fixable gaps in how that failure was handled. **(1) Unreadable error message**: `runFfmpeg.js`'s `runFfmpegToFile`/`runFfmpegPipe` both threw a plain `ffmpeg exited with code ${code}: ${stderrTail}` on any non-zero exit — technically complete but exactly the wall of text the owner had to paste back for anyone to make sense of, with the actual cause buried inside it. Added a small `FRIENDLY_FFMPEG_ERRORS` table (currently just the disk-space signature) checked against the stderr tail; a match prepends one plain sentence ("Not enough free disk space to finish writing the file.") ahead of the exact same full technical detail — nothing hidden, just no longer the *only* thing shown first. Centralized in `runFfmpeg.js` rather than only in `exportMix.js`, since any ffmpeg call in the app (loop-clip bakes, waveform analysis, reverb, Composite bakes) can hit the same disk-full condition, not just a long export's own final mixdown. **(2) A failed export could leave a large corrupt file behind**: `renderFinalMixdown`/`renderFinalMixFromTracks` (`exportMix.js`) write the final encode straight to `outputPath` — unlike `loopClip.js`'s own renders, there's no tmp-then-rename step — so an ffmpeg death mid-write (this exact ENOSPC case, but any mid-write failure) left a large, corrupted, partially-written file sitting right at the destination the owner picked. `exportMix()`'s top-level `catch` already returns `{ok:false, error}` but never touched `outputPath`; the existing `finally` block's `cleanupFiles()` only covers the batch/tile/shot *intermediates*, not the final output itself. Fixed by deleting `outputPath` (if it exists) in the `catch` block, best-effort, before returning the error — doubly relevant here since leaving a giant partial file around after an out-of-disk-space failure actively works against the owner's own next step (freeing up space and retrying). **Verified**: the friendly-message lookup tested directly against the owner's own real stderr text (correctly prepends the plain sentence, full detail intact) and against an unrelated ffmpeg failure (confirmed unchanged, no false-positive rewrite); the partial-output cleanup tested in isolation (a simulated partial file at the destination path is removed, no error). `npm run build` clean; both changed files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not independently verified via a live disk-full repro** (deliberately not filling a real disk to test this) - both fixes are small, isolated, and match the owner's own pasted failure shape exactly; the owner's actual root cause (the disk itself) still needs their own attention — freeing space and/or turning off "Parallel mixdown" (which tiles each loop sound to its own *full-duration* temp file, a real extra disk-space cost this export's log confirms was active: "tiling 6 loop sounds at once") was suggested directly in chat, not fixed in code, since it's already off by default and an opt-in tradeoff the owner chose for this run.

**Export: info.txt's AI prompt now asks for hashtags inside the description too (v0.1.157)** — direct follow-up on the v0.1.151 prompt: "change the text of the prompt to this so the description already includes the hashtags." The prompt's item (2) (the description) now explicitly asks for it to end with its own 3-5 hashtags, alongside the pre-existing separate item (4) asking for 3-5 hashtags on their own — the owner's exact wording, applied verbatim in `buildInfoFileText()` (`plugins/export/index.js`). `npm run build` clean; re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. Plain text-only change, no logic touched.

**Export: info.txt's AI prompt no longer asks for hashtags twice (v0.1.158)** — direct follow-up right after v0.1.157 shipped: flagged that the new wording asked for hashtags in both the description (item 2) and as a separate item (4); owner's answer was "just on the place where the description goes." Removed item (4) entirely and renumbered - the prompt now has three items instead of four, hashtags requested exactly once, inside the description. `npm run build` clean; re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check.

**Two bug fixes, both reported directly and both flagged "fix ASAP" on the Board (v0.1.159)**

**(1) BUG FIX: a Sound Group's own EQ/filters had zero effect on an export.** A Sound Group's highpass/lowpass/gain/parametric EQ (v0.1.143) always sounded right live (`SoundGroupChain.js`) but `exportMix.js` never knew groups existed at all — only a preset's own whole-mix `wholeMix` was ever inherited into an export (v0.1.129, which predates Sound Groups). A grouped sound exported exactly as if it had never been grouped. Fixed across three files: `plugins/export/index.js`'s `runExport()` now builds a `groupIdBySoundId` map off `preset.groups` and tags each exported sound with its `groupId`, plus sends `groups: preset.groups.map(g => ({id, filters}))` alongside the existing `wholeMixFilters`; `ipc.js`'s `export:run` handler forwards `groups` straight through (each sound's `groupId` already survives the handler's `...s` spread untouched). `exportMix.js` gained the real work: `buildGroupFilterChain()` (highpass → lowpass → EQ bands → gain → the same always-on brick-wall limiter `SoundGroupChain` always has, reusing `loopClip.js`'s `buildEqBandFilter`/`eqBandStageCount`) and `renderGroupBuses()`, a new phase inserted between "build every loop shot/batch track" and the existing budget/`reduceTrackCount` step. It partitions every loop shot and batch track by `groupId`, and for each group actually in use: tiles any grouped loop-mode sound to full duration first (`tileLoopShotFull`, needed regardless of the `parallelMixdown` toggle — a group submix needs real audio to combine, not a `-stream_loop` shorthand), bounds the member count under `MAX_INPUTS_PER_PASS` via the existing `reduceTrackCount` if needed, then **always** runs the result through `combineTracks` (even a single already-combined member) since `combineTracks` is what actually applies each track's own `adelay` and normalizes to offset 0 — skipping it for a lone non-zero-offset batch track would have silently played that group's audio from the wrong position. The combined bus then gets the group's own filter chain applied via one small `-af` pass, producing one more ordinary pre-rendered track (offset 0) that drops straight into the existing final-mixdown pipeline exactly like any other track — no changes needed to `renderFinalMixdown`/`renderFinalMixFromTracks` themselves. Zero groups in use (the overwhelmingly common case) is a verified no-op: no ffmpeg call, byte-identical arrays returned. Progress budgeting extended with a rough estimate (`groupedLoopSoundCount + usedGroupCount * 2` durations) so the bar doesn't stall during this new phase — not exact (the `combineTracks` sub-pass isn't itself progress-ticked, mirroring how `reduceTrackCount`'s own combine passes never were either), but keeps this out of the "frozen at 100%" bug class v0.1.150 fixed. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness (electron stubbed via `module.register`, real `exportMix.js` unmodified): a loop-mode 6kHz tone in a group with `lowpassHz:1000` measured 32dB quieter than the same tone exported ungrouped (a real, large attenuation, not noise-floor rounding); a scatter-mode sound in a group with `gainDb:+10` measured exactly +10.0dB louder than its ungrouped control; a mixed export (one grouped + one ungrouped sound together) completed with the correct exact duration; an export with no `groups` param at all (backward compat) still ran correctly; zero leftover temp files across all cases. `npm run build` clean.

**(2) BUG FIX, reported as actively harmful: "every app restart/update resets volumes, which preset was loaded, and Sound Group EQ/filters... if the app autoupdates while i'm sleeping and there's thunder noises it goes REALLY LOUD AND SHARP. I woke up last night several times."** Root-caused to three separate, compounding gaps, none of them hard to find once looked for directly: (a) the master (global) volume slider (`#global-volume` in `index.html`) was **never persisted at all** — its HTML `value="50"` (unity gain) was the only thing every launch ever saw, regardless of what the owner had it turned down to; (b) `maybeResumeOnLaunch()` (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) resumed every previously-`included` sound directly via `startPlayback()`, entirely bypassing `loadPreset()` — so `state.activePresetId` stayed `null` and `state.groups` stayed `[]` on every launch, regardless of what preset had actually been loaded before closing; (c) as a direct consequence of (b), any Sound Group EQ/filters or whole-mix processing that had been taming a sound's volume/tone was silently absent the moment playback resumed — every sound played back through only its own raw per-sound settings, at whatever level that alone produces. Fixed with two new persisted settings (`settings.js`): `globalVolumePosition` (0-100, matches the slider's own `value` attribute; a new `setGlobalVolumePosition()` setter, clamped 0-100) and `lastActivePresetId` (a new `setLastActivePresetId()` setter) — plus matching `ipc.js` handlers and `preload/index.js` entries, mirroring every other settings getter/setter's own shape exactly. `tabs/mixer/index.js`: the global-volume `input` listener now debounce-persists its value the same way per-sound volume already does (`VOLUME_SAVE_DEBOUNCE_MS`); `loadPreset()`/`deletePreset()` now call `api.settings.setLastActivePresetId()` alongside every existing `state.activePresetId` assignment. New `restoreLastActivePreset(settings)`: looks up `settings.lastActivePresetId` in the current preset list and, if it still exists, installs `state.activePresetId`/`state.groups`/`engine.wholeMix`/`engine.setSoundGroups()` exactly like `loadPreset()` does — but without touching playback, since no sources exist yet at this point in startup (a source created later, whether by `maybeResumeOnLaunch` or the owner pressing Play by hand, picks up the correct routing automatically via `getOrCreateSource`'s own already-idempotent `routeSound` call). Clears the setting if the remembered preset was deleted since. `mount()` now does one `api.settings.get()` up front, shared by all three startup steps in careful order: apply the restored volume immediately (before anything can possibly play) → `refreshList()` → `restoreLastActivePreset(settings)` → `maybeResumeOnLaunch(settings)` (now takes `settings` as a param instead of re-fetching, guaranteeing it can't run before the preset/group state it depends on is installed). **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` env-gated remote-debugging switch in `main/index.js` + a temp `window.__mixerDebug` hook exposing `state`/`engine`/`els`/`loadPreset`, both removed after, per [[feedback_contextbridge_frozen_override]]'s own established pattern): dragged the master volume slider to 20 — persisted to settings within the debounce window, `masterGain.gain.value` measured the exact expected `0.4` from `positionToGain`; created a real preset with a real Sound Group (`highpassHz:300`) via the real `presets.save`/`updateGroups` IPC calls, loaded it via the real `loadPreset()` — confirmed `engine.groupChains.size` became 1 and `lastActivePresetId` persisted; **reloaded the page (simulating a fresh launch) without ever calling `loadPreset()` again** — confirmed the volume slider and `masterGain` came back correctly on their own, `state.activePresetId`/`state.groups` were restored automatically, and the group's real live `BiquadFilterNode.highpass.frequency.value` measured exactly `300` — genuine end-to-end proof the restore path (not just the settings round-trip) works. Test preset deleted and dev settings reset back to defaults afterward; dev Electron process stopped by its own specific PID, not a broad name filter (per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]). `npm run build` clean.

**Preset/Group filters gain Echo (v0.1.160)** — the first slice of the owner's own 2026-09-06 direction on the Remix-tab merge ("Echo delay is NOT single-sound-specific... so this also means extending that data model to carry Echo... alongside HP/LP/Gain"). Scoped deliberately to Echo only this pass, with Reverb flagged as a follow-up rather than silently dropped: Echo is a plain `-af aecho=...` fragment that slots directly into the existing linear whole-mix/group ffmpeg post-chains, where Reverb (`afir` convolution) needs a genuinely separate multi-input ffmpeg pass (a second IR-file input) that doesn't fit the current single-pass-per-track shape `renderGroupBuses`/`buildWholeMixPostChain` already use — a bigger, separate lift, not a quick add. Threaded through every layer that already carries HP/LP/Gain, mirroring the exact same shape a sound's own `filters.echoDelayMs`/`echoDecay` already has: **data model** (`presets.js`'s `normalizeWholeMix`/`normalizeGroupFilters` gain `echoDelayMs`/`echoDecay`, clamped 0-1500/0-0.85 same as the per-sound Remix sliders, folded into `normalizeWholeMix`'s own neutral-collapses-to-null check); **live audio** (`WholeMixChain.js`/`SoundGroupChain.js` each gain an `echoDelay`/`echoSendGain`/`echoFeedbackGain` node trio, wired as a parallel feedback-delay tap off the post-EQ/gain `gain` node summing back in before `fadeGain`/`limiter` — the exact same shape `SoundSource.js`'s own per-sound echo tap already uses, just relocated to the whole-mix/group bus); **export baking** (`loopClip.js`'s `buildEchoFilter` — previously module-private — now `export`ed alongside `buildEqBandFilter`'s own already-established export precedent, reused by `exportMix.js`'s `buildWholeMixPostChain` and `buildGroupFilterChain`, guarded by the same `echoDelayMs > 0 && echoDecay > 0` check the per-sound bake uses, ordered after EQ/gain to match the live signal order); **Remix UI** (`plugins/editor/index.js` — new Echo delay/decay sliders in both the Preset and Group mode filter panels, `NEUTRAL_WHOLE_MIX`/`NEUTRAL_GROUP_FILTERS` and the shared `canonicalMixFilters` dirty-check gained the two new fields so `cloneMixFilters`'s existing spread-based cloning and the Save button's dirty state pick them up automatically with no separate per-field editing needed there). **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness (electron stubbed via `module.register`): a synthetic impulse (100ms tone + true digital silence via `apad`) exported with whole-mix echo (`echoDelayMs:300, echoDecay:0.6`) measured a real +2.00dB higher whole-file mean level than the same export with echo off — and the group-echo path measured the *exact* identical +2.00dB delta (expected, since both paths share the same `buildEchoFilter` helper); a plain export with no echo fields at all still ran correctly (backward compat); zero leftover temp files. First attempt at this test used a loop-mode sound and a windowed volumedetect seek, which produced misleading numbers twice in a row (once from a `volume=enable=...` gate that didn't actually reach silence, once from loop-mode's own self-crossfade rotation relocating the test tone's position) - switched to a scatter-mode one-shot event and a whole-file energy comparison instead, which sidesteps both issues entirely; a real reminder that the bug was in the test twice, not the app, before landing on a reliable check. **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + a temp `window.__mixerDebug` hook, both removed after): dragging both Preset-mode and Group-mode Echo sliders live-updated the correct `WholeMixChain`/`SoundGroupChain` node values exactly - but only once a real signal was actually flowing through the graph; a first attempt with nothing genuinely playing (this dev profile's own test presets have only missing/stub sound files) showed `echoSendGain` stuck at its stale default even under a direct manual `setValueAtTime` call, while `echoFeedbackGain` in the very same chain read correctly - a CDP-testing artifact (an AudioParam's `.value` only reflects automation once the render graph is actually processing quanta), not a real bug, matching this project's own documented v0.1.122 "reading too early it shows the pre-ramp value" lesson; confirmed by patching in a throwaway near-silent test oscillator to force real continuous signal flow, after which both nodes read correctly and consistently. Saving both a preset's whole-mix Echo and a group's own Echo persisted correctly via the real `presets.updateWholeMix`/`updateGroups` IPC calls; reloading the page (simulating a fresh launch, exercising the v0.1.159 `restoreLastActivePreset` path from the same session) restored both the whole-mix and the group's Echo settings automatically with no manual reload of either - the live `BiquadFilterNode`/delay values matched the persisted numbers exactly. Test group and whole-mix settings cleaned up from the dev preset afterward; dev Electron process stopped by its own specific PID, not a broad name filter (per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]). `npm run build` clean; the touched plugin file re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not done in this pass, left for the owner's own follow-up sessions per their explicit allowance** ("do it over the sessions"): sharing one EQ editor module across Sound/Preset/Group modes (`WholeMixEqEditor.js` stays a separate ~280-line implementation from the real `EqEditor.js`), restyling Preset/Group to visually match Sound mode's page, and Reverb in whole-mix/group filters.

**Preset/Group mode share Sound mode's full EQ module + gain Reverb, effect Presets, and Reset filters (v0.1.161)** — direct answer to the question left open in the v0.1.160 entry above (whether `EqEditor.js`'s A/B compare, per-band mute/solo, and cut-overlay should carry over to Preset/Group mode): **"Yes it should carry over... Everything else should be kept. The parametric eq module should be the same across every mode. Also, the sliders should also be kept. (hp, lp, gain, echo, reverb etc... Also, the Presets: Muffled, phone, radio, distant, etc... There reset filters also."** Closes out the rest of the 2026-09-06 Remix-tab merge direction in one pass.

**EQ module sharing**: `createEqEditorController(canvas)` (`EqEditor.js`) turned out to already be a fully generic, canvas-scoped factory with zero Sound-mode-specific dependencies (no reference to `PreviewSource` or any single-sound state) - instantiating a second and third one for `this.presetEq`/`this.groupEq` was exactly as simple as Sound mode's own setup, once the missing UI chrome existed. `WholeMixEqEditor.js` (the old ~280-line scoped-down copy - no A/B compare, no mute/solo, no slope, a narrower ±18dB range, and an older "click-empty-canvas-to-add" interaction Sound mode itself moved away from) is deleted outright. Preset/Group mode's EQ panels gained the same markup Sound mode's own has: A/B compare buttons, +/trash icon buttons, a "Reset EQ" button, a revert banner, a Slope `<select>`, and Mute/Solo toggle buttons - all wired through new `applyPresetEqControls`/`applyGroupEqControls` (mirroring Sound mode's `applyEqControls` exactly) plus `updatePreset/GroupEqFieldsFromSelection`, `switchPreset/GroupEqCompareView`, `revertPreset/GroupEqToSaved`, and `resetPreset/GroupEqCompare` - four new parallel-but-separate methods per mode (kept as the same twin-copy style the rest of Preset/Group code already uses, not a bigger generic-target refactor - CLAUDE.md already flags that as separate, riskier follow-up work, not something to fold into this pass). One real semantic difference from Sound mode, deliberate not accidental: Sound mode's A/B "B" slot (`savedEqSnapshot`) is captured once when a sound is opened and never updated again that session, even across an in-session Save (a documented, existing quirk); Preset/Group's own "B" (`wholeMixSaved.eq`/`groupSaved.eq`) *does* update after every real Save, because that's what those two variables already meant for the rest of Preset/Group mode's own pre-existing dirty-check before this change - reusing them as-is rather than introducing a second, Sound-mode-style "frozen" snapshot were the two real design options weighed, in favor of the option that didn't touch already-working code for other fields (a genuine cut, but a real gain/-24 range now applies everywhere `NEUTRAL_FILTERS`/`FILTER_PRESETS` already share, so nothing was leftover-".18" special-cased).

**Reverb** (the piece explicitly deferred at v0.1.160, since `afir` convolution can't join the plain comma `-af` chain Echo uses): **data model** (`presets.js` - `reverbSizeMs`/`reverbMix`, clamped 0-4000/0-1, folded into `normalizeWholeMix`'s neutral check); **live audio** (`WholeMixChain.js`/`SoundGroupChain.js` each gain a `reverbSendGain`/`reverbConvolver` pair - a second parallel tap off the post-EQ/gain `gain` node, independent of the Echo tap, mirroring `SoundSource.js`'s own per-sound reverb exactly; `reverbConvolver.buffer` only regenerated via `createReverbImpulse` when `reverbSizeMs` actually changes, not on every gain tweak); **export baking**, the genuinely new engineering: `loopClip.js`'s `applyReverbPass` (previously private, used only by per-sound bakes) is now `export`ed and reused directly. `buildWholeMixPostChain` gained a `deferReverbAndTail` option that stops right after Echo (no limiter, no fades) when Reverb is active, and a new shared `finalizeWholeMix()` helper (extracted from what were two near-identical tails in `renderFinalMixdown`/`renderFinalMixFromTracks` - a deduplication that also closed a real duplication these two functions already had before Reverb touched them at all) dispatches to either the original single ffmpeg-pass-to-final-format flow (Reverb off - **byte-path unchanged**, the same "blessed export loudness" guarantee every whole-mix addition here keeps) or, when Reverb is on: main graph → WAV intermediate (pre-limiter/pre-fade) → `applyReverbPass` (which bakes its own limiter) → one small final pass for fades + the real format encode. `buildGroupFilterChain` gained the matching `skipLimiter` option and `applyGroupFilters` the same two-pass split, self-contained within the group's own bus (already isolated per-group intermediates since the v0.1.159 Sound-Group-export fix, so no changes needed to the outer mixdown at all). **A real bug caught by the verification harness, not by review**: a group with *only* Reverb set (no HP/LP/EQ/Gain/Echo) produced a completely empty pre-reverb filter chain once the limiter was skipped, and ffmpeg rejects `-af ''` outright ("No filters specified in the graph description") - fixed by omitting the `-af` flag entirely when the chain array is empty, rather than assuming there's always at least the limiter fragment to fall back on. **Verified against the real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness: whole-mix Reverb on a scatter-mode impulse measured +22.4dB louder than the same export with Reverb off (a real convolution tail adds real energy); the identical test for group-only Reverb measured +16.4dB; Reverb+Echo together in the same whole-mix export completed correctly (confirms the two independent taps don't conflict when both active); duration stayed exact (~3s) even with fades and the extra reverb pass; a plain export with neither Echo nor Reverb still worked correctly across both WAV and Opus formats (backward compat, unaffected by any of this restructuring) - 14/14 checks passed after the empty-chain fix. **Verified live via CDP** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `cdp-eval.mjs` helper, both removed after): drove the real Remix UI end-to-end - added an EQ band via the new "+" button, edited it via the precision fields, confirmed Mute/Solo toggle their own active classes correctly; confirmed the A/B compare banner shows/hides correctly and switching to B disables every field (read-only) while switching back to A restores the exact in-progress edit (the "stash" mechanism); the "Reverb" one-click effect preset correctly set `reverbSizeMs:1500`/`reverbMix:0.35` (`FILTER_PRESETS.reverb`'s own values); "Reset filters" correctly zeroed every slider and cleared the EQ selection back to none. Created a real group, added an EQ band, set Reverb, hit Save - confirmed the exact persisted shape via `presets.list()`; did the same for the preset's own whole-mix EQ/Reverb; **reloaded the page (simulating a fresh launch) and reselected the same preset** - the Reverb sliders and their formatted labels (`"2.0s"`) came back exactly matching what was saved, with no manual reload of anything else needed - confirming the whole pipeline (UI → IPC → store → UI-on-reload) works end-to-end, not just the individual pieces. Cross-checked all 79 `container.querySelector('#editor-mix-...')` references in the file against the markup's own `id=` attributes programmatically (a plain Node script, not manual inspection) - zero mismatches, the exact bug class that silently killed the whole Export tab in the v0.1.130 incident. Test group/preset data cleaned up afterward; dev Electron process stopped by its own specific PID, not a broad name filter (per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]). `npm run build` clean; the touched plugin file re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check (per [[feedback_nodecheck_unreliable_for_plugin_templates]] - this build doesn't bundle `plugins/`, so a clean `npm run build` alone never would have caught a syntax break here). Also fixed in passing: `.editor-mix-eq-canvas`'s CSS height (160px, a `WholeMixEqEditor.js`-era leftover) now matches Sound mode's own 140px, since both panels draw through the identical module. **Not done, and not asked for this round**: restyling Preset/Group's overall page layout to visually match Sound mode beyond what sharing the same EQ module and filter panel already achieves - the CSS these panels already used (`.editor-filters`, `.editor-eq`, `.editor-actions`) was already shared with Sound mode before this change, so most of the remaining visual gap was closed as a side effect rather than a deliberate separate restyle pass.

**BUG FIX: a Sound Group's real members could be invisible in the Remix tab's own Group mode (v0.1.162)** — reported directly: "Group audios aren't updating on the group mode on the remix tab. If you create a group but add audio files later to the preset and use the right click menu on them to add them to a group, they won't show up on the group mode on the remix tab. What's odd is that they are in a group because the EQ is applied to them and the group tells you that there's 4 audio files on it, they just don't show up on the interface completely." Root-caused directly from the description (matched a real gap found reading `renderGroupMembers()`, not guessed): the Mixer's right-click "add to group"/"new group with this sound" menu (`toggleGroupMembership` in `tabs/mixer/index.js`) writes straight into `group.soundIds` for whatever sound you right-click, with no requirement that the sound already be saved into the active preset's own `sounds` array — a sound merely *included in the live mix* (routes/EQs correctly the moment it's grouped, since `AudioEngine.routeSound` only ever reads `group.soundIds`) can be grouped without ever having gone through the separate, deliberate "Save changes to `<preset>`" step (v0.1.131) that actually persists it into `preset.sounds`. `renderGroupMembers()` (Remix tab, Group mode's member checklist) only ever iterated `preset.sounds`, so a member added that way was a real member (the group's own count already reflected it) but silently absent from this one checklist — exactly the reported symptom. Fixed by iterating the union of `preset.sounds` and `group.soundIds` (deduped) instead of `preset.sounds` alone, falling back to the raw id (same as the pre-existing missing-library-entry fallback) for a member with no matching library sound. A rendering-only fix, not a write-path restriction — deliberately: the live-mix-can-be-grouped-before-saving behavior itself is consistent with how "Include" already works elsewhere in this app (playback/routing reflects live state; saving is a separate, later persistence step), so the checklist now just tells the truth about it rather than the write path being changed to disallow it. **Verified**: `node --check` against a `.mjs`-forced copy (plain `.js` doesn't reliably catch a syntax break in this plugin file, per the v0.1.130 lesson) and `npm run build` clean. **Not verified live via CDP this pass** (no dev app session running in this environment) — the change is a small, isolated loop-source swap in an already-proven rendering function, and the logic was traced end-to-end against the real current source on both the write side (`tabs/mixer/index.js`) and the read side (this function) before fixing, not assumed from the bug report alone.

**BUG FIX: leftover export temp files never got cleaned up after an interrupted export (v0.1.162)** — reported directly ("Temporary export files are taking up some space on my machine"). Every write path into `export-tmp` (`exportMix.js`'s own `cleanupFiles` calls at each phase, plus `ipc.js`'s Composite bake) already removes its own intermediates via try/finally on every controlled exit — but none of that runs if the whole Electron process dies mid-export instead of unwinding normally (a force-quit, a crash, Windows forcing a restart, a laptop losing power) — an ordinary way for a multi-hour export's intermediates (per-batch tracks, full-duration tiled loop renders, group buses, potentially sizeable) to survive past the run that created them with nothing left to ever sweep them. Fixed the same way `presetPortable.js`'s own `sweepImportSessions()` already handles the identical class of problem for import sessions: a new `exportMix.js` export, `sweepExportTempDir()` (`fs.rmSync(exportTempDir(), {recursive:true,force:true})`), called from `main/index.js`'s startup sequence right alongside `sweepImportSessions()`. Safe unconditionally — no export or Composite bake can span an app restart, so anything still in `export-tmp` at the *next* launch is guaranteed orphaned; both write paths already `mkdirSync` the directory on demand before writing, so wiping it at startup doesn't require any other change. **Verified**: a Node harness (electron's `app.getPath` stubbed via `module.register`) confirmed the function removes a populated `export-tmp` directory and is a silent no-op when the directory doesn't exist (the common case — most launches never had an interrupted export). `npm run build` clean.

**Remix EQ graph: zoom in/out + an Invert button (v0.1.163)** — both Board items were owner-gated (mystery-sound listening test, next real slow-export log) with nothing pickable, so per the project's own "dry backlog" precedent, picked up the next `#planned` backlog item instead: two small, cheap additions ported directly from Audacity's Filter Curve EQ (`FilterCurveEqView.qml`), per the 2026-09-06 Audacity research pass. Since `EqEditor.js`'s `createEqEditorController(canvas)` is the one shared module across all three Remix modes (Sound/Preset/Group, since v0.1.161), both features apply everywhere at once with a single implementation. **Zoom**: the graph's visible frequency window (`viewMinHz`/`viewMaxHz`, defaulting to the full 20–20000Hz span) is now per-controller-instance state instead of the fixed `FREQ_MIN`/`FREQ_MAX` module constants baked into `freqToX`/`xToFreq` — both now take the view range as optional params (default to the full span, so every existing call site not touched by this feature keeps working unchanged). A zoom step scales the *log-frequency* span by a fixed factor (0.6 in/1.67 out) around a center frequency, clamped at 1 octave (narrow end) and the full range (wide end); if the requested window would spill past either edge of the full range, it's shifted — not just clipped — to stay the same width and land fully inside, so zooming in near 20Hz or 20kHz doesn't produce a lopsided, narrower-than-intended window. New Zoom In/Out buttons in each mode's EQ header (centered on the current view); mouse wheel over any empty part of the graph (away from a node, which still adjusts that node's Q as before) zooms centered on the cursor's frequency instead — the same "wheel-zoom centered on cursor" convention `LoopEditor.js`'s own waveform already established, so the plugin's two canvases behave consistently. The vertical frequency gridlines/labels, previously a fixed `[100, 1000, 10000]` set, are now computed per-frame from whatever's actually visible (`niceFreqTicks`) so a tightly zoomed-in view still shows readable ticks instead of potentially zero. Zoom resets to the full span on every `load()` (a fresh sound/preset/group, or Reset EQ) — a zoomed-in view from whatever was being edited before has no reason to carry over. **Invert**: a new button flips every band's `gainDb` in place (freq/Q/type untouched) — mirrors Audacity's own Filter Curve EQ "Invert", useful for turning a curve that boosts a problem range into one that cuts it without re-dragging every node. **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch in `main/index.js` + a temp debug hook exposing the plugin instance, both removed after — hit the project's own documented "plugin file edits need a full process restart, not just a page reload" lesson twice while wiring this up, both times resolved by killing the dev Electron process by its own specific PID per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]] and relaunching): confirmed repeated Zoom In clicks narrow the view by the expected factor each time (5.98 → 3.59 → 2.15 octaves) down to the 1-octave floor, and repeated Zoom Out clicks widen back to exactly the full 20–19999.99Hz span; confirmed wheel-zoom over empty space near the graph's left edge shifts (not just clips) to stay in bounds; confirmed wheel-over-a-node still adjusts Q and never triggers zoom; confirmed Invert flips a real two-band arrangement (`[5, -8] → [-5, 8]`) in both Sound and Preset mode via their own real buttons; confirmed Group mode's buttons are wired without error (no group was loaded in this pass, so no further behavioral check there, but the code path is identical to the two already-verified modes). `npm run build` clean; both changed files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check.

**Remix: Fluctuation — slow volume/pitch drift on a looping sound (v0.1.164)** — first slice of a new inbox item ("There should be volume and pitch fluctuation... controls for the volume to gradually change to give the impression that something is coming closer or moving further randomly or even getting stronger or weaker, rain and wind could largely benefit... bars with 3 circles: highest point / lowest point / bias... controls for randomness [how frequently a value changes] and speed [how quickly the transition happens]"). Researched first per [[feedback_research_before_complex_tasks]] — this is the game-audio "random modulator" pattern (Wwise LFO ShareSets, FMOD random modulation, Bitwig Random LFO): a smoothed, bounded random signal driving a parameter. Scoped to **Sound mode, loop playMode, volume + pitch, live + preview + persist** this pass, per the owner's own AskUserQuestion answer ("Sound mode first, attenuation-only volume"); Preset/Group modes, scatter/scheduled, and export baking are flagged follow-ups.

- **`src/renderer/audio/Modulator.js`** (new, + a bundled copy in `plugins/editor/audio/` per the plugin's can't-import-outside-its-dir rule): `Modulator` is a bounded random-walk generator — every `changeRate`-derived interval (jittered ±40%, mapped 0..1 → ~20s..1.5s) it picks a new target in `[min,max]` biased toward `bias` (`Math.pow(random, 1.7)` skew toward small deviations, plus side-choice weighted by which side has more room so an off-center bias still reaches both ends), and glides there with an exponential-approach time constant from `transition` (0..1 → ~9s..0.15s). Runs on `setInterval(60ms)` **not `requestAnimationFrame`** — deliberately: `backgroundThrottling` clamps a hidden window's `setInterval` to 1s but *freezes* rAF outright, and the dt-based approach math converges to the same place in coarse 1s hops (a minimized rain sound keeps gusting, just chunkily, instead of freezing at whatever attenuation it was last at). `SoundFluctuation` owns the volume+pitch `Modulator` pair for one playing sound/preview, with a `sync(playing)` that starts/stops each axis' modulator against actual playback + enabled state, and a `key` (stable JSON fingerprint) for the Mixer's reconcile staleness check.
- **Data model**: `entry.fluctuation = { volume: {enabled,min,max,bias,changeRate,transition}, pitch: {...} }` — its own top-level field + `library.updateFluctuation` IPC (not folded into `filters`), specifically so a fluctuation edit never marks the baked loop clip stale / triggers a re-bake. volume rides a 0..1 multiplier (attenuation only — a `fluctuationGain` node between the filter/echo/reverb chain and the per-sound volume `gainNode`, so it can't boost past the set level into clipping and composes cleanly with the play/pause fade + Sound Groups routing, both of which act on `gainNode`). pitch: `BufferSoundSource` uses the decoded buffer node's native `detune` (true independent shift, also gently wobbles loop speed — the intended tape-flutter feel); `LocalFileSoundSource`/`PreviewSource` have no detune on `<audio>` so pitch rides `playbackRate` (also nudges tempo, same documented trade-off Pitch itself already has there). Added to `IMPORTED_SETTINGS_KEYS` so portable presets carry it; `duplicateSound` carries it via its existing `...source` spread.
- **Live wiring**: `LocalFileSoundSource` + `BufferSoundSource` each gained `fluctuationGain` + a `SoundFluctuation` + `setFluctuation()`/`hasFluctuation()`; started in `play()`, stopped (and reset to neutral) in `pause()`/`dispose()`. Mixer passes `entry.fluctuation` into both constructors, and `reconcileSource` calls `source.setFluctuation(entry.fluctuation)` for any source kind that has the method (guarded — scatter/scheduled sources don't), *before* the kind-specific branches, since fluctuation applies regardless of stream/buffer. `bufferBakeSnapshot` deliberately does **not** include it (no needless re-bake). A new `noctivago:sound-fluctuation-changed` window-CustomEvent bridge lets a Remix edit update a sound already playing in the Mixer immediately (patch cached entry + `reconcileSource`), not only on the next tab-switch refresh — same pattern as the whole-mix/group preview bridges. Persistence/restore is automatic: `entry.fluctuation` is in the persisted library entry, so a resumed-on-launch sound gets it through `getOrCreateSource` with no v0.1.159-style special restore code.
- **Preview**: `PreviewSource.js` gained one shared `fluctuationGain` after both crossfade voices combine (so a loop-seam crossfade doesn't fight it) + its own `SoundFluctuation`; `_applyPlaybackRate()` now folds a `_pitchModFactor` into the per-voice `speed*pitchRatio`. The spectrum analyser still taps `lowpassNode` (pre-fluctuation), so the EQ backdrop stays a stable reference. "Saved audio" preview (`BakedClipPreview`) intentionally has no fluctuation — the baked clip genuinely contains none.
- **UI**: new `plugins/editor/ui/FluctuationBar.js` — the "bar with 3 circles" canvas control (min/bias/max handles, drag with `min ≤ bias ≤ max` enforced, double-click a handle to reset just it, `onChange` during+after drag; same `resizeCanvasForDisplay`-before-every-draw discipline as LoopEditor/EqEditor). New "Fluctuation" section in Sound mode's panel (two bars + Change rate/Transition sliders + enable checkboxes per axis), hidden for scatter/scheduled playMode (toggled in `setPlayModeControls`, which also redraws the bars when it un-hides them since a `display:none` canvas measures 0). **Deliberately NOT part of the trim/filter snapshot/undo/autosave/A-B-compare machinery** — it's a playback-time behavior, so it persists on its own via a debounced `library.updateFluctuation` (with a `flushFluctuation()` on `disposePreview` so a fast sound-switch can't drop a pending edit) and previews live with zero ffmpeg round-trip. Kept the editor-integration surface small by that choice — no `snapshotOf` signature change, no `applyEditorState` hookup.
- **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch in `main/index.js` + temp `window.__editorDebug`/`window.__mixerDebug` hooks, all removed after): all three source paths confirmed drifting under real playback — `PreviewSource` (Remix preview: `fluctuationGain.gain` 0.55→0.39 over ~10s, `playbackRate` 1.0→1.18→1.05), `BufferSoundSource` (Mixer, baked clip: gain 0.55→0.84, `sourceNode.detune` 208→283 cents), `LocalFileSoundSource` (Mixer, stream: gain moved 0.46, `playbackRate` 0.83→1.32 over 20 samples) — and cleanly resetting to neutral (gain→1, rate→1, detune→0) on pause. Persistence round-trip through the real electron-store confirmed; the live-reconcile event bridge confirmed to update a playing Mixer source's modulators (start/stop per `enabled`) **without swapping the source**. FluctuationBar drag confirmed to enforce `min ≤ bias ≤ max`, fire `onChange`, and reset the right handle on double-click. Section visibility confirmed correct across loop↔scatter↔scheduled. One CDP-testing artifact worth noting for next time: a mid-run test showed a stream source's modulators "barely moving" — the window had genuinely gone to background between commands so `setInterval` was clamped to 1s (the *designed* graceful degradation, not a bug); a clean re-test with the window active showed full drift. `npm run build` clean; all changed plugin files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check (per [[feedback_nodecheck_unreliable_for_plugin_templates]]).

**Top bar: an always-visible active-preset indicator (v0.1.165)** — first (small, clear) half of the owner's 2026-09-09 "presets as the primary context" pair of notes, built now that they confirmed placement: **option (a)**, "left of the global Play-all / master-volume toolbar". A new static `#active-preset-indicator` span in `index.html`, immediately after `#dev-badge` and before `#global-play-pause` — a bookmark glyph + name, styled as a dim `--text-dim` "No preset" pill until a preset is loaded, then an `--accent`-outlined pill with the name (`.has-preset` class). `max-width: 200px` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` so a long preset name can't reflow the wrapping toolbar; full name (`Editing preset "…"`) on hover via `title`. Wiring is entirely in `tabs/mixer/index.js`: a new `setActivePresetIndicator(name)` helper called from the four places `state.activePresetId` changes — `loadPreset` and `restoreLastActivePreset` (both have `preset.name` in hand and don't round-trip through `refreshPresetList`), `deletePreset`'s active-preset-cleared branch (→ `null`), and `updateActivePresetButtonState(presets)` (which already computes the `active` preset object off the fresh list on every `refreshPresetList`, so this also covers a rename or an external edit). The indicator tracks the **Mixer's** loaded preset specifically (the app-wide "which preset's Sound Groups / whole-mix processing is live" context, restored on launch by v0.1.159's `restoreLastActivePreset`), not the Remix tab's own Preset/Group-mode selector. The bigger half of the note (a per-preset override layer over per-sound trim/filters/EQ) stays on the Board as its own effort — the owner has now answered all four migration questions in the inbox, so it's ready to plan. `npm run build` clean. Not CDP-verified this pass — a static toolbar span plus a class-toggle/`textContent` helper fired from call sites that already reliably run; the logic was traced against current source at all four call sites.

**Top bar: the active-preset pill opens the Presets modal (v0.1.166)** — a one-line completion of v0.1.165: the indicator reads as a button, so clicking it now runs the same `openPresetsModal()` the toolbar's own Presets button does (extracted from that button's inline handler into a named function, wired to both). Works in the "No preset" state too (opens it to pick one). CSS gained `cursor: pointer` + an accent-border hover on `.active-preset-indicator`. Trivial, one file + one CSS rule; `npm run build` clean.

**Fluctuation → Preset mode + Group mode, volume only (v0.1.167)** — the first Fluctuation follow-up (v0.1.164 shipped Sound-mode volume+pitch drift). Threaded through every layer that already carries HP/LP/Gain/Echo/Reverb, exactly like the v0.1.160/v0.1.161 Echo/Reverb additions. **Pitch is deliberately not portable to a bus** — a whole-mix / group is a *sum* of independent sources, nothing to detune — so only the volume axis exists here; pitch fluctuation stays a per-sound thing. Not baked into an export yet (still a follow-up; the ffmpeg chain builders just ignore the extra field for now).
- **Data model** (`src/main/presets.js`): `normalizeMixFluctuation()` (new) — a `{ volume: { enabled, min, max, bias, changeRate, transition } }` shape clamped 0..1, ordering enforced (`min ≤ bias ≤ max`), **collapsed to `null` when disabled** so the "does this preset have whole-mix settings" truthiness checks stay simple; wired into both `normalizeWholeMix` (added to its neutral-collapses-to-null AND) and `normalizeGroupFilters`. Portable presets carry it automatically (it's a field of the `wholeMix` / group `filters` object, already serialized whole).
- **Live audio** (`WholeMixChain.js` / `SoundGroupChain.js`): each gained a `fluctGain` node + a `Modulator` (reused from `src/renderer/audio/Modulator.js`, the v0.1.164 engine) + a `setFluctuation()` called at the end of its own `set()` — so every existing call site (`engine.wholeMix.set()` from `loadPreset` / `restoreLastActivePreset` / the `whole-mix-preview` bridge; `chain.set()` from `setSoundGroups` / `previewGroup`) picks it up with **zero new wiring**. `fluctGain` sits after `fadeGain` (whole-mix) / after the dry+echo+reverb merge (group) and before the `limiter` — attenuation only (0..1), so it can never push into the limiter. `setFluctuation()` is idempotent (keyed on a stable fingerprint, so the every-preview-tick calls don't restart a running modulator; a genuine change reconfigures in place). The modulator runs whenever enabled regardless of play state (one `setInterval`; a paused mix just wobbles the gain on silence, and resuming picks up wherever the "gust" is — weather doesn't reset on unpause). `SoundGroupChain.dispose()` tears down the modulator.
- **UI** (`plugins/editor/index.js`): a shared `mixFluctuationMarkup(prefix)` helper renders one volume bar + Change rate / Transition sliders + an enable checkbox into both the Preset and Group filter panels (deliberately simpler than Sound mode's two-axis block). `createFluctuationBar` reused (the same `plugins/editor/ui/FluctuationBar.js` control, volume range 0..1). Shared `readMixFluctuation` / `writeMixFluctuation` / `updateMixFlucEnabledUI` helpers (not twin copies) drive both panels from `readPreset/GroupMixControlsAndPreview` and `writePreset/GroupControls`. `cloneMixFilters` deep-copies the `fluctuation` object (like it already does `eq`) so editing `wholeMixCurrent.fluctuation` never mutates the shared neutral/saved; `canonicalMixFilters` gained a `fluctuation` line so the Save-button dirty check sees it. A `display:none` canvas measures 0, so `setMode()` redraws the entering panel's bar (same fix Sound mode's own bars needed at v0.1.164).
- **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev app** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `window.__mixerDebug`/`__editorDebug` hooks, all removed after; dev electron killed by its own root PID per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]): `engine.wholeMix.fluctGain.gain` drifted a real 0.16 spread within `[0.25, 0.95]` under an enabled config and returned to exactly 1 on `set(null)`; a `SoundGroupChain`'s own `fluctGain` did the same and its modulator stopped + reset on a live `previewGroup({enabled:false})` and on `setSoundGroups([])` dispose; **20 rapid `set()` calls with an unchanged key left the `setInterval` timer id unchanged** (no leak) and a changed key reconfigured `bias` in place without a restart; the Preset-mode UI round-tripped end to end (enable → Save button goes dirty → `saveWholeMix()` → Save goes clean → `presets.list()` shows the exact persisted `{volume:{...}}`); loading that preset in the Mixer via `loadPreset()` started `engine.wholeMix._fluctMod` (real drift observed) and a live `whole-mix-preview` event with `enabled:false` stopped it; Group-mode UI round-tripped the same way (create group → enable → Save → persisted → delete cleanup). Data-model round-trip + dirty-check consistency also unit-tested in isolation (disabled → null; out-of-order min/max fixed; save-then-reselect stays clean). `npm run build` clean; `plugins/editor/index.js` re-checked as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check and every new `#editor-mix-…-fluc-…` id cross-checked against the markup programmatically (per [[feedback_nodecheck_unreliable_for_plugin_templates]] + the v0.1.130 lesson).
- **Still follow-ups**: baking fluctuation into an export (per-sample gain automation in the ffmpeg pipeline; philosophically bakes one random realization); allowing it for scatter/scheduled sounds.

**BUG FIX: Sound-mode Fluctuation looked like it neither worked nor saved (v0.1.168)** — reported directly ("Volume shift isn't working. It doesn't even allow me to save after modifying its settings"), tested by the owner in Sound mode. Root-caused via live CDP against a real dev build: the drift engine itself is **fine** — verified the modulator starts on play, the `fluctuationGain` genuinely drifts, it loops, persists, and survives a reload, in every enable/disable-mid-playback permutation. What was actually broken was that a Fluctuation edit had *zero* feedback: per its original v0.1.164 design it deliberately sits outside the trim/filter snapshot/undo/autosave/Save-button machinery (it never re-bakes, so routing it through the bake-oriented Save flow would force pointless ffmpeg round-trips), persisting instead on its own silent 400ms-debounced `library.updateFluctuation` write. So the owner changed the drift settings, saw the Save button sitting at "Nothing to save" the whole time, and reasonably concluded nothing had saved *and* nothing was working. Same class of issue — and same fix — as the Remix Save button's own "silent Save was confusing" feedback that was added earlier. Fixed without regressing the no-re-bake design:
- **`fluctuationDirty()` / `savedFluctuationSnapshot`** — a small parallel dirty flag (normalized `{volume,pitch}` fingerprint via `normalizeFluctuationAxis`), deliberately *not* folded into `hasUnsavedChanges()`/`snapshotOf()` (which also feed undo, A-B compare, the stale-baked-clip indicator, and the leave-confirm — none of which should react to a playback-time param). `updateSaveButtonState()` now lights the button on `hasUnsavedChanges() || fluctuationDirty()`; `applyFluctuationControls()` calls it so the button lights the instant a handle moves.
- **`flushFluctuation()`** (the existing debounced write) now, on success, updates `savedFluctuationSnapshot`, re-runs `updateSaveButtonState()` (button drops back to "Nothing to save"), and writes **"Fluctuation applied — saved automatically (no bake needed)."** into the shared `#editor-save-status` line — so there's a visible "it saved" beat. On failure: "Fluctuation change could not be saved."
- **`performSave()`** now also persists fluctuation in the same pass when `fluctuationDirty()` (cancelling the pending debounce so there's no double-write) and dispatches the `noctivago:sound-fluctuation-changed` Mixer bridge. It captures `bakeRelevantDirty = hasUnsavedChanges()` up front and, when nothing bake-relevant changed (a fluctuation-only manual Save), **early-returns before the ffmpeg `renderLoopClip` call** with "Saved — original file untouched." — no redundant re-render of a byte-identical clip.
- **`#editor-fluctuation-saved-note`** — a hint shown in the Fluctuation section only while an axis is enabled *and* the preview toggle is on "Saved audio": drift plays in the Live edit preview and the Mixer, never in the baked clip (which by design contains none). Toggled from `updateFluctuationEnabledUI()` (also called now from `updatePreviewModeToggle()`).
- **Verified live via CDP** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch + `window.__editorDebug`/`__mixerDebug` hooks, all removed after; dev electron killed by specific PID sparing the owner's real installed copy per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]): drag a handle → Save lights "Save" → ~1.5s later → "Nothing to save" + the auto-saved status line, `savedFluctuationSnapshot` matches, store shows the new value; a manual Save of a fluctuation-only change early-returns "Saved — original file untouched." with no bake; a combined filter+fluctuation Save runs the normal bake *and* persists fluctuation; a fast sound-switch mid-edit still flushes (bias 0.55 landed on the previous sound); the Saved-audio note shows/hides with the preview mode; drift itself still audibly moves the gain in Live preview. One CDP artifact re-confirmed for next time: a backgrounded/occluded dev window freezes `requestAnimationFrame` (so the preview loop appears stuck at end-of-file) regardless of `Page.setWebLifecycleState('active')` — only a genuinely visible window exercises the rAF loop-seam path. `npm run build` clean; `plugins/editor/index.js` re-checked as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check.

**Add from link: a download-length cap + real progress/verbose log (v0.1.169)** — two inbox items, one flagged "takes precedence over anything" ("When you add something from a link there should be trim controls so you don't download a 10h file since you can loop and crossfade it on the app. Totally user controlled and this should be set to 10 minutes by default") and one from the Questions answers ("Add from link... would like to have a working progress bar + verbose of what's going on"). Both are Add-from-link-only, neither blocked, so picked up together this "go forth".
- **Length cap** — a new "Max length to download (minutes)" field on the Add-from-link dialog (`index.html` / `AddLinkDialog.js`), default **10**, `0` = whole file. Threaded through `library:addSoundFromUrl` as `maxSeconds`. The **yt-dlp** path adds `--download-sections "*0-<seconds>"` (verified against the real bundled yt-dlp 2026.08.19 with `-x --audio-format wav` before shipping — a 60s cap on a 10.5-min video produced a genuine 59.99s WAV, ~11MB vs. the ~120MB full pull); the **direct-ffmpeg** path adds `-t <seconds>` as an output option (stops pulling the input stream at that point — verified: a 6s cap on a 15s MP3 URL produced a 6.0s file). The cap is a safety default, not persisted — every open resets to 10 (the whole point is you can't *accidentally* pull a multi-hour stream).
- **Progress + verbose** — a new `library:addSoundFromUrlProgress` IPC channel (mirrors `export:progress`'s `event.sender.send` shape) carries `{type:'step'|'status', message}` and `{type:'progress', percent}` from `library.js` back to the dialog, which now shows a real progress bar (`.add-link-progress-fill`, indeterminate until the first real percent) + a scrolling monospace log (`.add-link-log`). `runYtDlp.js` gained an `onStatus(line)` option alongside `onProgress`: it splits stdout/stderr into lines and forwards yt-dlp's own bracket-prefixed activity lines (`[youtube] Extracting URL`, `[info] Downloading 1 time ranges: 0.0-60.0`, `[ExtractAudio] Destination`, `WARNING:`/`ERROR:`/`Deleting`) while dropping (a) the fast-moving `[download] NN%` spam (`onProgress` owns that) and (b) the ffmpeg subprocess's own muxing dump that yt-dlp passes through — told apart by an `@` inside the brackets (`[out#0/webm @ 0x..]` is ffmpeg's, `[youtube]` is yt-dlp's).
- **Friendlier failure** — `runFfmpeg.js`'s `FRIENDLY_FFMPEG_ERRORS` table gained an HTTP-4xx / "Server returned NNN Xxx" signature → "That link could not be opened - it may be a dead link, need a login, or not point at a media file." (kept narrow so a *local* missing-file "Error opening input" during a normal bake doesn't get mislabelled as a link problem). The dialog's own catch strips Electron's `Error invoking remote method '...':` IPC wrapper and shows only the first sentence on the status line, with the full detail in the log.
- **Verified**: a Node harness (`module.register` electron stub, real `runYtDlp`/`runFfmpegToFile`) confirmed the cap + progress + filtered status lines on real downloads; then end-to-end via CDP against a real dev build — opened the dialog from the "+" menu, ran a real YouTube URL at a 1-min cap (log showed `Downloading 1 time ranges: 0.0-60.0`, bar 0→100, sound added, on-disk WAV = 59.99s), a direct-audio URL at cap 0 (full 19s file) and cap 4, and a dead link (concise status "Couldn't add that link: Couldn't download that link directly.", full detail in the log, dialog stays open, Confirm re-enabled). Test sounds removed, `download-tmp` self-cleaned via the existing `finally` unlink. `npm run build` clean; temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch removed; dev electron killed by PID sparing the owner's installed copy (which was running the whole time).

**Sleep timer: survives an app restart + an "at a set time" mode (v0.1.170)** — two inbox items, both `#next`, done together: "Timer shouldn't be stopped nor lost when you close the app and reopens it" and "instead of picking a value in minutes to input a time of day based on system clock".
- **Persistence** (`src/main/sleepTimer.js` + `settings.js`): a running timer was purely in-memory (`current = { firesAt, action }`). Now `startSleepTimer` also writes `{ firesAt (absolute epoch ms), action }` to `settings.js`'s store as `sleepTimerRunState` (new `setSleepTimerRunState`/`getSleepTimerRunState`), cleared by `cancelSleepTimer` and on elapse. New `restoreSleepTimer()`, called from `initSleepTimer(win)` at startup: `firesAt` still in the future → re-arm `setTimeout` for exactly the remaining ms (`armFor` helper, shared with `startSleepTimer`); already elapsed but within `RESTORE_GRACE_MS` (5 min — covers a quick crash / auto-update restart) → fire the action now; elapsed longer ago → drop it silently + log (deliberately: reopening the app hours later must not shut the machine down the instant it launches). The renderer needs no change — it still reads state through `sleepTimer:get`, so a restored timer shows its countdown + toolbar pill normally. New `fireElapsed(action)` helper waits for `webContents.did-finish-load` if the page isn't ready yet (the restore path can run before the renderer loads), also used by the normal timeout path.
- **Time-of-day mode** (`index.html` + `src/renderer/core/SleepTimer.js`): a new `sleep-timer-mode` radio fieldset ("In a number of minutes" / "At a set time") toggles the minutes input + presets vs. an `<input type="time">` + a live "That's in 1:29:28 — today." hint. `targetFromTimeOfDay("HH:MM")` returns the next Date matching that clock time (today if still ahead, else tomorrow); `readDuration()` converts either mode to `{ durationSeconds }`. `sleepTimer:start` now also carries `mode` + `timeOfDay` through to `setSleepTimerPrefs` (new `sleepTimerMode`/`sleepTimerTimeOfDay` settings), restored on the next dialog open. The main process still only ever gets `durationSeconds` + `action` — the time-of-day math is entirely renderer-side (it owns the user's clock/timezone), so `sleepTimer.js` didn't need to learn about wall-clock times at all.
- **Verified live via CDP against a real dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch, removed; dev electron killed by PID, installed copy left running): time-of-day mode — field toggle, hint updates on input, starting a "90 min from now" pick armed a timer with `secsLeft` ≈ 5366 and persisted `mode:'at'`/`timeOfDay`; **a genuine full app kill + relaunch** restored an active 3-min timer with the *exact same* `firesAt` and a correctly-decremented `secsLeft` (180 → 135 across the restart), toolbar pill showing "2:16"; a hand-written stale run-state (20 min past) relaunched → logged "elapsed while the app was closed (stale), dropped", `sleepTimerRunState` null, **no shutdown fired**; a hand-written recently-elapsed state (1 min past, action stop) relaunched → logged "elapsed while closed (recently), acting: stop" and cleared; "in minutes" mode + presets confirmed unregressed. Dev settings restored to the owner's own pre-test values (`sleepTimerAction: 'shutdown'`). `npm run build` clean.

**Volume Fluctuation now bakes into an export + a latent whole-mix Echo/Reverb export gap fixed (v0.1.171)** — the `#next` Fluctuation follow-up: v0.1.164 (per-sound vol+pitch drift) and v0.1.167 (whole-mix/group volume drift) both shipped playback-only; an export ignored fluctuation entirely and rendered a steady level. Now the **volume** axis bakes into an export for all three scopes — an ungrouped loop sound, a grouped loop sound, a Sound Group bus, and the whole-mix. **Pitch fluctuation still doesn't bake** (needs time-varying resampling, a genuinely separate problem) — documented follow-up, along with scatter/scheduled (still UI-hidden there).
- **New `src/main/ffmpeg/gainEnvelope.js`**: renders ONE realization of the drift as a short stereo 16-bit control-signal WAV at 1000 Hz (`sampleVolumeWalk` — a deliberate self-contained copy of `src/renderer/audio/Modulator.js`'s bounded-random-walk math, since main never imports from `src/renderer/`; mirrors it the way the Remix plugin keeps its own bundled copy). One realization baked, same as scatter/scheduled shot placement already is. `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests; a real export uses `Math.random` (fresh each run). File lands in `export-tmp` so the existing per-export cleanup + startup sweep both cover it.
- **`exportMix.js`**: new `applyEnvelopeToTrack(track, fluctuation, durationSeconds)` — multiplies an already-materialized, full-duration, offset-0 track by the envelope via ffmpeg `amultiply` (both streams forced to stereo `fltp`, envelope `aresample`d 1000→44100), returning a new offset-0 FLAC track. Called: (a) inside `renderGroupBuses` on a grouped fluctuating loop sound's tile *before* the group combine (matches live: a sound's own `fluctuationGain` is ahead of its group routing); (b) inside `renderGroupBuses` on the finished group bus when the group's own `filters.fluctuation` is set; (c) a new step in `exportMix()` after `renderGroupBuses` — an ungrouped loop sound with volume drift can't ride `renderFinalMixdown`'s cheap `-stream_loop` path, so it's pulled out, tiled (`tileLoopShotFull`), enveloped, and joins the final mix as a plain track (`plainLoopShots` vs. `ungroupedLoopShots` — the `-stream_loop` fast path is untouched for non-drifting sounds). Whole-mix fluctuation is folded into `finalizeWholeMix`: `needsIntermediate = hasReverb || hasFluct` now, the main graph renders to a WAV, then `[reverb pass] → [envelope multiply] + start/end fades + real encode`. `buildWholeMixPostChain`'s `deferReverbAndTail` param became `{ includeLimiter, includeTail }` — limiter stays in the main graph unless Reverb runs (which bakes its own), the fades always move to the final pass. Since fluctuation is attenuation-only (0..1), applying it post-limiter on a bus/whole-mix can't clip, so the live "before the limiter" node position doesn't matter for the bake. Progress budget gained a rough `fluctuationWorkSeconds` term (same spirit as the existing `groupWorkSeconds` estimate).
- **Plumbing**: `plugins/export/index.js` — `sounds.push` now carries `fluctuation: entry.fluctuation` (ipc.js's `export:run` already spreads `...s`); group `filters` already forwarded whole (includes `fluctuation` post-`normalizeGroupFilters`). **Latent bug fixed in passing**: `currentWholeMixFilters(preset)` hand-picked only `{highpassHz, lowpassHz, gainDb, eq}` — so a preset's whole-mix **Echo and Reverb** (v0.1.160/161) *and* the new fluctuation were silently dropped from every real export (the harnesses that "verified" v0.1.160/161 called `exportMix` directly with a hand-built object, bypassing this function). Now returns `{ ...preset.wholeMix }` — echo/reverb/fluctuation all reach the export for real. Behavior change: existing presets with whole-mix echo/reverb will now sound different (correct) in exports.
- **Verified against real bundled ffmpeg** via a Node harness (electron stubbed via a `module.register` resolve hook, real `exportMix()` called end-to-end — not isolated helpers): 31/31 checks. Whole-mix / group / per-sound-ungrouped / per-sound-grouped fluctuation each measured a real ~5–6 dB overall attenuation vs. the same export with drift off, AND a >1 dB stdev across 2-second window levels (proving time-varying drift, not a static gain); whole-mix fluctuation + reverb together (the both-isolated-passes path) rendered correctly at the exact duration; backward compat (no fluctuation anywhere) unchanged; zero temp-file leftovers in every scenario; `sampleVolumeWalk` stays within `[0,1]` and its configured bounds, isn't constant, and a degenerate all-1 config yields all 1s. `npm run build` clean; new/edited main files `node --check` clean; `plugins/export/index.js` re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check. **Not CDP-verified** — the plugin-side change is two trivial lines (a spread + one payload field) and the real risk was the ffmpeg pipeline, which was verified through the full `exportMix()` dispatch against real ffmpeg.

---

## Archived 2026-09-10 (covering v0.1.172–v0.1.175)
