**Likely fix for the "weird synth-like sound" mystery — WSOLA time-compression buzz on aggressive per-shot Speed (v0.1.172)** — the owner's 2026-09-06 inbox answer on the long-running "weird sound" mystery: "I disabled both randomized pitch and randomized speed audios and this seems to have fixed it... could be a thunderclap audio with something bugged on the speed randomizer cause pitch was working fine for a long time." Investigated the per-shot Speed path (v0.1.135) and found a real, specific defect matching every part of that report. `pitchStretch.js`'s hand-rolled WSOLA (used by the *live* `BufferScatterSource`/`BufferScheduledSource` shot playback — the export path uses rubberband, which is fine) computes an overall stretch `ratio = 2^(semitones/12) / speedFactor`. When `ratio < 0.5` the analysis hop (`SYNTH_HOP / ratio`) grows past `FRAME_SIZE`, so consecutive analysis frames stop overlapping and the similarity search can't bridge the skipped input — on broadband non-periodic content (a thunderclap is the pathological case) the overlap-add then splices mismatched material every synthesis hop, injecting a metallic buzz around SR/SYNTH_HOP (~86 Hz). An isolated harness on chaotic broadband noise measured HF energy unchanged through ratio ~0.7, ~1.5x by 0.6, ~2.3x by 0.5, ~3.4x by 0.25. Crucially this is *only* reachable via the Speed feature: pure pitch never drops below `ratio = 0.5` (pitchRatio itself at -12 st), but pure speed 2x hits 0.5, and speed >1.67x with any downward pitch goes lower — exactly "pitch was fine for a long time, speed broke it." **Fix**: new `MIN_CLEAN_STRETCH_RATIO = 0.6` in `pitchStretch.js`; `shiftAndStretchBuffer` returns `null` below it, and both callers (`ScatterSoundSource`/`ScheduledSoundSource`) *already* handle `null` -> plain resample (`playbackRate = speedFactor` + `detune`), which is clean (no buzz) at the cost of coupling pitch to tempo for that one shot — the exact varispeed trade already accepted and documented for the >12s and stream-mode paths. Surgical: one guard line, no change to the WSOLA itself, export path untouched (separate rubberband implementation). **Verified** via an isolated Node harness importing the real `pitchStretch.js`: (a) an artifact probe confirming the HF-buzz onset at ratio <= 0.6 on broadband content (and its absence on tonal content and on expansion, ratio > 1); (b) 17/17 boundary checks — every `{semitones, speedFactor}` combo returns `null` iff `ratio < 0.6`, no-op still passes the original buffer through, >12s still `null`, and the caller's resample-fallback `durationSeconds` formula matches real wall-clock for every combo. **Not CDP/audibly verified** — "no buzz" isn't something CDP can assert, and the true test is the owner's own thunderclap; flagged in the changelog for them to re-enable per-shot speed and confirm the noise is actually gone. If it isn't, the mystery reopens (the export-completion-chime and Sound-Group-EQ angles are still on the table). `npm run build` clean; `pitchStretch.js`/`ScatterSoundSource.js`/`ScheduledSoundSource.js` `node --check` clean.

**BUG FIX: the mix was empty on launch after closing the app while paused — "Play all" did nothing (v0.1.173)** — reported directly (Board `#next`): "opened the app after an update and no preset was either playing nor loaded cause once i hit the play button nothing played." Root-caused by tracing every write to `state.included` in `tabs/mixer/index.js`: the Set that `toggleGlobalPlayback()` iterates, and that every `SoundRow` reads for its In Mix / Add label and the "Current Mix" bucket, was **only ever populated during a session** (by `startPlayback`/`toggleIncluded`) or by `maybeResumeOnLaunch` — which is gated on `settings.wasPlayingOnClose` and so only runs when the app was actively *playing*, not merely paused, at close. `refreshList()` seeded `state.volumes` from each entry's persisted value but never did the same for `included`. So: close the app while paused → next launch, `state.included` is empty → every sound shows "Add", the Current Mix section is empty, and "Play all" (which loops over `state.included`) is a silent no-op. The "nor loaded" half is a separate, benign consequence — `lastActivePresetId` only shipped in v0.1.159, so the first launch of a version that has it has nothing to restore; and a mix built by hand (add sounds + toggle Include) legitimately has no active preset. **Fix**: `refreshList()` now seeds `state.included` from the persisted per-sound `included` flags (missing sounds excluded, matching `maybeResumeOnLaunch`'s own filter) — but only on the **first** load, guarded by a module-level `includedSeeded` flag, since after that `state.included` is authoritative and kept in sync with disk by `startPlayback`/`toggleIncluded`; re-seeding on every tab-switch `refreshList()` would risk a fire-and-forget `persistIncluded()` write that hasn't landed yet clobbering an in-session removal (same "seed once per session" intent as the adjacent `state.volumes` line, which uses `if (!state.volumes.has(...))` for the same reason). Auto-resume behavior is unchanged — actual playback on launch still only happens when `wasPlayingOnClose`; this only restores *mix membership* so the manual Play button works. **Verified live via CDP against a real dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` switch in `main/index.js` + a temp `window.__mixerDebug` hook, both removed after; dev electron killed by its own root PID sparing the owner's installed copy per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]; dev profile's `library.json`/`settings.json` backed up and restored): set two real `ok` sounds to `included:true` with `wasPlayingOnClose:false`, launched → `state.included` came back seeded `["Loopy","Chirp"]` with `state.playing` empty (no unwanted auto-resume), both rows rendered "In Mix", the global button read "Play all"; clicking it started both sounds (`state.playing` → both, 2 sources created, zero errors, button flipped to "Pause all") — a genuine no-op before the fix. Removing a sound from the mix via its row button then left `state.included` correctly at `["Loopy"]` (the `includedSeeded` guard makes a re-seed structurally impossible after first load). `npm run build` clean.

**Noise gate — a new per-sound filter in Remix (Sound mode) (v0.1.174)** — the lowest-scored ready backlog item (`noctivago_task_list` #17, from the 2026-09-06 Audacity research pass, `noctivago_audacity_research`), picked up per the project's "dry backlog" precedent: both Board `#next` items are still owner-gated (the "weird sound" per-shot-speed listening test; the export mixdown-phase optimization needs the owner's next real slow-export log, now that v0.1.154 saves it to a file), and the "presets as primary context" big half needs its own planning pass, not a "go forth" pick.

- **Scope**: threshold / reduction (depth) / attack / release, folded into the existing per-sound `filters` bag (flat fields `gateThresholdDb`/`gateRangeDb`/`gateAttackMs`/`gateReleaseMs`, alongside `highpassHz`.../`echoDelayMs`...) so it reuses every bit of the existing filters plumbing — `updateFilters`, the `loopClipFilters` staleness JSON-compare (both `mixer/index.js`'s `loopClipEligible` and `library.js`'s copy), `bufferBakeSnapshot`, `snapshotOf`'s dirty-check, the portable-preset `IMPORTED_SETTINGS_KEYS` `'filters'` whole-object copy — with zero new schema/IPC/staleness code, exactly like the EQ and Echo additions before it. Off unless `gateThresholdDb > -80 && gateRangeDb > 0` (threshold at its -80 floor OR zero reduction = "do nothing", matching every other 0-means-off control). Sound mode only, per the research scoping (a whole-mix / group bus is a sum of sources, no single noise floor to gate); `NEUTRAL_WHOLE_MIX`/`NEUTRAL_GROUP_FILTERS` and `canonicalMixFilters` deliberately untouched.
- **Bake** (`loopClip.js`'s new exported `buildGateFilter`, prepended first in `buildFilterChain` — gates the raw trimmed source before highpass/EQ colour it, the conventional channel-strip order and the same position the live node sits at): ffmpeg's own `agate=threshold=<lin>:range=<lin>:attack=<ms>:release=<ms>:ratio=9000:detection=rms`. Maps the UI's dB to agate's linear 0..1 threshold/`range` (`range` is the *floor gain*, so a bigger reduction is a *smaller* linear value: `10^(-rangeDb/20)`). `ratio=9000` = a hard gate, not a downward expander. **Verified against the real bundled binary before writing app code** and then end-to-end through `renderClipToPath` (electron stubbed via a `module.register` resolve hook): a pink-noise floor at -54dB gated down to -91dB while -29dB events passed through untouched; the crossfade/rotation render path (`buildRotatedFilterComplex` → `buildFilterComplex` → `buildFilterChain`) proven separately (quietest 0.35s window -57dB → -91dB); the off/neutral case returns `null` so `buildFilterChain` emits nothing and the default path stays byte-identical; zero leftover temp files.
- **`agate` has no `hold` parameter** (Audacity's `noisegate.ny` does — a lookahead envelope that keeps the gate open through brief dips). A generous Release covers the same "don't chatter on a decaying tail" need in practice, so Hold isn't exposed. **The optional high/low crossover from that same reference is a deliberate follow-up, not shipped**: verified directly that a naive `lowpass=f=F` + `highpass=f=F` split summed back together produces a **-36dB null at exactly the crossover frequency** (the two bands are 180° out of phase there) — doing it right needs a complementary-filter pass (allpass-sum or subtractive), real extra work out of proportion for this item's size. Noted in `buildGateFilter`'s own comment.
- **Live preview** — new `src/renderer/audio/NoiseGate.js` (+ a bundled copy in `plugins/editor/audio/` per the plugin's can't-import-outside-its-dir rule, per [[feedback_nodecheck_unreliable_for_plugin_templates]]). Web Audio has no native gate (`DynamicsCompressorNode` only ever acts *above* its threshold), so `NoiseGate` is a polled envelope follower: an `AnalyserNode` taps the input, a `setInterval(25ms)` loop reads short-term RMS and, on a threshold crossing (with 3dB hysteresis so a signal hovering at the edge doesn't machine-gun), ramps the gate's own `GainNode` toward 1 (open, attack τ) or `10^(-rangeDb/20)` (closed, release τ) via `setTargetAtTime` — the "poll the decision, let Web Audio do the smoothing" split `Modulator.js` (Fluctuation) already uses, and `setInterval` not rAF for the same reason (a minimized-to-tray window freezes rAF outright but only clamps `setInterval`). `configure(filters)` / `sync(playing)` / `dispose()`, same lifecycle shape as `SoundFluctuation`. Wired first-in-chain (before highpass) in `LocalFileSoundSource` (on the summed output of both crossfade voices) and per-voice in the Remix `PreviewSource` — the same two places every other live filter node lives. **Buffer mode needs no live node at all** — a baked clip already contains the gate. `StreamScatterSource`/`StreamScheduledSource` deliberately skip it, consistent with how they already skip EQ/echo/reverb live (the baked clip has it; the >10min stream fallback is a documented degraded path).
- **Verified live via CDP against a real running dev build** (temp `NOCTIVAGO_CDP` remote-debugging switch in `main/index.js` + a temp `window.__editorDebug` hook, both removed after; a minimal built-in-`WebSocket` CDP driver since no puppeteer/playwright is installed; dev electron killed by its own root PID — 29872, sparing the owner's real installed copy which was running the whole time, per [[feedback_scoped_process_kill]]): drove the real `NoiseGate` class in-browser through an oscillator whose level was toggled across the threshold — `output.gain` measured exactly 1.0 above threshold, 0.01 (the -40dB floor) below, 1.0 again on re-opening; the off case (`gateThresholdDb:-80, gateRangeDb:0`) correctly reported `enabled:false` with no running timer; `dispose()` clean. Separately confirmed the real `PreviewSource` wiring on a real loaded sound — `voices[0].noiseGate` present, `enabled:true`, `floorGain` correct for the set reduction, `currentFilters()` round-tripping the gate fields, zero page errors. `npm run build` clean; both changed plugin files re-verified as valid ESM via the `.mjs`-forced check.
- **Not shipped / follow-ups**: the frequency crossover (above); a proper `hold` (would need a custom envelope-follower ffmpeg pass); and extending it to Preset/Group modes (out of scope by design — nothing single to gate on a bus).

**BUG FIX: "weird synth-like sound" take two — per-shot Speed no longer runs through the live WSOLA at all (v0.1.175)** — the owner re-reported the loud, sleep-disrupting synth-like noise (inbox, flagged ASAP): still there with per-shot random **Speed** on a thunderclap, disabling Speed still fixes it — v0.1.172's `MIN_CLEAN_STRETCH_RATIO = 0.6` guard on `pitchStretch.js`'s WSOLA compressor wasn't enough. An envelope-modulation probe on broadband noise (`timeStretch` at a sweep of ratios) showed a strong periodic ~86 Hz AM (the synthesis-hop-rate COLA-breakdown buzz) reaching well up into the 0.6–0.9 ratio band on non-periodic content, not just below 0.5 — the NCC similarity search is near-useless on a broadband transient, so the join quality is essentially bare OLA of a compressed signal. Rather than keep chasing a "safe" threshold, **took per-shot Speed off the WSOLA path entirely**: `BufferScatterSource`/`BufferScheduledSource` `_playOneShot` now call `pitchShiftBuffer(ctx, buf, semitones)` (pitch only — the length-preserving stretch that shipped v0.1.111 and was never the complaint) and apply the per-shot speed as plain `node.playbackRate = speedFactor` varispeed (couples pitch to tempo, tape-machine style — clean, cannot buzz). The duration math at the call site was already exactly right for this split (`dur/speedFactor` when the pitch stretch succeeds, `dur/(pitchRatio*speedFactor)` on the resample fallback) so only two lines changed per file. `MIN_CLEAN_STRETCH_RATIO` dropped back to `0.5` (the true COLA cliff) since it now only gates the rare pitch-down-past-`-12`-st case, restoring the exact pre-v0.1.172 pitch behavior. **Export path untouched** — it keeps Speed/Pitch independent via rubberband's own offline tempo pass, which doesn't have this artifact. **Trade-off, flagged to the owner in the inbox**: per-shot Speed in the live Mixer no longer keeps pitch fixed (v0.1.135's original promise) — if they want that back it needs a genuinely different live approach (an offline pre-render of speed-varied shot variants, or a WASM rubberband), planned separately. **Verified**: a Node harness (fake `AudioContext`, real `pitchStretch.js`) — 15/15 checks: a speed-only shot returns the original buffer (zero WSOLA work), a pitch shot still stretches to `dur*pitchRatio` length across -12…+12 st, pitch past the range and clips >12 s return `null` for the resample fallback, and the caller's duration formula lands the right wall-clock time for every pitch×speed combination. `npm run build` clean; `pitchStretch.js` / `ScatterSoundSource.js` / `ScheduledSoundSource.js` `node --check` clean. **Not CDP/audibly verified** — "no buzz" isn't CDP-assertable and the real test is the owner's own thunderclap; but this removes the buzzy code path outright rather than narrowing a guard, so there's no "still reachable" gap left.
